# Theme — CoWoS / Advanced Packaging

_Wiki · generated 2026-06-19 · cross-company theme · sources: equity calls (`../_equity_calls/Semis`), briefing roll-ups (`../_briefings/by-ticker`), earnings transcripts, company pages. Company pages: [../00_INDEX.md](../00_INDEX.md)._


## 🆕 2026-09-24 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — **MS ON CHINA ADVANCED PACKAGING: "MODERATE OVERCAPACITY IN 2027" STANDS, BUT THE ONE VARIABLE THAT COULD KILL IT IS GPUs PER CoWoS WAFER — SOME TOOL VENDORS SAY FUTURE LARGE PACKAGES FIT ONLY 6-8 PER WAFER, AGAINST ~21 IN MS'S MODEL.**

- **Morgan Stanley · Daisy Dai / Charlie Chan / Daniel Yen / Henry Zhao / Ethan Jia / Lucas Wang — "China – Advanced Packaging: Key Questions and Feedback" (11pp, 2026-09-24; Industry View Attractive):** (1) China overcapacity has **limited impact on global** supply/demand — the markets are *"increasingly segmented"* by US restrictions and customer qualification, so Chinese OSATs struggle to supply NVIDIA/AMD-class US accelerators. (2) **GPUs per wafer:** some equipment vendors suggest future larger packages fit **6-8 GPUs per CoWoS-equivalent wafer vs ~21** in MS's model, which would *"materially increase packaging demand and reduce oversupply"*; MS holds its view because its supply forecast is already conservative and China supply-chain checks show **~20 local GPU dies per wafer at ~80% back-end yield**. (3) GDDR/LPDDR: limited substitution for training, potential in cost-sensitive inference. (4) JCET valued as a group; PT implies **~44x 2027e P/E**, in line with the A-share OSAT median.
- ⚠️ **Why the GPUs-per-wafer number matters beyond China:** it is the conversion factor between every kwpm figure on this page and accelerator units. A move from ~21 to 6-8 per wafer is a ~2.6-3.5x swing in wafers per GPU — larger than the whole UBS-vs-MS 2027 CoWoS gap. No source here says which packages or which year; logged, not applied.

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-23 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — **THE FIRST *2028* COWOS NUMBER FROM THE HOUSE THAT HAS NEVER PUBLISHED ONE: MS's CHARLIE CHAN GOES TO **325kwpm CoWoS (+41% Y/Y)** INSIDE **ADVANCED PACKAGING CAPACITY +50% Y/Y**, WITH **[[INTC]] EMIB-T +125% TO 45kwpm** AND A FIRST, TINY **TSMC CoPoS AT 3.75kwpm**. ⚠️⚠️ **AND HIS OWN GROWTH RATE BACK-SOLVES TO A 2027 BASE OF ~230kwpm — *BELOW* BOTH THE UBS 270k AND THE JPM 260k THIS PAGE CARRIES FOR END-'27.**

**Source:** Morgan Stanley · **Amir Amerian** (Asia Tech sales) — *"Asia Tech - stars aligning, Semis vs Shell growth, all roads lead to ABF…"*, **2026-09-23**, relaying **MS Head of Greater China Semis Charlie Chan**'s preliminary 2028 CoWoS estimate published ~2026-09-09. ⚠️⚠️⚠️ **SOURCE CLASS FIRST: this is *"SALES COMMENTARY ONLY (NOT A PRODUCT OF RESEARCH)"* relaying a research analyst's published estimate. The numbers are Chan's; the framing and the emphasis are the salesperson's. No rating, no price target, no company model is carried here.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **(1) THE 2028 CAPACITY SPLIT, AS PRINTED.** *"He assumes **2028 Advanced Packaging Capacity +50% YoY**, with **CoWoS +41% (325kwpm)**, **INTC's EMIB-T +125% YoY (45kwpm)**, and **small TSM CoPoS (3.75kwpm)**."* ➤➤ **Three things are net-new to this page: (a) a 2028 CoWoS level at all; (b) an EMIB-T capacity number — 45kwpm, growing faster than CoWoS off a small base, which is the first time [[INTC]]'s packaging platform has been sized against TSMC's on the same axis; (c) a CoPoS number, at 3.75kwpm essentially a pilot line. Note the arithmetic: if total advanced packaging grows +50% while CoWoS grows +41%, the NON-CoWoS platforms are growing faster than the headline — EMIB-T at +125% is where that comes from.**
- ⚠️⚠️🔴 **(2) THE INCONSISTENCY THIS PAGE MUST NOT SMOOTH OVER, AND IT IS ARITHMETIC.** **325kwpm ÷ 1.41 ⇒ an implied 2027 CoWoS base of ~230kwpm.** This page currently carries **UBS at 270kwpm END-'27** (09-22) and **JPM at 260kwpm END-2027** (the 09-17 "convergence" mark). **MS's implied 2027 base sits ~13-15% BELOW both.** ⚠️ **THE MOST LIKELY RECONCILIATION IS A BASIS DIFFERENCE, NOT A DISAGREEMENT — UBS and JPM both say *"end-'27"* (an EXIT rate) while an annual +41% growth rate is naturally computed off a FULL-YEAR AVERAGE. An exit rate of 270k is perfectly compatible with a 2027 average of ~230k in a fast-ramping year. ⛔ **BUT NEITHER DOCUMENT STATES ITS BASIS, SO THIS IS FLAGGED AND NOT RESOLVED. Do NOT chain MS's 325k onto UBS's 270k to compute a growth rate, and do NOT present ~230k as an MS forecast — it is a derived figure, not a published one.** 🔬 **SETTLED BY: Chan's own note stating whether 325kwpm is an exit rate or a full-year average.**
- 🔴🔴 **(3) THE ARGUMENT THE NUMBER WAS PUBLISHED TO MAKE, AND IT IS THE USEFUL PART — A DEFENCE OF 2028 AGAINST A DECELERATING CAPEX AND MEMORY-PRICE BACKDROP.** Amerian relays Chan's two stated motives: *"(1) **even if MS US Internet analyst Brian Nowak currently has 2028 CSP Capex +12% YoY and market talks about moderate memory price growth in 2028, much of AI Semis/Infra can still enjoy strong growth in 2028. DC shell spend and memory price inflation may slow in 2028, but Semi units and all the related content can still grow meaningfully**; (2) he provides differentiated analysis of emergence of new Advanced Packaging technologies."* ➤➤ **This is a "the mix saves you" argument and it is falsifiable: it requires packaging UNITS and CONTENT to grow ~50% in a year when the same house has hyperscaler capex growing only +12%. Those two MS numbers are in the same building and they are hard to hold simultaneously unless packaging intensity per capex dollar rises sharply. Carry MS 2028 CSP capex +12% Y/Y as a mark in its own right — it is a low number against this theme's own capex ladder.** ⚠️ **Chan's own caution on why he had not published a 2-year-out CoWoS figure before: *"CoWoS equipment lead times are generally ~6-9mo"*, so he *"preferred to wait until checks have reasonable visibility."* A 2028 estimate published in Sept-2026 sits outside that visibility window by his own standard — this is his most speculative CoWoS print, not his most confident.**
- 🔴🔴 **(4) THE SUBSTRATE CONCLUSION — "ALL ROADS LEAD TO ABF" — AND THE MECHANISM IS A YIELD ARGUMENT, WHICH IS NEW.** *"Whether it be Agentic AI / CPU demand or more positive signals (yield & commercial) around EMIB-T, I personally think **all roads lead to substrates, and specifically ABF Substrates**. **CPUs are historically and still today a large driver of demand for ABF Substrates, so higher CPU pricing and volumes are a positive.** The potential success of EMIB-T has multiple positive benefits: **1) initial substrate yields are likely lower than CoWoS, leading to MORE CONSUMPTION**; and **2) it likely pushes TSMC to accelerate introduction of competitive offerings like Glass Core and CoPoS** (even if on the July call TSMC welcomed EMIB-T as a way to unlock its own front-end wafer growth). These new packaging technologies are known to also have **more value-add opportunities for ABF Substrate makers**."* ➤➤ **Point (1) is the genuinely differentiated insight and this page did not have it: a LOWER-yielding packaging platform CONSUMES MORE substrate per good die, so an EMIB-T ramp is substrate-positive precisely to the extent that it struggles. That inverts the usual read of a yield problem. Named beneficiaries in the note: Ibiden, Unimicron, Kinsus.** ⚠️ **Sales-desk opinion (*"I personally think"*), not a research conclusion — flagged as such.**
- 🆕 **(5) THE PASSIVE-COMPONENT LEG, WITH A RAISED FORECAST — adjacent to this theme and net-new to the wiki.** *"MS Japan Electronic Components analyst **Shoji Sato** recently **raised MLCC 2025-31 Market Growth CAGR from +14% to +20%** on the back of stronger AI/DC, with **2027-28 ASP Growth +18%/+22%**."* The strategic point: *"**Murata's strategy is changing**… he thinks Murata is shifting **from trying to supply MLCCs across a wide variety of applications and prices, to trying to focus capacity more on the high-end**"* — explicitly NOT like-for-like price hikes, which Sato has consistently said Murata would avoid for competitive reasons. Amerian reads it as a **2017-18 analogue** (*"back then, it was Murata reportedly cutting some SKUs to focus capacity on Auto/GP Server"*) and notes the same *"quality over quantity"* message from **SUMCO** management on a recent NDR. ➤ **A +14% → +20% CAGR revision with 2027-28 ASP growth of +18%/+22% is a significant tightening signal in a component layer this wiki barely covers. Logged as a mark, not adopted into any model.**
- 🆕 **(6) THE PCB/CCL LAYER IS BEING INITIATED AROUND THIS THEME.** *"MS Greater China Hardware analysts **Howard Kao & Irene Yen recently initiated on 4 Taiwan CCL/PCB companies**, and along with their Japan/Korea/China counterparts see **significant content growth to support increased connectivity needs within AI servers**"*, with new **CCL and HVLP Copper Foil models** available. Corroborating tape from the same week: Jefferies' Asia wrap reports **GCE (2368) +8.7% on media reports of AI ASIC and 1.6T transceivers driving PCB layer-count increases of ~20% and spec upgrades to M8/M9** (Jefferies Silk Road, 2026-09-23 — ⚠️ sales product, trade-press sourced). ➤ **A ~20% layer-count increase plus an M8/M9 spec migration is the PCB-side analogue of the substrate content story in (4).**
- ⚠️ **(7) THE STOCK-LEVEL FRAMING, FOR CONTEXT ONLY.** Amerian's named longs in the subject line — *"LONG Advantest, Hynix, Ibiden/Unimicron, Murata/SEMCO, Delta/AVC, TDK/Sony, Aspeed, KYEC/Chroma"* — include two covered names ([[ADVANTEST]], [[SKHYNIX]]). **These are SALES-DESK positioning suggestions, not ratings, and nothing on any company page is superseded by them.** He also lists the Semi/HW names that made new all-time highs in 2H-July/August despite the drawdown, including **Advantest, Unimicron, Kinsus, Aspeed, GUC, Elite Materials, Asia Vital, Wiwynn** — a breadth observation, useful as tape colour only.

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-23 (/run-inbox Work Unit E) — **THE FIRST FULLY-COSTED BOTTOM-UP MODEL OF WHAT A HYBRID-BONDING LINE ACTUALLY COSTS, AND IT REFRAMES THE WHOLE ADOPTION DEBATE: AT THE THROUGHPUT THE INDUSTRY *ACHIEVES* RATHER THAN THE ONE ON THE DATASHEET, A HYBRID BOND COSTS $1.48 AGAINST MASS-REFLOW'S $0.33 — AND A *WAFER-TO-WAFER* BOND COSTS $0.11, WHICH IS CHEAPER THAN MASS REFLOW.**

_(Rothschild & Co Redburn · **Nabeel Aziz** (w/ Timm Schulze-Melander), **"Bondernomics"**, BE Semiconductor, **2026-09-23**, 57pp. **BESI Buy retained, PT €250 from €350**; share €187 priced 18-Sep-2026. Copy generated for Felipe Monteiro / Capstone. Ratings disclosed in footnotes, **not new calls**: Nvidia Buy · Broadcom Buy · Google Buy · Marvell Neutral · AWS/Amazon Neutral · ASML Buy · Lam Buy · AMAT Neutral · KLAC Neutral · ASMI Neutral; TSMC, Intel, AMD, Qualcomm, MediaTek, SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron, ASM Pacific, Kulicke & Soffa, Hanwha, SanDisk and Rapidus all **Not Rated**.)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE METHODOLOGICAL MOVE FIRST, BECAUSE EVERYTHING ELSE DEPENDS ON IT: REDBURN REFUSES NAMEPLATE THROUGHPUT AND BACK-SOLVES REALISED FACTORY OUTPUT FROM TWO OBSERVABLE FLEETS — AND CRUCIALLY IT DOES SO FOR THE *INCUMBENT* TECHNOLOGY TOO, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THE COMPARISON HONEST.**
  - **[[AMD]], CY2025 (hybrid bonding, fleet installed at [[TSM]] in SoIC):** Epyc-X CPU **0.7m units × 10 bonds = 7.0m** · Ryzen-X3D **7.9m × 1 = 7.9m** · Instinct MI355X **0.5m × 8 = 4.0m** → **19.0m bonds → an implied bonder throughput of 75 uph against a 1,500 uph datasheet spec, −95%.**
  - **[[SKHYNIX]], CY2025 (MR-MUF, the incumbent HBM flow):** HBM revenue **$20.1bn ÷ $350 ASP = 57.4m units × 12 bonds = 689.1m bonds → 445 uph against a c1,200 uph nameplate, −63%.**
  - ➤ 🔴 **BOTH TECHNOLOGIES MISS THEIR SPEC BY A LOT. That is the finding this page should carry forward: *"Anchoring our cost work in observed factory output rather than headline tool specification is the foundation of understanding underlying bonding economics."* Every uph figure on this theme — including the 09-15 Bernstein "50nm bonder ~1,500 → ~3,000 UPH" mark and BESI's 8800 Chameo Flex at a "4,000 uph nameplate" — is a NAMEPLATE number and must not be compared with these.** ⚠️ **Redburn rules out "TSMC bought ahead of the ramp" as the AMD explanation "given its further investments in its AP7 fab in 2026 offer MORE THAN DOUBLE THE SoIC CAPACITY of the existing AP6 fab" — an independent corroboration of the AP7 > 2× AP6 census this wiki logged from JPM on 09-15.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE COST LADDER — THE SINGLE MOST REUSABLE TABLE IN THE NOTE.** Fully loaded per bond (cleanroom over 20 years, tools over 4 years, 17h/day uptime, modelled per 10,000 sq ft of line): **observed HB $3.97 · TC-NCF $1.02 · MR-MUF $0.33 · Kinex HB at 200 uph $1.48 · W2W HB at 2,651 bonds/hour $0.11.** Incremental versus MR-MUF: **+$3.63 observed → +$1.15 with Kinex → MINUS $0.22 for wafer-to-wafer.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE W2W NUMBER IS THE ONE THAT CHANGES THIS THEME'S FRAMING: wafer-to-wafer hybrid bonding is not a cost PENALTY over mass reflow, it is a cost SAVING — and Redburn now calls the W2W alternative *"the principal basis for our rising caution on HBM adoption"*, an escalation from June when it was merely one of "two risks".**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL FINDING FOR A PACKAGING THEME: *"the bonders themselves account for 31% OF THE TOTAL CAPITAL COST of a HB advanced packaging fab. Assessing the economics of HB on the price of the bonder alone therefore MATERIALLY UNDERSTATES the true cost of adoption."*** Per 10,000 sq ft: **HB (SoIC) $168.2m** (tool capex $119.5m + **ISO3/Class-1 cleanroom $48.8m**) · **TC-NCF $162.1m** ($132.1m + $30.0m) · **MR-MUF $100.6m** ($90.1m + $10.5m). Per bonder line: **$9.6m / $4.5m / $4.0m**. Bonders housed per 10,000 sq ft: **18 / 36 / 25**.
  - **The net-new adjacent content is FRONT-END in character and is $0.0m in BOTH solder flows:** Cu/dielectric CMP (bond-surface) **$19.1m** · post-CMP wet clean **$8.8m** · plasma activation + degas **$8.8m** · temporary bond/debond (carrier) **$8.8m** · overlay/placement + AFM metrology **$6.6m** · fab-grade defect/particle inspection **$6.2m** · acoustic C-SAM void inspection **$0.8m** · plasma dicing **$1.6m** · laser/stealth dicing **$0.9m**.
  - **Running the other way, the KGD stacked-die test line is LOWER for HB: $5.6m vs $19.5m (TC-NCF) and $24.1m (MR-MUF)** — ⚠️ **but the three lines house different bonder counts, so per bonder it is ~$0.31m / ~$0.54m / ~$0.96m; the direction survives the normalisation but this is a capex-per-floor-area model built for a bonder thesis and Redburn draws no test conclusion from it. Flagged, not adopted.**
  - ➤ 🔴 **READ AS A SEGMENT STATEMENT: hybrid-bonding adoption drags CMP, wet clean, plasma activation and FAB-GRADE inspection/metrology out of the front end and into the back end — [[AMAT]] and [[KLAC]] content that scales with HB units rather than with bonder units. And it is the mechanism behind the UBS item immediately below, in which customers give "higher clean room requirements + more back end tools" as a reason hybrid bonding is being pushed out. Two houses, two methods, one mechanism, one day apart — one modelling it as a cost, the other hearing it as an objection.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE ADOPTION HURDLE, STATED AS A RULE AND THEN APPLIED — THIS IS THE FALSIFIABLE CORE. Redburn's criterion is that *"the maximum acceptable incremental cost to a logic device is 2%"* of COGS (100bp of gross margin at a 50% GM). Applied to memory: eight D2W bonds into a 16-high HBM stack (COGS $154) cost $9.19 per stack = 6.0% of COGS at 200 uph. The sensitivity is the table to keep: 200 uph → $9.19 / 6.0% · 250 → $6.82 / 4.4% · 300 → $5.24 / 3.4% · 350 → $4.12 / 2.7% · 400 → $3.27 / 2.1% · 450 → $2.61 / 1.7%.** ➤ **So the whole HBM hybrid-bonding question reduces to one number this industry has never disclosed: REALISED FACTORY OUTPUT IN UPH. 200 today, ~400 needed. Redburn gives that 2× step a 40% probability.**
- 🔴 **W2W AND THE "HYBRID-HYBRID" ARCHITECTURE — the most specific description of a middle path this theme has carried.** W2W thrives where dies are homogeneous, front-end yields are very high and each die is low-value; CIS and NAND lines run **>20 wafers/hour**, which Redburn haircuts to **6 wafers/hour (a 10-minute cycle)** for advanced-DRAM alignment tolerances and still gets **c13× the units-per-hour of D2W**. The offset is yield compounding: **with front-end DRAM yields at c95% and HB yields near 100%, a 2-high W2W pair lands at 90% overall and the penalty compounds to up to 50% at 16-high** — so only the 2-high pairing works. Hence **hybrid-hybrid: W2W-bond DRAM pairs, dice and sort as KGD, then D2W-stack the tower — halving the D2W bond count.** ⚠️ **Even halved, *"the D2W portion remains expensive on today's economics"* — which is why the 400 uph threshold above is the gate on the middle path too, not just on the full stack.**
- 🔴 **THE BANDWIDTH ARGUMENT, WHICH IS THE STRONGEST *PRO*-ADOPTION CASE IN THE NOTE AND CUTS AGAINST THE STACK-HEIGHT FRAMING THIS THEME HAS USED: *"Stack height is too often treated as the north star for HB adoption… we assess simply adding more layers MISREPRESENTS the problem facing AI chipmakers. The primary issue is scaling the 'MEMORY WALL'… It is BANDWIDTH, NOT CAPACITY, that gates performance."*** The mechanism is a pin count: **HBM4 took a one-time doubling of I/O channels from 1,024 to 2,048, which requires a c40-micron bond pad pitch — "the limits of mass reflow processes". To double again requires tighter pitch and therefore HB.** Generation table: max capacity (Gb) 4/4/16/16/36/36/64/64 · bandwidth (Tb/s) 0.1/0.3/0.3/0.8/1.0/3.0/4.1/6.1 · pins 1,024 ×5 then 2,048 ×3 · bandwidth gen-on-gen 93%/22%/158%/26%/**193% (HBM4)**/**37% (HBM4E)**/**50% (HBM5)**. ➤ **The de-spec debate on this wiki is a CAPACITY debate. Redburn's point is that capacity was never the binding variable — "four layers can be removed from an HBM stack without the system materially suffering, which would not be the case if capacity were the binding constraint." If he is right, the 8-Hi/12-Hi argument is much weaker evidence against hybrid bonding than this theme has been treating it as. ⚠️ Logged as a competing FRAME, not adopted over the de-spec evidence.**
- 🔭 **THE BASE-DIE INSERTION, WHICH IS THE PART EVERYONE AGREES ON.** BESI's 2026 investor-day disclosures as relayed: all leading HBM players are evaluating BESI HB · **HBM with a custom logic base die drives bond pad pitch down to 12 micron** · **HBM4e is the insertion point for HB in HBM stacking** · **the first mover is preparing for HVM in 2027** · hybrid and TCB will coexist with hybrid taking a larger share each generation. Redburn concurs with the first four and says the fifth has a range of outcomes. **Base-die bonding is sub-2% of COGS even at 8-high, so it clears the hurdle**; Redburn models **shipments from 1H27 for base-die bonding at Samsung** and probability-weighted DRAM-stacking shipments only from **2H28**. **Hot Chips, Aug-2026: Hynix's roadmap is 16-high HBM on a COMBINATION of advanced MUF and HB, with 20-high stacks EXCLUSIVELY on HB.** 🔭 **And an ownership point worth carrying: logic makers designing the base die (e.g. [[NVDA]]'s NVHBM) drive STANDARDISATION and raise HB ubiquity across the industry (DRAM bonded to a 4nm base die), whereas memory makers' custom base dies drive differentiation — so who owns the base die partly determines how fast HB spreads.**
- 🔭 **THE LOGIC CANDIDATE TABLE, WHICH IS THE CLEANEST PUBLISHED MAP OF WHO USES HYBRID BONDING TODAY: AMD Ryzen YES (high-end X3D only, one V-cache die) · Intel PC CPU NO (chiplets on microbumps; Panther Lake uses microbump Foveros) · **Apple PC CPU YES (M5 Pro/Max, two HB compute tiles)** · Apple / Samsung / MediaTek / Qualcomm smartphone APUs NO (Apple on chiplet WMCM, the others monolithic) · AMD Epyc YES (12 bonds per chip in X SKUs) · **Intel server CPU YES — Clearwater Forest Xeon 6+, 12 compute tiles bonded to a base die, shipping 2H26** · Arm-based server CPUs NO (chiplets with microbumps) · **Nvidia/Broadcom CPO YES, ramping in scale-up networking 2028/29** · AMD Instinct GPU YES (eight HB'd XCDs) · **Nvidia GPU NO — Rubin is a dual chiplet, microbump bonded; HB expected with Feynman** · **Google TPU NO — dual chiplet with microbumps** · **Broadcom ASICs VARIES — the 3.5D XDSiP process uses HB.**** ⚠️ **Redburn's own caveat travels with that last group: the architectures of Google TPU, OpenAI Jalapeno and AWS Trainium are *"closely guarded, so there is little way to independently verify where HB has yet been adopted."***
- 🔭 **SIZING, FOR THE RECORD: a 721-unit logic bonder market by 2030 (BESI 652 at 90% share) against c150 installed today, plus a risk-adjusted 274-unit memory market (BESI c80%, with Hanwha and SEMES as second sources) → 225 memory shipments — a total 877-unit installed base by 2030, 19% BELOW a 1,079-unit sell-side consensus. By chipmaker (market/BESI): TSMC 448/401 · Intel 118/108 · AMD 111/101 · Apple 111/99 · Nvidia 110/98 · China 57/47 · OSATs 50/45 · Qualcomm 38/34 · Samsung foundry 38/32 · Arm+ASICs 34/30 · Wearables&other 19/17 · CPO 17/15 · MediaTek 8/7 · Rapidus 10/19.** ⚠️ **CPO is a two-thirds cut to Redburn's own June leg (50 → 17) because each optical engine needs only ONE PIC-to-EIC bond at a $450 ASP against a $15bn CPO TAM and 20m engines by 2030. Full treatment on [[BESI]].**
- 🔭 **AND THE MAINSTREAM BACK-END CYCLE, WHICH IS THIS THEME'S OTHER HALF: back-end intensity fell from 9.2% of WFE in 2015 to 3.3% in 2025 and is argued to have stabilised; BESI's assembly-market revenue share rose 11% (2019) → 19% (2026); BESI's Investor Day 2026 top-down is 37 packaging fab projects worth $125bn (US 7/$33bn · Taiwan 6/$30bn · Korea 3/$26bn · India 3/$10bn · Singapore 1/$7bn · China 7/$6bn · Europe 5/$5bn · Vietnam 2/$5bn · Malaysia 3/$3bn) → $69bn of assembly equipment → a ~$12bn BESI opportunity; ASE ran 80-85% blended utilisation in 2Q26 guiding "very heavy capex" to continue, and is building fab SHELLS so equipment spend likely rises even on flat headline capex; market tightness in leading-edge 2.5D has produced OVERFLOW ORDERS FROM TSMC TO THE OSATs AND TO INTEL. ⚠️ Against all of that, Redburn models a MAINSTREAM DIGESTION IN 2028 on the historical 2-3-year back-end trough-to-peak rhythm, shallower than prior cycles because of 2.5D. ⚠️ The $125bn is CUMULATIVE PROJECT SPEND with no stated completion window — not an annual TAM.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-22 (/run-inbox Work Unit E) — **UBS TAKES INDUSTRY CoWoS TO 270kwpm END-'27 FROM THE 260k THIS PAGE AGREED ON FIVE DAYS AGO — AND, FROM THE SAME 40 MEETINGS, ASMPT SAYS TCB FOR HBM IS NOW EXTENDING *POTENTIALLY TO HBM5*, "IN-LINE WITH COMMENTS FROM BOTH SAMSUNG AND SK HYNIX", WHICH IS A PUSH-OUT OF HYBRID-BONDING VOLUME ADOPTION.**

_(UBS · **Nicolas Gaudois / Randy Abrams / Sunny Lin / Timothy Arcuri / Jimmy Yu / Jerry Su / Jimmy Yoon / Atsuhiro Kinoshita** et al., **"Global I/O: Tech Hardware & Semis — 3Q26 UBS APAC Tech Tour: AI strength unaltered, Semiscap noticeably bullish"**, **2026-09-22**, 27pp, off **40 company meetings across China/HK/Taiwan/Korea/Japan** plus the 2026 Taiwan Summit. Priced 21-Sep-2026. UBS is **Overweight OSATs** and Overweight Wafer Fab Equipment. ⚠️ Document footer marks the copy as provided for Daniel Grozdea at Capstone.)_

- ⚠️🔴 **THE CAPACITY NUMBER MOVES AGAIN, AND IT IS UBS SUPERSEDING UBS INSIDE A WEEK: *"We forecast industry CoWoS capacity to reach 270kwpm by end-'27, versus 160kwpm by end-'26."*** **SUPERSEDES the 260kwpm end-2027 figure this page adopted on 2026-09-16 (UBS Taiwan Summit: "we raise CoWoS industry capacity by end-2027 to 260kwpm from 250kwpm, up from 160kwpm end-2026", with TSMC held at 180kwpm, ASE lifted to 60kwpm and Amkor kept at 20kwpm). Old value and the per-vendor split moved to `## Changelog`.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE END-'26 BASE IS UNCHANGED AT 160kwpm; ONLY END-'27 MOVES, +10kwpm / +3.8%. AND NO REVISED PER-VENDOR SPLIT IS PUBLISHED — so the 180/60/20 decomposition is now a STALE breakdown of a superseded total and must NOT be re-used against the 270k.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE KNOCK-ON IS THAT THE 09-17 CONVERGENCE THIS PAGE CELEBRATED IS NOW PARTIALLY UNDONE.** That block recorded *"the industry CoWoS number converged at 260k wfpm end-2027 across two houses in 24 hours"* — JPM at 260k (TSMC 200k + OSATs 60k) and UBS at 260k, off different bottom-ups. **JPM's 260k is unchanged; UBS is now at 270k. The convergence is therefore JPM 260k vs UBS 270k — a ~4% gap, which is small but real, and the SPLIT remains the genuinely contested part: JPM puts 77% of its 260k inside TSMC, while UBS's framing is materially more outsourced.**
- 🔴 **AND UBS HARDENS THE OUTSOURCING LEG: *"We believe TSMC is likely to bring in ANOTHER OSAT as an additional 'wafer-on-substrate' outsourcing partner ALONGSIDE ASE to meet the sizable capacity needs. While this would introduce some competition for ASE, we expect ASE's LEAP revenue to grow steadily to above US$7.5bn in '27E and increase further in '28E, supported by the faster ramp of full-process CoWoS, ongoing on-substrate packaging collaboration with TSMC, and continued probing outsourcing."*** ➤ **The second-OSAT call was first made on 09-16 as "tools in from 2027, volume in 2028"; it is now restated with a named consequence for the incumbent and a revenue floor attached (ASE LEAP >US$7.5bn '27E). That is the first time this page has had a dollar line under the second-source thesis.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE HYBRID-BONDING PUSH-OUT, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THE NOTE FOR THIS THEME BECAUSE OF *WHO* SAID IT: *"Likely longer use of TCB tools for HBM; hybrid bonding adoption push-out: In-line with comments from BOTH SAMSUNG AND SK HYNIX during the Tech Tour, ASMPT is seeing use case of TCB tools for HBM being extended POTENTIALLY TO HBM5, suggesting push out of VOLUME adoption ('INSERTIONS' STILL POSSIBLE) of hybrid bonding tools. COST (higher clean room requirements + more back end tools) but also recent decision at key customer to DE-SPEC HBM DENSITY (12Hi > 8Hi) may have contributed to push out with adoption. We believe ASMPT MAY HAVE SIGNED A JOINT EVALUATION PROGRAM WITH SAMSUNG on development of HBM TCB tools targeting HBM5."*** ➤ 🔴 **THREE THINGS TO SEPARATE. (1) It is a VOLUME statement, not a technology rejection — "insertions still possible" is the same distinction Redburn draws between base-die insertion (affordable, 1H27) and full/half-stack (6.0% of COGS at 200 uph). (2) The COST half of UBS's explanation IS Redburn's 31%-of-capital-cost finding, reported as a customer objection rather than as a model output — independent corroboration from the other side of the trade, one day apart. (3) ⚠️ It materially weakens the 09-15 Bernstein reading that Samsung is the vocal HB champion and Hynix the cautious laggard: UBS heard the TCB extension from BOTH, and has the TCB competitor evaluating HBM5 tools with Samsung itself.** ⚠️ **Bernstein's Outperform and its "adoption trend unchanged" conclusion are NOT withdrawn — recorded as a disagreement between two houses' channel checks.**
- 🔴 **ASMPT's LOGIC BOOK, BY CONTRAST, IS STRONG AND IS EXPANDING ONTO FOUR NEW SURFACES: >50 chip-to-substrate TCB orders from OSAT customers announced in July, with the order outlook "remaining strong on AI related CoWoS capacity expansion into 2027"; growing visibility on CHIP-TO-WAFER TCB with "more meaningful transition from FC bonders to TCB at TSMC and hence tool POR into '28/'29"; and further logic-TCB TAM upside from (1) CHIP ON PANEL — evaluation units sent to a leading logic customer (CoPoS), with an evaluation unit also set for delivery in early '27 for **[[INTC]] EMIB-T**; (2) **CPO — developing TCB AND HYBRID BONDING tools for electronic IC (EIC) on photonic IC (PIC) stacking**; and (3) AI compute TAM shifting to inferencing/CPU, supporting higher IC units per tool.** ⚠️ **Item (2) is a direct competitive entry onto the CPO hybrid-bonding leg in the same week Redburn cut that leg from 50 bonders to 15.**
- 🔭 **PROBE AND TEST GAIN CONTENT INSIDE THE PACKAGING FLOW, WHICH IS THIS THEME'S QUIET SECOND LEG: *"the move toward CHIPLET ARCHITECTURES in next-gen AI/HPC designs is set to boost demand for WAFER PROBING and associated test interfaces… probing is playing a bigger role in WEEDING OUT WEAKER DIES ahead of the more complex packaging stages."* KYEC expects robust growth as AI chip volumes across server CPUs and ASICs expand its testing TAM, with "increasing test intensity, higher power consumption, greater HBM integration and growing wafer-level probing requirements" as structural tailwinds. Same direction as the 08-26 JPM probe-thermal work already on this wiki.**
- 🔭 **AND THE DEMAND DENOMINATOR UNDER ALL OF IT, WITH THE BASIS GUARDS: accelerator (GPU+ASIC) units +32%/+40% YoY to 17.5mn/24.9mn in '26E/'27E, but total DIE count 28.3mn/46.6mn, +44%/+65% — dies per rack going 95 ('26E) → 109 ('27E) → 201 ('28E). ⚠️ DIES ≠ CHIPSETS ≠ RACKS ≠ wpm. For a packaging theme the DIE count is the closer proxy to bonding operations, and it is growing materially faster than the chipset count — which is the arithmetic that makes advanced packaging outgrow accelerator units even in a de-spec world.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-21 (/run-inbox 23h) — **THE MANAGEMENT PRIMARY ON THE SUBSTRATE BOTTLENECK ARRIVES, AND IT RELOCATES THE PROBLEM PRECISELY: [[INTC]] SAYS THE EMIB-T CONSTRAINT IS *"OBTAINING ACCEPTABLE LARGE-FORMAT SUBSTRATES AND IMPROVING SUBSTRATE SUPPLIER YIELDS RATHER THAN INTEL'S INTERNAL PACKAGING YIELD"* — AND WARNS AGAINST ASSUMING ~40% GROSS MARGINS BY LATE 2027. MEANWHILE [[AVGO]] DISCLOSES ITS OWN PACKAGING LINE WITH A DIE-COUNT LADDER OF **2 → 4 → POTENTIALLY 16 DIES PER PACKAGE**, AND THREE INDEPENDENT PARTIES PUT NPO IN 2027 AND CPO IN 2028/29.**

_Source: **Bernstein · Stacy A. Rasgon, Ph.D.** (w/ Alrick Shaw, Arpad von Nemes, Eva Zhang), [*"U.S. Semiconductors and Semicap Equipment: Notes from the road — takeaways from management meetings in Silicon Valley"*](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_260438.html), **2026-09-21** — 2-day Silicon Valley investor bus tour, 11 companies (5 covered, 6 non-covered). Ratings/TPs: **[[AVGO]] Outperform $575**, **[[NVDA]] Outperform $400**, **[[INTC]] Market-Perform $110**, **[[AMAT]] Outperform $700**. [[ALAB]] and GlobalFoundries are **not covered**; **Lightmatter is PRIVATE** (Nick Harris Founder/CEO, Simona Jankowski CFO) and appears on this page for the first time. ⚠️⚠️ **MANAGEMENT COMMENTARY RELAYED BY A BROKER — first-party statements, not a desk estimate. This is the PRIMARY behind the one-line Intel substrate-yield quote already carried on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md) from the 09-21 spec-sales relay of the same tour.**_

### 🔴🔴🔴 ① THE EMIB-T CONSTRAINT, NAMED BY THE OWNER OF THE PLATFORM — AND IT IS NOT WHERE THE STREET HAS BEEN LOOKING
- John Pitzer (Corporate VP, Treasurer and IR): *"Intel continues to target **approximately 40% gross margins for advanced packaging over time**, but **cautioned against assuming EMIB-T reaches that level by LATE 2027**. **The principal challenge is obtaining acceptable LARGE-FORMAT SUBSTRATES and improving SUBSTRATE SUPPLIER YIELDS rather than Intel's INTERNAL PACKAGING YIELD once substrates arrive.** Recent capital raising was **partly intended to secure substrate capacity**, and Intel is **working directly with suppliers to facilitate investment after many were burned by the post-COVID inventory correction.**"*
- ➤➤ **THIS SETTLES THE LAYER QUESTION THIS WIKI OPENED ON 09-21.** UBS put >2m substrates of EMIB-T capacity behind [[MEDIATEK]]'s TPU v9 with silicon-bridge yields *"targeted to cross over 50% in 1H:27"*; Intel management says the bridge is not the problem — **the ORGANIC SUBSTRATE underneath is, and the yield that matters belongs to the SUPPLIER, not to Intel.** The two statements are about different layers and are both consistent with a programme that slips. **The falsifiable marker is now explicit and dated: if EMIB-T advanced-packaging gross margin is anywhere near 40% by late 2027, management was too conservative here.**
- 🔴 **AND THE SIZE OF THE BUYER, WHICH IS NEW TO THIS PAGE: *"Intel remains the **WORLD'S LARGEST SUBSTRATE BUYER, with [[NVDA]] NOW APPROACHING SIMILAR SCALE**"*, and management *"**does not assume its EMIB-T position will remain exclusive** as competing technologies emerge."*** ➤ **Two buyers of that scale bidding for the same large-format substrate supply is the cleanest explanation on this page for why the ABF/laminate lead times logged since 09-06 have not normalised. It also means substrate is a SHARED constraint across the merchant-GPU and the custom-ASIC roadmaps — not a differentiator between them.**
- ⚠️ Corroborating from the same document, on the CPU side: *"**substrates were the BIGGER SERVER CONSTRAINT in the most recent quarter**"* — not wafers. **Third independent statement in one note that the binding layer is substrate.**

### 🔴🔴🔴 ② [[AVGO]] IS BUILDING ITS OWN ADVANCED PACKAGING — WITH A DIE-COUNT LADDER AND AN EXPLICIT *SUPPLEMENT, NOT REPLACE* FRAMING
- Hock Tan (President and CEO), Amie Thuener (CFO), Ji Yoo (Head of IR): *"Broadcom is **building proprietary advanced-packaging capacity in MALAYSIA** because future architectures become increasingly difficult to manufacture (**e.g. 2 dies per package today, to 4 next year, and potentially 16 the following year**) and the company noted that **this capacity is intended for Broadcom's OWN custom-chip customers rather than as a MERCHANT packaging business** and will **SUPPLEMENT, RATHER THAN REPLACE, CoWoS.**"*
- ➤➤ **THE DIE-COUNT LADDER IS THE MOST USEFUL NEW NUMBER IN THIS BLOCK BECAUSE IT IS A *COMPLEXITY* SERIES, NOT A CAPACITY SERIES: 2 → 4 → potentially 16 dies per package across three annual generations. This page has argued the 2.5D-vs-3D fork and the reticle ladder (5.5x → 14x, TSMC CFO, 09-09/10) without ever having a die-count. It also explains the *"supplement"* framing — a 16-die package is an integration problem the customer's own architecture team has to own, which is precisely the *"XPU + memory subsystem + networking architecture + rack design"* scope AVGO describes on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md).**
- ⚠️⚠️ **GUARDS: *"potentially 16"* is management's illustration of a direction, not a roadmap commitment; no capacity figure, no date beyond *"next year"* and *"the following year"*, and no statement of what share of AVGO packaging this represents. DO NOT net this against the industry CoWoS ladder on this page (160k wfpm exiting 2026 → 260k exiting 2027) — it is a different unit and an explicitly ADDITIVE line.**

### 🔴🔴 ③ THE CONSTRAINT STACK FROM THE LARGEST CUSTOMER, RESTATED WITH PACKAGING STILL IN IT
- [[NVDA]] (Colette Kress CFO, Toshiya Hari VP IR): NVIDIA previously indicated *"approximately **70% supply-constrained growth in FY27**, with the constraints spanning **wafers, CoWoS, memory and, increasingly, OPTICS/TRANSCEIVERS and available LAND, POWER and SHELL capacity.** Management believes **SUBSTRATES ARE MANAGEABLE** but emphasized that **virtually every part of the supply chain is being pushed aggressively.**"*
- ⚠️⚠️ **DIRECT CONFLICT WORTH RECORDING RATHER THAN RESOLVING: NVIDIA says substrates are *manageable*; Intel says substrates are the binding constraint on both EMIB-T and servers, and names NVIDIA as a buyer approaching Intel's own scale. Both are management statements from the same 48 hours. The most likely reconciliation is FORMAT — NVIDIA's packages are on a CoWoS supply chain it has contracted years ahead, while the disputed layer is LARGE-FORMAT substrate for panel-class/EMIB-T packages. NOT ADOPTED either way; the discrepancy itself is the datapoint.**
- ✅ Note also what moved UP the list: **optics/transceivers is now named explicitly in NVIDIA's constraint set**, which it was not in the constraint lists this page carried through August.

### 🔴🔴🔴 ④ THE OPTICS TIMING CONVERGES — THREE INDEPENDENT PARTIES, ONE DOCUMENT, AND THEY ALL SAY **NPO FIRST**
- **[[AVGO]] (the incumbent with the least incentive to hurry):** *"Broadcom's **400G-class SerDes allows customers to retain COPPER within AI racks for longer**, which they prefer because **copper remains cheaper, lower-latency and lower-power than optics.**"* Management's read of the competition: *"competitors discussing NPO/CPO more aggressively **partly reflects WEAKER CAPABILITIES**, whereas Broadcom can **defer the optical transition until it is economically necessary**."* Dated: *"roughly **80% of connectivity within [[GOOG]]'s scale-up topology remains COPPER**, while optical connections are already standard for longer-distance scale-out. As per-XPU bandwidth rises toward **200-400Gbps per lane** and electrical reach becomes insufficient, optics will progressively move closer to compute, but management **does not expect CPO to become a major scale-up technology until roughly 2028/29.**"*
- **[[ALAB]] (not covered — Jitendra Mohan CEO, Des Lynch CFO, Nicholas Aberle IR):** *"Management expects **copper and optical interconnects to coexist for several years** but believes growing cluster sizes will ultimately drive broader optical adoption. **Near-packaged optics (NPO) could begin appearing around 2027, while co-packaged optics (CPO) is likely further out given COST and SUPPLY CHAIN constraints.**"* Astera also has **two active NVLink Fusion engagements** and frames **UALink as a 2027 deployment story with more meaningful revenue in 2028.**
- **Lightmatter (private — Nick Harris CEO, Simona Jankowski CFO):** *"**NPO has emerged as the most important near-term opportunity, with customer trials expected in 2027 and a much larger volume ramp in 2028.** The advantage of NPO is that it **delivers much of the bandwidth and power benefit of CPO while LEVERAGING THE EXISTING PLUGGABLE OPTICS MANUFACTURING AND TEST INFRASTRUCTURE, significantly reducing implementation risk.**"* Management expects **initial product sampling in 1Q followed by shipments during 2027**, with the larger opportunity in 2028.
- ➤➤ **THREE PARTIES WITH OPPOSITE BOOKS — the copper incumbent, a neutral connectivity supplier, and a photonics start-up that sells the thing — independently place **NPO IN 2027 AND CPO NO EARLIER THAN 2028**, and the start-up's stated reason is the same one the incumbent gives (existing test/manufacturing infrastructure and economics, not physics). **THAT IS THE STRONGEST CORROBORATION CLASS THIS WIKI ACCEPTS, AND IT PUSHES BACK THE CPO INSERTION TIMING THIS PAGE HAS BEEN ARGUING SINCE 09-06.** Read with the [[TER]] CPO-test TAM ladder already on this page (~$100m 2026 → $300-700m 2028 → ~$1bn around 2030) — a 2028/29 CPO ramp is CONSISTENT with that ladder's back-end weighting and INCONSISTENT with pulling the ~$300-700m into 2027.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE COUNTERWEIGHT, FROM A FOURTH PARTY IN THE SAME DOCUMENT:** GlobalFoundries (not covered; no wiki page) expects **silicon-photonics revenue to double in 2025 and double again in 2026, reaching a $1B annualized run-rate by 2028**, and says explicitly that *"its **$1B target is largely driven by EXISTING PLUGGABLE OPTICS demand and DOES NOT REQUIRE a rapid transition to NPO or CPO architectures.**"* It holds **~300k-350k wafers of photonics-capable capacity in its New York facility** convertible as demand grows, says **capacity — not customer demand — is the primary bottleneck**, and has **>$675M of government awards across photonics ($300M) and quantum ($375M)**. ➤ **The volume money in photonics between now and 2028 is still PLUGGABLE. NPO/CPO is the architecture debate; it is not yet the revenue.**

### 🔴 ⑤ LIGHTMATTER'S OWN SPECS — FIRST TIME ON THIS PAGE, AND THEY ARE INTERCONNECT-DENSITY NUMBERS RATHER THAN PACKAGING NUMBERS
- The stated problem: *"one of the biggest challenges as scale-up domains expand is **the NUMBER OF PHYSICAL FIBERS required to connect accelerators**, particularly given that conventional links use **separate fibers for transmit and receive.**"*
- The claim: *"Lightmatter has demonstrated **16 WAVELENGTHS ON A SINGLE BIDIRECTIONAL FIBER**, allowing one fiber to perform both functions while materially increasing bandwidth density. Its link provides **800Gbps per fiber, 400Gbps in each direction.**"* Its **Guide laser platform** uses *"a **300mm wafer-based approach** to integrate large numbers of lasers"*, with configurations *"integrating **128 LASERS PER CHIP and 16 wavelengths of light**"*; the lasers can be sold standalone or power its own **Passage** interconnect platform, and the differentiation claimed is combining **interposer, laser and modulation** (micro-ring modulators, low insertion loss, bidirectional wavelength management).
- Scale and ecosystem: management *"noted that it has already demonstrated **scale-up domains incorporating roughly 131k GPUs**"*, while *"longer term expecting architectures to **move away from pure ALL-TO-ALL connectivity** because the number of required connections grows exponentially."* It is *"working with multiple compute platforms, **including [[QCOM]]**, and has **joined [[NVDA]]'s NVLink Fusion ecosystem**"*, staying compute-architecture agnostic.
- ⚠️⚠️ **EVERY NUMBER HERE IS A PRIVATE COMPANY'S OWN DEMONSTRATION CLAIM, RELAYED BY A BROKER THAT DOES NOT COVER IT. NO REVENUE, NO CUSTOMER NAMED, NO THIRD-PARTY VERIFICATION. Logged for the roadmap, NOT ADOPTED — and note that the *"131k GPUs"* is a demonstrated scale-up DOMAIN claim, which is not the same quantity as any deployed cluster size on this wiki and must not be compared to one.**

### ✅ ⑥ AND THE FRONT-END VENDOR CONFIRMS PACKAGING IS MOVING INTO THE FAB
- [[AMAT]] (Zhebo Chen, Bala Haran, Marty Parker): *"**Advanced packaging is also increasingly converging with front-end wafer manufacturing**"*, with **backside power** the early example, *"expects it to proliferate across high-performance compute, followed over time by **denser nanosheet architectures and eventually STACKED TRANSISTORS/CFET**"*; and *"**Advanced packaging is also moving into the 300mm fab.**"* AMAT also puts **advanced packaging inside the trio that supplies roughly 80% of INCREMENTAL 2027 WFE growth** (with leading-edge logic and DRAM). ➤ **The capex read-through for this page is on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md); what belongs here is the direction — packaging tool spend is migrating from the OSAT line item into the 300mm fab line item, which is exactly the accounting boundary that makes the industry CoWoS-capacity tables on this page harder to reconcile over time.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-17 (/run-inbox) — **THE INDUSTRY CoWoS NUMBER CONVERGED AT 260k wfpm END-2027 ACROSS TWO HOUSES IN 24 HOURS — AND THE SAME NOTE THAT CONFIRMS IT ALSO RETIRES CoWoS AS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT: THE DEMAND-SUPPLY GAP IS DOWN TO ~10% AND *"LEADING EDGE WAFERS AND SUBSTRATE [ARE] BECOMING THE BIGGER BOTTLENECKS."* THIRD INDEPENDENT DESK IN ONE WEEK TO POINT AT THE LAMINATE/SUBSTRATE STACK.**

(JPM · **Gokul Hariharan** / Jennifer Hsieh / David Chou / Jason Chen / Subham Singhania, *"TSMC — CoWoS and Advanced Packaging Updates"*, **2026-09-17**, 17pp. [[TSM]] Overweight, Jun-27 PT NT$3,200 vs NT$2,425.)

### 🔴🔴🔴 (1) THE CONVERGENCE — AND THE PART THAT DID *NOT* CONVERGE

JPM raises industry CoWoS capacity **+4%/+6% for 2027/28**: **TSMC 200k/225k wfpm end-2027/28**, **OSATs combined 60k/95k wfpm** → **industry 260k end-2027, 320k end-2028.**

➤ **This page recorded UBS on 2026-09-16 at *"260kwpm by end-2027 from 160kwpm at end-2026"* — the identical end-2027 number, published one day earlier, off a different bottom-up. The industry figure this page has carried as contested since 09-06 is now agreed by two houses.**

⚠️ **THE SPLIT IS STILL CONTESTED, AND THAT IS NOW THE LIVE QUESTION.** JPM puts **77% of the 260k inside TSMC** (200k), leaving 60k at OSATs. UBS's framing (09-16) has been that TSMC adds a **second wafer-on-substrate OSAT alongside ASE**, tools in from 2027 and volume in 2028 — a materially more outsourced shape. **Same total, different owner. Whoever is right determines whether the incremental margin sits at [[TSM]] or at ASE/Amkor/SPIL.**

🔴 **JPM'S OWN 09-14 NUMBER IS SUPERSEDED AND PRESERVED:** this page's 2026-09-14 entry carried JPM at *"CoWoS ~190-200k wfpm by end-2027 (+65% y/y) and SoIC as the 2028 priority at ~65k wfpm."* **Now: CoWoS end-2027 200k (top of the old range, as a point estimate); SoIC end-2028 65k → 70k, with a new end-2027 rung at 35k.** A nudge, three days later — not a re-rating.

### 🔴🔴🔴 (2) THE BOTTLENECK MOVES OFF THE PACKAGING LINE — AND THREE DESKS SAY SO IN ONE WEEK

Verbatim: *"**CoWoS demand-supply gap is now in the 10% range, and has shrunk a bit recently, with leading edge wafers and substrate becoming the bigger bottlenecks for AI customers.**"*

➤ **Put this next to what this page and its neighbours already carry from the same seven days:**

| Date | Source | The claim |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-06 | This page §(2) | A near-monopoly **substrate tool's lead time went from six months to two years** |
| **2026-09-13** | **MS Global Materials (Rachel Zhang et al.)** | **High-end glass fabric ~40% supply deficit in 2026, WORSENING in 2027; HVLP4+ copper foil ~31% deficit in 2027 (3,270 t/mo demand vs 2,495 t/mo capacity)** — see [[GLW]] |
| **2026-09-17** | **JPM (this note)** | **CoWoS gap down to ~10%; "leading edge wafers and substrate becoming the bigger bottlenecks"** |

**Three independent desks, three different methods, one conclusion: the binding constraint has moved DOWNSTREAM of CoWoS into the laminate/substrate material stack.** ⚠️ **This does NOT say CoWoS is loose — a 10% gap is still a shortage. It says the marginal accelerator is now more likely to be gated by a substrate panel than by an interposer slot.**

🔴🔴 **AND IT FEEDS THE OPEN T-GLASS DEBATE AT §(5) OF THE 2026-09-06 BLOCK BELOW, WHICH THIS PAGE EXPLICITLY LEFT UNRESOLVED ("ALL THREE RECORDED, NONE ADOPTED").** MS Global Materials supplies a **fourth, fully quantified position**: glass fabric constrained by **limited availability of Toyota weaving machines, tight high-end yarn supply and high technical barriers**, with **China prices more than doubling YTD on extremely low inventories and further hikes expected**, and a deficit path of **~40% (2026) → worse (2027) → ~30% (2028)**. ⚠️ **STILL NOT ADOPTED — logged as a fourth data point on a contested number, from a materials team rather than a semis team (i.e. a genuinely independent method). Named beneficiaries: Nittobo, Asahi Kasei, China Jushi, Taishan/Sinoma, Grace Fabric, Kingboard Laminates.**

### 🔴🔴 (3) THE 2.5D-vs-3D FORK: TSMC IS STEERING CUSTOMERS *AWAY* FROM GIANT PACKAGES, AND THE REASON IS SILICON WASTE

*"TSMC's preferred approach is for customers to leverage **3D SoIC as the additional dimension for scaling**, rather than trying to scale CoWoS-L to extremely large package sizes (**14x reticle size will only yield 3 packages per CoWoS wafer**, resulting in significant wastage of silicon)."*

- Current largest package in production: **MI450 at 5.5x reticle.**
- **AMD MI500 scaled DOWN from a planned 14-15x to ~9x**, with a **Plan B to stay at ~6x**.
- **NVDA Feynman caps at 6-7x precisely because it adopts 3D SoIC** (JPM's read: four dies in a two-stack configuration, two A16 logic compute dies per stack, I/O on separate dies), still fitting **4 compute dies + 12-16 HBM cubes**.
- Progress toward 9-10x CoWoS-L in 2027-28 is *"slightly slower"* on **thermal and warpage**.
- **Adoption split: GPU customers willing (AMD already, NVDA from Feynman); large ASIC clients (TPU, Trainium) still hesitant — MTIA 600 and OpenAI Serrano likely the first SoIC-based ASICs, 2028-29.**

⚠️🔴 **THIS IS A DIRECT TENSION WITH FIRST-PARTY GUIDANCE ALREADY ON THIS PAGE. The 2026-09-09/10 block records TSMC management putting the reticle roadmap at *"5.5x → 14x in a couple of years."* JPM's supply-chain checks say the customers are not going there — the one program that was aiming at 14-15x cut its own target to 9x. Logged as an OPEN conflict between what the foundry can offer and what the customers will buy. Neither adopted.**

### 🔴🔴 (4) SoIC — A CAPACITY LADDER AND A CONVERSION CONSTANT (⚠️ ESTIMATE-GRADE)

**TSMC SoIC: 35k wfpm end-2027 → 70k wfpm end-2028**, expansion accelerating from 2H27. JPM's own back-of-envelope: ***"~10k wfpm of SoIC capacity to support 1M accelerators (each containing 2 reticle size base dies, similar to AMD MI 350 or 450)."***

⚠️ **DO NOT PROMOTE THIS TO A CANONICAL CONSTANT.** JPM itself flags that *"converting SoIC capacity to chip output is not as straightforward as CoWoS, given the **wide variance in bottom-die specifications and yield**."* It is specified for a 2-base-die accelerator and breaks for any other configuration. Using it to back out units without stating a yield and die-count assumption reproduces exactly the basis error this wiki keeps catching elsewhere.

### 🔴 (5) COST, CoPoS AND EMIB-T — THREE SMALLER MOVES

- **EMIB-like (RDL on substrate) is up to 50% cheaper than an equivalent-reticle CoWoS package at optimal yields**, and demand is rising from fabless customers on that basis. **TSMC is itself exploring the approach with substrate vendors Unimicron and Kinsus** and with OSATs. ⚠️ **Treat as the SAME claim already on [[INTC]] ("~50% cheaper") viewed from the foundry side — not independent confirmation.**
- **CoPoS SLIPS:** *"progressing more slowly than expected, with risk production likely only in 2028 and mass production in 2029 at the earliest"* — cause is **warpage and glass cracking**. Corroborated from the OSAT side (ASE's PLP equivalents *"not expected to reach mass production before 2029"*). ⚠️ **[[themes/outros-asia]]'s UBS call on GPTC share rising to 70-80% in CoPoS is unaffected in SHARE terms; the DATE it applies to moves out.**
- **EMIB-T reads READY despite the yield noise this page logged on 09-06:** on track to ramp **early 2028 with TPUv9 the primary product**, plus some Trainium 4. **Substrate-level yields ~50% at leading substrate partners; final packaging yields 90-95%** — *"sufficient to go into mass production in 12-15 months."* Value capture sits **with substrate vendors** (substrate formation, TCB die-attach for embedded bridges, lamination); OSATs get wafer bumping; **final package mounting and test stays in-house at [[INTC]] (Arizona or Malaysia).** ➤ **This partially settles the 09-06 item where three houses converged on execution and the tool but NOT on yield: JPM now puts a number on both legs of the yield question.**

## 🆕🔴 2026-09-17 — **AMKOR'S ARIZONA PHASE 2 FINALLY CARRIES A DOLLAR FIGURE — *"NEARLY DOUBLING THE CAMPUS, $12B TOTAL"* — AND JPM READS IT AS THE ANSWER TO THE *"WILL IT FILL?"* QUESTION.**

_Source: J.P. Morgan · **Harlan Sur** — **Fall Series 2026 Webinar — Semis & Semi Cap Equipment**, 2026-09-17 10:30am ET (w/ Mayur Ramdhani) (pasted exhibit; broker/analyst confirmed by Felipe 2026-09-17, deck identified from the Fall Series webinar invitations in Felipe's Outlook, 2026-09-15 / 09-16; deck PDF not on disk, NetRoadshow-gated), slide *"SMID Cap Semis: Selective on Opportunities Within The Group"*. ⚠️⚠️ **AMKR IS NOT A COVERED NAME ON THIS WIKI — no page, no folder, no house model. This is filed as a CAPACITY read-through to the advanced-packaging chain only. The rating and PT are recorded as JPM's, not adopted.**_

- 🔴 **THE SIZING, WHICH IS NET-NEW:** *"**AZ Phase 2 (nearly doubling the campus, $12B total)** — confirms **demand > supply** and answers the 'will it fill?' question."* JPM rates **AMKR OW, PT $85**, on the thesis that it is *"the only U.S. HVM advanced-packaging OSAT, levered to one of the tightest links in the AI supply chain."* ✅ **THIS PAGE AND [[BESI]] ALREADY CARRY THE ANNOUNCEMENT BUT NOT THE NUMBER:** Rothschild (· Rowan Adley, 09-15) referenced *"Amkor's Arizona Phase 2 announcement last week"* when reporting that **ASE subsidiary SPIL is in the final stages of an Arizona advanced-packaging fab** — relayed with no capex figure attached. **$12bn for the total campus is the first dollar sizing on this wiki for the US back-end build.** ➤ **Read the two together and the US advanced-packaging leg now has two OSATs committing inside one fortnight (Amkor Phase 2; SPIL entering), against TSMC's own *"Phase 4 and the first phase of advanced packaging in the US"* already on [[TSM]]. That is a capacity-broadening datapoint for [[BESI]] and the die-attach/hybrid-bonding suppliers, and it is the supply-side counterpart to the CoWoS tightness this page tracks.**
- ⚠️ **HANDLE THE *"DEMAND > SUPPLY"* CLAUSE AS AN INFERENCE, NOT A DISCLOSURE.** JPM's argument is that a near-doubling of the campus answers the fill-rate question by revealed preference — Amkor would not double it without demand. **That is a reasonable read and it is not evidence: no utilisation, no committed volume, no customer and no start date were given on the slide.** ➤ **The falsifiable version worth tracking: does Phase 2 come with a named anchor customer or a prepayment, the way the memory LTAs on [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md) did?**
- **CONTEXT FROM THE SAME SLIDE, LOGGED BECAUSE IT EXPLAINS A SEGMENT OPTIC RATHER THAN THE AI STORY:** *"Record 2Q, record 3Q guide — beat driven by **AI compute and auto**, not handset seasonality"*, and *"**Comms air-pocket is by design** — trading SiP volume for higher-margin advanced packaging capacity in Korea."* ➤ **A deliberate mix rotation out of consumer SiP and into advanced packaging is the OSAT-level version of the capacity reallocation this page tracks at the foundries — worth noting that the capacity is being created by CONVERSION as well as by new build.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-16 (/wiki-ingest 21h — UBS Taiwan Summit, Days 2-3) — **UBS RAISES INDUSTRY CoWoS CAPACITY TO 260kwpm BY END-2027 FROM 160kwpm AT END-2026 (+62.5% IN ONE YEAR), AND SAYS TSMC MAY ADD A SECOND "WAFER-ON-SUBSTRATE" OSAT ALONGSIDE ASE — TOOLS IN FROM 2027, VOLUME IN 2028**

_(**UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Ryan Sun** — "Asia Semiconductor Insight: Takeaways from UBS Taiwan Summit 2026 – Days 2 and 3", 2026-09-16; meetings with ASE, Aspeed, All Ring, Hon Precision, Silergy, Vanguard, Realtek, FOCI · plus **DigiTimes** relays via the UBS TECH and Barclays TMT desks)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE CAPACITY NUMBER, AND IT IS A RAISE: *"CoWoS industry capacity expansion continues to accelerate into 2027-28. We recently raised CoWoS industry capacity to **260kwpm by end of 2027, up from 160kwpm end of 2026**."* **That is +100kwpm, +62.5%, in twelve months — and it is an INDUSTRY figure, not a [[TSM]]-only figure, which is the distinction this page has repeatedly had to enforce.
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL CLAIM UNDERNEATH IT — A SECOND WoS OUTSOURCER: UBS believes **TSMC could introduce a NEW OSAT partner as an additional "wafer on substrate" outsourcing partner on top of ASE**, with that partner **moving tools in from 2027 for volume production in 2028**. Even with the competition, **ASE is still expected to grow LEAP revenue to US$7.5bn+ in 2027** and higher in 2028 on full-process CoWoS, continued on-substrate collaboration and probing outsourcing. **Read: TSMC is choosing to meet the incremental WoS requirement with partner capacity rather than with its own capex — capital-light at the margin, and a direct positive for the OSAT chain. It also means "CoWoS capacity" increasingly is not all TSMC's, which changes who captures the packaging dollar.
- 🔴 **AND A TEST READ-THROUGH THAT BELONGS TO [[TER]] AND [[ADVANTEST]]: *"AI/HPC's trend toward chiplet in next generation products should create larger demand for probing test and related test interface… probing's importance is also increasing to identify lower performance dies before the complicated packaging process."* **Known-good-die screening BEFORE an expensive package is the cleanest economic argument for test intensity this page has carried, and it is now coming from the packaging side rather than from the testers' own IR.
- 🔴 **A COMPETING PACKAGING PATH, NET-NEW: DigiTimes reports **[[SAMSUNG]] (with SEMCO) and [[QCOM]] are jointly developing ORGANIC BRIDGES for 2.1D packaging** — the lower-cost alternative to silicon-interposer 2.5D for high-bandwidth die-to-die links. **Worth tracking precisely because it does NOT consume CoWoS capacity: if 2.1D organic bridges take the mobile/PC-class high-bandwidth tier, the CoWoS queue is relieved at the low end rather than at the AI end.** ⚠️ trade-press sourced, no timeline or product attached.**
- 🔭 **Also from the same relay set: **Hanmi Semiconductor reportedly lands its first back-end foothold at Terafab** (DigiTimes) — logged as an equipment-side datapoint for the Terafab build that [themes/semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md) has been tracking at ~$2bn of WFE in 2027/28.**


## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/run-inbox — GS Communacopia 2026 MANAGEMENT compile, dated 2026-09-13) — **[[TER]] MANAGEMENT PUTS A THREE-POINT TAM LADDER ON CPO TEST (~$100m 2026 → $300-700m 2028 → ~$1bn AROUND 2030) AND — MORE USEFUL — A FIRST-PARTY VENDOR MAP OF WHO IS STRONGEST AT WHICH INSERTION, WHICH DROPS STRAIGHT INTO THE UBS-vs-NOMURA INSERTION-2 FIGHT THIS PAGE LOGGED ON 09-06. PLUS THE CLEANEST STATEMENT YET OF WHY NPO IS THE BRIDGE: IT REMOVES THE MOST YIELD-SENSITIVE FINAL PACKAGE INSERTION.**

_(**MANAGEMENT SOURCE, NOT GOLDMAN SACHS RESEARCH** — §13 Teradyne of the analyst's own conference compile from **Goldman Sachs' Communacopia + Technology Conference 2026, San Francisco**, built from the **09-09 GS Communacopia transcript PLUS a separate CEO small-group meeting**. ⚠️⚠️ **No rating, no price target and no house estimate anywhere in the document — do not attribute any line below to GS as a call.** [FULL SUMMARY - UPDATED, 2026-09-13](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GS_Conference_2026_FULL_SUMMARY_UPDATED_2026-09-13.html).)_ ⚠️ **DEDUP: the [[TSM]] packaging content from the same conference week — CoWoS outsourced to OSATs, advanced packaging "low-teens percentage of the business", the 5.5x → 14x reticle roadmap — is ALREADY on this page in the 09-09/10 Capstone company-meeting block below, from the primary transcripts. NOT re-inserted.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE CPO TEST-TAM LADDER, WITH TWO NEW ANCHORS EITHER SIDE OF THE ONE THIS WIKI ALREADY HAD: *"2026 silicon-photonics CPO test-equipment TAM estimated around **$100m**; 2028 estimate **$300-700m**; management sees **~$1bn test-equipment TAM around 2030**"*** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Teradyne meeting, 2026-09-13). ➤ **The $300-700m 2028 figure has been on [[TER]] since the Q2 CY26 call (2026-07-29) and is NOT new — what is new is that it now has a base year and a terminal year, which turns a point estimate into a slope: roughly **3-7x from 2026 to 2028**, then a further **1.4-3.3x to 2030**. Read it against the 07-29 primary's own warning that 2027 is guided to the LOW end (*"the low-end would be in the $200 million range"*), i.e. the ladder is back-loaded and the 2028-30 legs carry all of it.** ⚠️ **Vendor estimate of an equipment TAM, not an industry forecast — same order of magnitude as the burn-in SAM on [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md) ($626m 2026 → $2.0bn 2030), which is itself the useful cross-check.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE INSERTION MAP, FIRST-PARTY — AND IT IS A THIRD INPUT INTO THE OPEN FIGHT THIS PAGE RECORDED ON 09-06: *"For the first major device, **Teradyne is strongest at insertion 2 and insertion 4; [[ADVANTEST]] is strongest at insertion 1; a Taiwanese supplier is strongest at insertion 3**"*** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Teradyne meeting, 2026-09-13). ➤ **This page carries UBS and Nomura publishing OPPOSITE reads of insertion 2 on the same day (09-06): UBS says the trend *"may be to streamline insertion 2… and lean more toward insertion 3"*; Nomura says certain test items *"cannot be covered by subsequent test insertions"* and that insertion 2 gates foundry yield and supply-chain accountability. **The commercial stake is now explicit: insertion 2 is a Teradyne position and insertion 3 is a Taiwanese supplier's.** So UBS's "streamline 2, lean to 3" is, in vendor terms, a share transfer AWAY from Teradyne — and Teradyne's own management (07-29 primary, already on [[TER]]) argues insertion 2 CANNOT be skipped, because the EIC is bonded to the PIC wafer and a polymer lens is applied between the two insertions. ⚠️ That makes Teradyne an INTERESTED PARTY in the UBS/Nomura dispute, which is exactly why this is logged as a third position and NOT as a resolution. The fight stays open.** ⚠️ **The "Taiwanese supplier" is unnamed; this page's candidates at the handler/optical layer are ficonTEC (not Taiwanese), Chroma, MPI, WinWay and Hon Precision — **do not guess**. Also note Teradyne's own caution on the taxonomy: *"we were a good 15 minutes into the conversation before we discovered that we and the customer had an entirely different definition of what insertion 2 was."***
- 🔴🔴 **WHY NPO IS THE BRIDGE, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS A YIELD ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A COST ONE: *"**NPO removes the most yield-sensitive final package insertion and can bridge the industry toward full CPO**"*, with *"copper remains important for scale-up over the next few years; **pluggables grow through 2026-27 and level off around 2028**; NPO/CPO inflect later"*** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Teradyne meeting, 2026-09-13). ➤ **This page has carried the NPO-as-bridge thesis from brokers (Mizuho 08-17 "2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO"; the 09-06 UBS sequencing of *"mass production for scale-out now through 2027, hybrid scale up 2028-29, full CPO scale up 2029-30"*). A test vendor supplies the MECHANISM the broker versions leave implicit: the reason NPO can carry volume ahead of CPO is that it takes the highest-risk insertion out of the FINAL package, where accumulated value is greatest — which is the same compound-yield logic behind the latent-defect argument this page already cross-files to [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md).** ✅ **Consistent with UBS's sequencing and with the "pluggables level off ~2028" shape; ⚠️ not a capacity or unit number, and it does not touch any CoWoS/SoIC wfpm ladder on this page.**
- ⚠️ **NOT RE-INSERTED, BECAUSE THIS PAGE ALREADY HOLDS THE PRIMARY:** the compile's conference-level line that *"multi-die packages raise the cost of latent defects and force more test insertions"* is the same claim this page has carried since 2026-08-01 from the [[TER]] Q2 CY26 call — *"a single latent defect has a greater impact on final device yield, raising test intensity per die in multi-chip packages"* (see the cross-theme bullet under the Aug-01 block). **A conference paraphrase of a primary already on the page is not a second source and was not logged as one.**

Other pulls named: **agentic-AI CPUs** and **rising ASIC volumes (TPU v8, Trn3)**.

Both halves matter; the wiki has carried the bull version of this argument unchallenged.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — **PACKAGING & TEST CAPEX GOES TO +36% Y/Y AT US$33bn FOR 2027, THE SUBSTRATE SCARE OF THE WEEK (BROADCOM/TOPPAN SINGAPORE) IS RULED NON-INCREMENTAL, AND TSMC PUSHES CROSS-GENERATION TOOL FUNGIBILITY.**
- 🔴 **BofA raises 2027 industry capex: LOGIC FOUNDRY +23% y/y to US$105bn and PACKAGING & TESTING +36% y/y to US$33bn — "the spending strength continues to be led by [[TSM]] but now SUPPLEMENTED BY COMMITMENT FROM OSATs" — with utilisation staying high in '27 across advanced nodes AND PACKAGING "even with aggressive expansion", and ADVANCED-TECHNOLOGY PRICING RISING 10-15%+ Y/Y IN '27** (BofA · **Haas Liu**, "Semicon Taiwan: AI drives innovation across the semiconductor stack", 2026-09-07/08). ➤ *Packaging capex growing 13 points faster than logic foundry capex is the numerical version of what this page has been arguing qualitatively all quarter.*
- 🔴 **THE METHOD CHANGE, WHICH IS NEW: "foundries and OSATs are COLLABORATING FOR STCO (SYSTEM-TECHNOLOGY CO-OPTIMIZATION) as the technology development is SHIFTING FROM SERIAL TO PARALLEL across chip production, package, materials and system players. Specifically, TSMC noted the importance of higher-level BACK-END TOOL AUTOMATION AND MODULARIZATION as it could REDUCE TIME FOR RE-VALIDATION and provide the supply chain with HIGHER LEVEL OF FUNGIBILITY FOR DIFFERENT GENERATIONS OF TECHNOLOGIES."** ➤➤ **Two consequences, and they are opposite. FOR TSMC/OSATs: parallel development plus tool fungibility compresses the time from node to package and raises capital efficiency — unambiguously good. FOR THE BACK-END TOOL VENDORS ([[BESI]], [[DISCO]], [[ADVANTEST]], ASMPT): "fungibility for different generations" is the polite phrase for FEWER NEW TOOLS PER GENERATION. This is the same structural argument [[ASML]]'s CFO made on 09-01/04 about productivity upgrades adding ~45% of wafer capacity out of the installed base — and this page should treat it as the same risk arriving at the back end.**
- 🔴 **AND THE SUBSTRATE SCARE OF THE WEEK IS DISMISSED — CAREFULLY. Nan Ya PCB fell 12-13% in two sessions (vs Taiex +~1%) on worries about SG-based substrate capacity from a Broadcom/Toppan JV. BofA: "Broadcom/Toppan's JV in Singapore is NOT A NEW/ADDITIONAL CAPACITY for substrate in our view, given that IT TAKES AT LEAST 1-2 YEARS to have the relevant facility ready ahead of the MP IN FY27" — and it is already inside BofA's August-2026 ABF S/D model** (BofA · **Mike Yang**, "NYPCB", 2026-09-07, Buy, PO NT$1,700 vs NT$1,055).
- ➤ **For this page the durable fact is not the supply relief (there isn't any) — it is that [[AVGO]] IS PUTTING ITS NAME ON UPSTREAM SUBSTRATE CAPACITY WITH AN FY27 MASS-PRODUCTION DATE, at the moment MS names ABF substrate capacity as one of the two hard AI bottlenecks ("lack of material capacity growth (ABF substrates)" and "back-end-of-line complexity of HBM4E" — MS · Shawn Kim, 2026-09-07). File under vertical integration.**
- **BT-substrate pricing, from the same NYPCB note: "such product ASP has been RAISED THREE TIMES YTD", 4Q pricing "still under negotiation", CCL price "still on the rise in 3Q26", and BofA now bakes in an 8% ASP HIKE for NYPCB's BT substrate business. Copper remains elevated and gold has come down from 2Q26; together they "account for 30-35% of total production costs for BT substrate."**
- 🔭 **CCL supply-side, sized: BofA initiated DOOSAN CORP at Buy (PO W1,800,000, ~50%+ implied upside) estimating Doosan holds "50%+" SHARE OF [[NVDA]] GPU CCL, plus "a solid position in [[AMZN]]'s switches and Korean memory packaging", with CCL sales of W4-5tn+ p.a. at 30% OPM in 2027-28E on new capacity in Korea/China/Thailand. Doosan is also acquiring 71% of SK SILTRON (a silicon-wafer maker) for W2.3tn by January 2027** (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-09-07). ➤ *A named CCL supplier with a share number and a margin is new for this page; and a CCL/laminate group buying a wafer maker is a second data point on vertical integration running UP the stack, not down.*
- **Jefferies/Fubon's SEMICON booth-tour takeaways, same weekend: "(1) ADVANCED PACKAGING BECOMES CORE ENGINE OF AI SYSTEM SCALING; (2) TEST, METROLOGY, OPTICAL I/O GAIN CONTENT AS AI COMPLEXITY RISES; (3) AI EXPANDS SEMICONDUCTOR PYRAMID BEYOND LEADING-EDGE NODES"** (Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, 2026-09-06).

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-06 — **THREE HOUSES COVER *ONE EVENT* (SEMICON TAIWAN 2026, 31 AUG – 4 SEPT) AND PUBLISH THE SAME DAY: THE CoWoS RAISE IS ENTIRELY *OUTSOURCED*, A NEAR-MONOPOLY SUBSTRATE TOOL'S LEAD TIME HAS GONE FROM SIX MONTHS TO TWO YEARS, T-GLASS SPLITS THREE WAYS INSIDE 24 HOURS, AND CPO TEST INSERTION 2 IS AN OPEN FIGHT BETWEEN TWO OF THE THREE.**

_Sources, all 2026-09-06: **UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri / Ryan Sun et al** — *"UBS Global I/O Semiconductors: SEMICON Taiwan 2026 — 5 key takeaways for advanced packaging, advanced logic, CPO & test"* (note dated 09-07, received 09-06); **Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan / Daniel Yen / Daisy Dai / Tiffany Yeh / Lucas Wang / Henry Zhao / Ethan Jia** — *"Greater China Semiconductors: SEMICON Taiwan 2026 key takeaways — we need more"* (11pp, Industry View Attractive); **Nomura · Aaron Jeng / Donnie Teng / Eric Chen / Vivian Yang / Anne Lee** — *"Greater China Semi — Equipment supply, photonics, and thermal"*; plus **Morgan Stanley · Howard Kao / Irene Yen / Andy Meng** — *"Greater China Technology Hardware: ABF Substrate: Risk to NYPCB and Unimicron ABF Supply Share?"* (9pp, Industry View In-Line). Event scale: **1,300+ exhibitors and 4,300 booths (vs 1,200 companies / 4,100 booths in 2025), 100,000+ visitors** (UBS)._

**UBS, verbatim:** *"We raise **CoWoS industry capacity by end-2027 to 260kwpm from 250kwpm, up from 160kwpm end-2026**. We **keep TSMC's capacity at 180kwpm end-2027** but **lift our ASE estimate to 60kwpm (from 50kwpm)** and **keep Amkor at 20kwpm**."*

| End-2027 CoWoS capacity (UBS) | Prior UBS mark | New UBS mark | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Industry total** | 250kwpm | **260kwpm** | **+10kwpm** |
| [[TSM]] own | 180kwpm | **180kwpm** | **unchanged** |
| **ASE** | ~50kwpm (end-27E) | **60kwpm** | **+10kwpm — the entire raise** |
| Amkor | 20kwpm | **20kwpm** | unchanged |
| _Memo: industry end-2026_ | — | **160kwpm** | — |

➜ **THE WHOLE RAISE IS OUTSOURCED CAPACITY.** ⚠️ **And UBS attaches the reason in terms, which is the bearish sentence for [[TSM]] inside an otherwise bullish note: *"With the incremental progress made on CoPoS, **TSMC appears to become slightly more conservative about its own CoWoS expansion into H2'27 and may lean more toward ASE for CoW outsourcing**."*** ➤ **This page has tracked "value migration to OSATs" as a thesis since May. It is now a published capacity table: on UBS's own numbers TSMC falls from 180/250 = 72% to 180/260 = 69% of end-2027 industry CoWoS-equivalent, and the marginal wafer is ASE's.** ✅ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED ON TSMC'S OWN LINE — 180kwpm end-2027 is the same figure this page has carried since Fubon 07-08 and which Fubon restated verbatim as MAINTAINED on 08-03. Three houses, one number, unmoved for two months.** ⚠️ **The ASE figure IS superseded (prior UBS mark ~50kwpm end-27E) — old value archived in `## Changelog` below.**

- **CoPoS — RESTATED, NOT MOVED, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** UBS: *"We believe TSMC's goal is to finalize **310x310mm² process and equipment selections by mid-2027 to mass produce in 2028**,"* with equipment suppliers *"becoming more constructive"* about it. ➤ **Identical to UBS's own 07-27 note already transcribed on this page (pilot line installed late Q2'26, first-generation 310×310mm², mid-2027 lock-in, 2028 MP). A restatement six weeks later with no date slip is corroboration, and the mid-2027 selection lock-in remains the single most trackable CoPoS milestone this page carries.** ⚠️ **The CoPoS MP date remains CONTESTED across the page and today's note does not settle it: UBS/Fubon **2028** · Mizuho **pull-in to 2H27E-1H28E** (08-09) then **2028E** (08-16, its own revision) · DIGITIMES/@QQ_Timmy **~2029** · Fubon models **20-30kwpm CoPoS at end-2028** with MP timing *"still uncertain."* All logged, none adopted.**
