### 🔴🔴 (2) THE HARDEST NEW NUMBER OF THE DAY — A NEAR-MONOPOLY SUBSTRATE TOOL GOES FROM SIX MONTHS TO TWO YEARS

**Nomura, verbatim:** *"IC substrate supply additions could be constrained by an extending equipment lead time… the lead time of **Nikko-Materials' ABF vacuum laminator has risen to TWO YEARS, vs SIX MONTHS in April/May**. Nikko-Materials' laminators have a **near monopolistic position in high-end ABF substrates**, but its **tool capacity expansion appears to be slower than existing substrate makers' expansion**."*

➜ **A ~4x lead-time extension in roughly four months, on a tool with no meaningful second source, at the exact layer three of the largest leading-edge buyers pointed to on 09-02 as the binding constraint.** ➤ **THE STRUCTURAL POINT, and it is why this belongs at the top of the page rather than in the substrate sub-section: the bottleneck has now recursed one level. The 09-02 block on this page has [[AVGO]], [[NVDA]] and [[INTC]] all naming SUBSTRATES as the constraint; today Nomura says the substrate makers' own expansion is gated by a TOOL, and its supplier is expanding more slowly than they are. And one layer above, TSMC's Deputy Co-COO said the same thing about WFE (see (3)). **Tool lead time is now the named constraint at three consecutive layers of the chain — wafer fab equipment → laminators → substrates → packaging.** ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED as a capacity cut: Nomura gives no wpm or unit impact, and no substrate maker's guide has moved on it. It is a lead-time datapoint, and the cheapest falsifier is Ibiden's / Unimicron's / Kinsus's own capex-timing commentary at their next results.**

### 🔴 (3) TOOL SUPPLY, SAID BY THE CUSTOMER — FIRST-PARTY MANAGEMENT COMMENTARY FROM THE CEO FORUM FIRESIDE

⚠️ **These are MANAGEMENT quotes relayed by MS from the SEMICON CEO Forum, not analyst opinion.**

- **[[TSM]] Deputy Co-COO Mr. Hou** — he *"had **never seen demand growing and changing this frequently as it has over the past six months**, while the **tool delivery from WFE partners are still not enough**."* ➤ **Corroborates the Nikko item above from the opposite end of the same chain, and corroborates the DIRECTION of the trade-press headline this wiki logged on 09-05 (*"TSMC fab equipment demand nearly doubles in six months… tool shortages"*) — different source, same claim, and this one is the company.**
- **[[MEDIATEK]] CEO Mr. Tsai** *"was asking for more capacity support from **ASE, TSMC, and Unimicron**."* ➤ **A named customer publicly queuing at all three layers this page tracks: back-end, front-end, and ABF substrate. Read against (1): the incremental end-2027 CoWoS capacity is at ASE, one of the three he named.**
- **MS's own framing: *"Taiwan is still one of the bottlenecks of AI infrastructure buildout."*** With *"memory becoming the major bottleneck and cost item for AI capex,"* investors are testing whether **CPO can replace copper connectors in XPU scale-up**, the objective being *"to reduce per-token cost."*

### 🔴🔴 (4) EMIB-T — THREE HOUSES, ONE DAY, CONVERGING ON EXECUTION AND ON THE *TOOL*, AND STILL NOT ON YIELD

- **UBS:** *"industry feedback is **positive regarding the yield improvement and overall execution**, consistent with what we highlighted in July. We are constructive on EMIB-T's supply supporting **>2m substrate volume for MediaTek's TPU v9 demand in 2028**. **Thermal compression bonder (TCB) demand should pick up strongly** for silicon bridge integration in substrates. We believe **Toray in Japan is the current leading supplier. ASMPT is developing the tool for qualification in early 2027**."*
- **MS:** *"We visited the **Toray** booth to check EMIB-T substrate manufacturing yield, because the company supplies the **TCB bonder**. We expect **[[INTC]] to provide technical support**, and expect **[[MEDIATEK]]'s TPU v9 (HumuFish) to get sufficient EMIB-T substrate supply in 2028**."*
- **Nomura:** *"**Toray Engineering's die bonders are the critical tools for bridge die embedding at Intel's EMIB-T substrate partners**. We notice the **lead time of the die bonder is around 12 months, and the move-in schedule is MANAGED BY INTEL with planned shipments through end-2027E**."* Plus the two named manufacturing challenges: *"**coefficient of thermal expansion (CTE) mismatch between silicon and organic films**, and **build-up layer cavity precision**."* And on the litmus test: *"**Google's potential reliance on Intel's EMIB-T for the next-generation TPU v9 (partnering with MediaTek) could be a CRITICAL LITMUS TEST for Intel's advanced packaging capabilities.**"*

✅ **WHAT THE THREE AGREE ON, INDEPENDENTLY: (a) execution/yield feedback is positive; (b) **2028 substrate supply is adequate for TPU v9** (UBS and MS, separately); (c) **Toray is the critical TCB / die-bonder supplier**, named by all three from three different booths, with **ASMPT only qualifying a rival tool in early 2027** — a single-supplier dependency this page did not previously carry; (d) Intel controls the ramp's tool schedule at its partners' plants, booked to end-2027E.**

⚠️⚠️ **WHAT NONE OF THEM DOES: publish a yield number. The EMIB-T yield spread on this wiki is therefore UNTOUCHED and remains its widest open contradiction — **Mizuho >97%** (08-16) · **UBS ~90%** measured on MediaTek's v9 prototype trial production runs (07-27) · **Fubon ~75% in production**, calling ~90% *"lab only"* (08-03) · **JPM ~60%+** (07-21) · **Unimicron: "not before 2027"** · against **Google's own stated 95%+ precondition** (Fubon 07-08). ➤ **Nomura's two manufacturing challenges are the first PHYSICS-level candidate explanation this page has for that spread — CTE mismatch and cavity precision are both substrate-side and both present as yield VARIANCE rather than a fixed level, which would let a 60% check and a 97% check both be honest. **LOGGED AS A HYPOTHESIS, EXPLICITLY NOT ADOPTED AS THE EXPLANATION.**

⚠️ **BASIS FLAG on the volume anchor: UBS's ">2m" is a **SUBSTRATE** count. It sits against JPM's **~3.0mn v9 UNITS in 2028** (raised from ~2mn, 07-21) and GS's ~3mn. Consistent only under a substrates-per-package assumption nobody publishes — **NOT netted.**

**Programme naming, settled here once because two of today's three houses drop the customer from the name:** UBS and MS write *"MediaTek's TPU v9"*; Nomura writes *"Google's… next-generation TPU v9 (partnering with MediaTek)."* **Same programme: [[GOOG]] = customer · [[MEDIATEK]] = ASIC/design partner · [[INTC]] = advanced packaging (EMIB-T) · TORAY = TCB bonder. Codename "HumuFish."** Full map on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md).

**Nomura also marks Unimicron constructively:** *"benefiting from more favorable pricing conditions amid supply bottleneck… **EMIB-T with manageable risk-reward**, and large reticle-size 2.5D"* (Buy).

### ⚠️⚠️🔴 (5) T-GLASS IS A THREE-WAY DISAGREEMENT AS OF 2026-09-06 — ALL THREE RECORDED, NONE ADOPTED

**Three positions published within 24 hours of each other, pointing in three different directions on the same input:**

| # | Source | Position | Basis given |
|---|---|---|---|
| **(a)** | **Morgan Stanley · Howard Kao / Irene Yen / Andy Meng** — *"ABF Substrate: Risk to NYPCB and Unimicron ABF Supply Share?"*, **2026-09-06** | *"We have expected **T glass supply constraints to peak in 2026 and gradually ease from 2027 as more suppliers are qualified**. Thus, it **would not surprise us if raw-material-driven price hikes plateau** going forward."* | Supplier qualification pipeline; substrate price hikes since 2H25 attributed to **raw-material cost inflation (T glass, gold, copper)** |
| **(b)** | **@zephyr_z9** (X, corpus), **2026-09-06** ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT ACCOUNT, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model** | *"**T-Glass shortage is about to get worse.**"* | ⚠️ **NONE STATED** |
| **(c)** | **@jukan05** (X, corpus), **2026-09-06**, relaying **Kinsus' August commentary** ⚠️ **RELAY — an independent X account relaying a substrate maker's own commentary; name both layers** | *"**High-end ABF production remains constrained by T-Glass shortages, preventing the company from capturing approximately 10–15% of its potential monthly revenue.**"* | **A substrate maker quantifying revenue it is losing RIGHT NOW** |

➤ **THE SHAPE OF THE DISAGREEMENT MATTERS MORE THAN THE DIRECTION: (a) is a FORWARD supply-qualification call, (c) is a CURRENT realised-loss measurement, and (b) is a directional assertion with no method. They are not strictly mutually exclusive — a shortage can be acute today and still peak this year — but (a) and (b) cannot both be right about 2027 and **this page adopts neither.** ⚠️ **AND A TENSION INSIDE MS'S OWN COVERAGE, FLAGGED NOT RESOLVED: this page already carries MS · Howard Kao (07-27) saying **ABF undersupply RETURNS from 2027 with a ~25% gap by 2030** and *"a ~20% supply/demand gap in low-CTE glass cloth (T-glass)."* Today the same analyst has T-glass EASING from 2027. Those are different materials in the same note-series (ABF film vs T-glass cloth), and MS keeps **OW on Unimicron and NYPCB precisely because its thesis rests on ABF tightness, which it does NOT call as easing** — so it is reconcilable, but the two dates should never be quoted as one view. **The adjudicating observable is cheap: whether substrate price hikes actually plateau in 4Q26/1H27.**

⚠️ **DEDUP, IMPORTANT — THE KINSUS NUMBERS IN (c) ARE ALREADY ON THIS PAGE AND WERE NOT RE-ENTERED.** The 08-27 block above already carries, from **Jefferies · Conor O'Mara (2026-08-26)** and **@zephyr_z9 (2026-08-27)**, the identical August commentary: **10-15% of potential monthly revenue unrealised**, **+25% high-end ABF capacity in 2027** with material allocations already secured and built at **Taiwan** facilities *"dismissing market speculation that existing BT production lines could be converted to ABF"*, **BT utilisation ~75% → ~85%**, and **2027-2029 customer capacity planning starting in 4Q**. ➤ **@jukan05's 09-06 post is a THIRD independent relay of the same underlying August commentary. That is corroboration of the transcription, not new information, and it is logged as such so the datapoint is not triple-counted as three separate shortages.**

- ✅ **NET-NEW FROM (c), AND THE ONLY GENUINELY NEW KINSUS CONTENT: the OPTICAL substrate leg gets a margin. *"The transition from **800G to 1.6T and 3.2T requires finer circuitry and better signal integrity, driving demand for mSAP**. Optical-communications substrates have **higher layer counts, ASPs, and margins**, with the project expected to generate a **gross margin of approximately 20%**."* Plus: Kinsus *"will make investment decisions based on **actual orders**"* for the 2027-2029 round. ➤ **A ~20% GM on optical substrates against Kinsus's corporate base is the first substrate-level economics this page has for the CPO/optical build-out, and it says the optical leg is accretive but not transformational — useful as a floor when the page's CPO section argues about who captures the value.**

### 🔴🔴 (6) CPO — THE TIMELINE, AND A DIRECT DISAGREEMENT ON TEST INSERTION 2 BETWEEN UBS AND NOMURA

**The sequencing (UBS):** CPO progresses toward *"**mass production for scale-out from now through 2027, hybrid scale up in 2028-29, and full CPO scale up in 2029-30**."* And **[[TSM]] showcased its COUPE roadmap to deliver 200G/lane in 2026 and scale to 400G/lane by 2030** — the first lane-rate ladder for COUPE on this page, and it should be read against the `Reference framework — TSMC COUPE` section below, whose 100G-per-lane FOWLP ceiling is precisely what forces [[AVGO]] onto COUPE.

**The architecture question (MS):** *"future GPU/ASIC scale-up would depend on CPO/NPO — '**inter-rack CPO connectivity remains Nvidia's preferred direction**'. For example, the **NV576 Rubin Ultra in 2027 may use CPO switch trays to connect eight NVL72 server racks**."* ⚠️ **A 2027 configuration described as a possibility ("may"), not a disclosure. Logged as such.**

⚠️⚠️ **AND THE OPEN FIGHT — SAME EVENT, SAME DAY, OPPOSITE READS. BOTH RECORDED, NEITHER ADOPTED:**

| | **UBS (2026-09-06)** | **Nomura (2026-09-06)** |
|---|---|---|
| **Insertion 2** (EIC on PIC wafer) | *"the trend **may be to streamline insertion 2** … given technology complexity"* | *"an issue in discussion is the **low throughput of ficonTEC's double-sided wafer test** in CPO test insertion 2, and whether TSMC and end-customers would consider **bypassing the testing**" — but argues AGAINST it |
| **Insertion 3** (singulated optical engine) | *"and to **lean more toward insertion 3**"* | *"**package-level test could be the one chosen by Mellanox (under NVIDIA) for its initial Insertion 3 ramping**, but the **die-level test appears to be an option considered by many vendors**"* |
| **Why** | Technology complexity; cycle time | *"While skipping insertion 2 might improve the overall cycle time, we note that **certain test items cannot be covered by subsequent test insertions**, and **insertion 2 also plays an important role to gate foundry yield and identify supply chain accountability**, as the rest of CPO processes take place in the venue of OSATs (i.e. **TSMC is only involved in optical engine (OE) production**)"* |
| **Conclusion** | Directional trend call | *"the **supply chain dynamics and process flows remain fluid**, and there is **no uniform standard or firm conclusion** how the entire process would shape up"* |
| **ficonTEC** | *"**FiconTEC, the leading test handler supplier for CPO, expects to ship 1k systems in next 12-18 months, outpacing 2k shipment in the last 25 years**"* | Its double-sided wafer test is the **throughput problem** |

➤ **THE TWO HOUSES ARE NOT EVEN DISAGREEING ABOUT THE SAME KIND OF THING, WHICH IS WHY NEITHER IS ADOPTED: UBS is calling a directional trend; Nomura is arguing a test-coverage and *accountability* constraint against that trend and explicitly declining to call it. **The commercially load-bearing sentence is Nomura's parenthesis — *"TSMC is only involved in optical engine production"* — because if insertion 2 goes away, the gate on [[TSM]]'s own OE yield moves downstream into the OSATs, i.e. it is a question about where TSMC's process liability ENDS, not merely a cycle-time trade.** ⚠️ **Note also that ficonTEC appears on BOTH sides of the argument: UBS uses its 1k-systems-in-12-18-months shipment plan as evidence the insertion-3 handler market is exploding; Nomura uses its double-sided wafer-test throughput as the reason insertion 2 is painful. Both can be true.** **Test-solution map from Nomura: **Chroma and ficonTEC have package-level OE test solutions; MPI has a die-level probing solution.** Nomura Buys Chroma, MPI, WinWay, KYEC.**

- **[[TSM]] PROBING — A THIRD OUTSOURCING VECTOR IN THE SAME UBS NOTE.** Wafer-level probing is *"seeing significant content upside by evaluating chip performance earlier, which reduces overall supply chain cost. **TSMC's probing demand should sustain strong growth and benefit ASE/KYEC as outsourcing partners**."* ➤ **Add it to the CoWoS raise going to ASE and the CoPoS chip-on-substrate hand-off already on this page: three separate 2027 volume vectors converting into OSAT rather than TSMC revenue.**

### 🔴 (7) THERMAL AND LIDS — A NEW SUB-THEME FOR THIS PAGE, WITH NAMED MATERIALS

**Nomura:** *"**Lidded AI chips are looking for better structural supports, CTE matching, and thermal spreading**, and **two-/multiple-piece lids are currently on the radar of chip designers**. Aside from more advanced architectures such as **vapor chamber lid (VCL) and microchannel lid (MCL)**, future lid-/ring-type designs will also involve material innovations… we notice **copper-diamond and thermadite (by Coherent) are potentially materials under investigation**."*

➤ **Why this belongs on the packaging page rather than on a cooling page: **CTE matching is the same physical problem Nomura names as an EMIB-T manufacturing challenge in (4)** — silicon-vs-organic expansion mismatch — and the lid is one of the levers for managing it. As packages get larger (14x reticle CoWoS, 310×310mm² CoPoS panels), the lid stops being a commodity part and becomes a structural and thermal component with its own material bill. ⚠️ **"Potentially materials under investigation" is exploratory language: no design win, no date, no volume. Logged as a watch item, NOT as a supply-chain call. [[COHR]] read-through only.**

- **BURN-IN TEST (BIT) — a demand vector created by TDP:** the surge in chip TDP and rising reliability requirements *"steer the necessity for BIT and could therefore **foster the demand for mass production burn-in ovens**"* (Nomura). ➤ **Net-new to this page. Consistent with the probing/test content-growth argument in (6): as packages get more expensive and more integrated, the cost of shipping a bad one rises faster than the cost of testing it, and test content grows structurally rather than cyclically.**

Not merged.** ➤ **If MS is right, it is the LESS disruptive of the two branches for this page's substrate map: a JV supply agreement adds contracted capacity without removing Unimicron or NYPCB from the [[AVGO]] socket — which is why MS keeps OW on both. Pass-through: **BMC price hikes of at least 15–20% in 4Q**, and *"we expect **foundry and OSAT to conduct price hikes of at least 10–15% and over 20%, respectively, in Jan 2027**"* on *"insufficient capacity and elevated material pricing."*
  - **J.P. Morgan · Albert Hung / Gokul Hariharan / Anthony Leng** — "ASPEED Technology Inc.: 3Q tracking ahead; 4Q guidance beats bull case expectations; OW", **2026-09-05** (**OW, PT NT$23,000 Jun-27, ~54x 12M fwd EPS; price NT$17,470 at 04-Sep**): August revenue **NT$1.63bn (+118% YoY, +7% MoM)** — **identical to MS's number, from a different house**. Jul-Aug = **68%/75% of JPMe/company 3Q guidance**; flattish September implies **3Q +23% Q/Q against guidance of +8% Q/Q**. *"ASPEED is introducing **new back-end suppliers from September**."* The guide is *"above our **street-high** estimate"* and *"supports our view that ASPEED will secure additional **BT supply** from 4Q."* JPM's industry research points to a **1 December price rise, double-digit**.
  - ⚠️ **SUBSTRATE-FAMILY ATTRIBUTION NUANCE, NOT A CONFLICT — AND IT IS THE ANALYTICALLY USEFUL PART: MS credits the 4Q upside to T-GLASS/E-GLASS (the ABF side); JPM credits ADDITIONAL BT SUPPLY.** Different substrate families, same quarter, same company. Both are recorded; neither is adopted as *the* cause. Read against the T-glass three-way split logged above, MS's *"gradual release of T-glass capacity support"* is a **third, downstream position** — a company already receiving relief — sitting between MS/Kao's "eases from 2027" and Kinsus losing 10–15% of revenue today.
  - 🔴 **THE BEAR FLAG, FROM JPM, AND IT APPLIES TO THIS WHOLE PAGE, NOT JUST ASPEED:** key downside risk is *"a potential **INVENTORY ADJUSTMENT IN 1H27 if upstream component builds outpace downstream rack shipments**."* ➤ **That is the falsifiable counter to every "component maker guides above consensus" datapoint on this page: component builds and rack shipments are not the same series, and the gap between them is inventory.**

- ⚠⚠ **SOURCE DEFECT CAUGHT — A 10x UNIT ERROR IN THE JPM NOTE. DO NOT PROPAGATE EITHER FIGURE UNCHECKED.** JPM writes the same 4Q guide as **"NT$66bn–68bn"** and its own street-high as **"NT$61bn"** — **exactly 10x** MS's NT$6.6–6.8bn. **The arithmetic settles it from the note's own data: Aspeed's August revenue is NT$1.63bn per MONTH, so a QUARTER at ~NT$6.6–6.8bn is consistent and NT$66bn is not** (it would exceed a full year of revenue several times over). ✅ **MS's decimal is ADOPTED; JPM's NT$66–68bn and NT$61bn are REJECTED as a unit slip.** Everything else in the JPM note — the growth rates, the percentages of guidance, the December price rise, the 1H27 inventory risk — is unaffected and retained. ➤ **Standing rule this instance re-proves: check `base × (1+growth)` against the monthly exhibits before quoting any absolute from a note summary.**

- 🔽 **CCL — THE FIRST SUPPLIER ON THIS PAGE WITH A NAMED SHARE OF NVIDIA GPU COPPER-CLAD LAMINATE.** (**BofA · Simon Woo** — "Doosan Corp: Initiate at Buy, W1.8mn PO: Leading AI / network CCL supplier; more than a holdco", **2026-09-06**.) ⚠️ **Doosan Corp (000150 KS) is OFF-COVERAGE — no page, one-line read-through only.** BofA: *"We highlight Doosan Corp's **dominant share of NVIDIA GPU CCL (50%+, in our view)**, as well as its **solid position in Amazon's switches** and Korean memory packaging. Doosan Corp's exposure to AI-related sales (server and network) appears **much higher than that of its Taiwanese/Chinese CCL peers**."* Economics: **CCL sales W4–5tn+ pa at ~30% OPM in 2027–28E**, on new capacity in **Korea / China / Thailand**, *"stable ASPs"* and strong shipment growth. Separately acquiring **SK Siltron** (silicon wafer maker; **W2.3tn for a 71% stake by Jan 2027**). ⚠️ **THE 50%+ SHARE IS BofA'S OWN ESTIMATE — the note says "in our view", not a disclosure — and is NOT ADOPTED.** Stated risks, in BofA's words: *"Unexpected AI capex cut by hyperscalers, competition from Taiwan/China CCL suppliers."* ➤ **Why it belongs on this page: CCL sits directly beneath the substrate layer this theme tracks, and the page has carried substrate and glass-cloth constraints for weeks without ever naming who supplies the laminate. It now has a name, a claimed share and a capacity plan — all from one house, none corroborated.**


## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-05 — **THE FIRST NAMED, TOOL-BY-TOOL CHINESE ASSAULT ON ADVANCED PACKAGING THIS THEME HAS CARRIED — HYBRID BONDING, DICING AND THINNING, ALL AT QUALIFICATION STAGE — PLUS AN [[AMD]] NUMBER ON WHY HYBRID-BONDED DRAM MATTERS AND WHY IT IS STUCK.**

- 🔴🔴 **CHINESE FRONT-END EQUIPMENT MAKERS ARE CROSSING INTO THE BACK END, AND THE ARTICLE'S STRUCTURAL POINT IS THE ONE THIS THEME HAS BEEN MAKING: *"as the expansion of HBM and three-dimensional chip stacking increasingly BLURS THE BOUNDARY between front-end and back-end processes, there is greater scope for existing front-end technologies to be adapted for packaging applications."*** (@jukan05, corpus, 2026-09-05, relaying Korean industry sources dated 2026-09-05.) The specifics:
  - **NAURA** — **two 12-inch HYBRID BONDING platforms disclosed in its H1 report: wafer-to-wafer (WTW) and die-to-wafer (DTW); the DTW system *"has already entered the customer application stage."*** Plus **TSV electroplating** (copper fill for vertical interconnect) and **panel-level packaging (PLP)** tools introduced this year, spanning RDL formation and large-panel surface treatment.
  - **AMEC** — from TSV etch into **plasma DICING, now at CUSTOMER QUALIFICATION at major Chinese customers**, and into **UBM deposition** (the metal layer under the interconnect bumps), development started H1. **Stated target: >70% of all advanced-packaging equipment categories within five years, by R&D plus M&A.**
  - **Hwatsing** — CMP into packaging: supplied advanced CMP to **HBM lines at major Chinese memory makers**, then added **wafer thinning and edge processing**, **shipping its first 12-inch thinning system for advanced memory in H1.**
  - ⚠️ **CARRIED VERBATIM, BECAUSE IT IS THE WHOLE CAVEAT: *"many of the newly announced systems from Chinese vendors are still undergoing customer qualification or remain in the early stages of commercialization. Their ability to demonstrate competitive yields, equipment uptime, stability, and productivity in actual high-volume manufacturing lines will be a key factor determining whether they can achieve broader market adoption."*** ➤ **NOT ADOPTED as share loss, and no [[BESI]] or [[DISCO]] estimate is changed. What HAS changed is the specificity: this theme previously carried Chinese packaging localisation as an ambition. It now has three named vendors, named tool categories, and a named stage for each — and DTW hybrid bonding at "customer application stage" is the single most uncomfortable line for [[BESI]], whose hybrid-bonding franchise is the reason the name is on this wiki.**
- 🔴🔴 **[[AMD]] QUANTIFIES THE PRIZE AND NAMES THE BLOCKER: hybrid-bonded 3D DRAM is *"17x MORE ENERGY EFFICIENT than an HBM stack that relies on microbumps"* — but **THERMAL HURDLES BLOCK COMMERCIALIZATION.*** (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini weekly, 2026-09-05, relaying wccftech.) ➤ **Read against [[BESI]]'s CEO on 09-01/03, who said HBM hybrid-bonding adoption is *"DICTATED BY [[NVDA]]"* — an ALLOCATION answer. AMD's is a PHYSICS answer. ⚠️ Both can hold and they gate different variables: NVIDIA decides WHEN the package spec calls for it, thermals decide WHETHER it can be built at volume. Neither is adopted as a date, and the 17x number is a vendor-conference claim, not a measured production result.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ARCHITECTURE THAT WOULD MOOT THE QUESTION: [[SAMSUNG]] at Hot Chips 2026 laid out a three-phase HBM roadmap ending in *"zHBM"* — **DRAM stacked DIRECTLY on top of the processor** — plus an HBM5 tease at 2x HBM4E performance.** (Susquehanna weekly, 2026-09-05, relaying tomshardware.) ⚠️ **Roadmap, no date, no customer. Logged here because if memory ends up on the logic die, the packaging value migrates rather than grows. Cross-ref `hbm-memory.md`, `cxl-memory-fabric.md`.**

Carry it as a HYPOTHESIS with a clear test — watch ABF substrate lead times and Unimicron/Ibiden/Shinko pricing against DRAM contract prints.**

BofA's fireside readout of the same executive: *"**memory and SUBSTRATE/BOUNDARY WAFERS** as the clear top-2 constraints"* (Vivek Arya). ➤ ⚠️⚠️ **These are not the same bottleneck. "Advanced wafers" is front-end foundry capacity; "substrate/boundary wafers" is packaging substrate. The distinction is the whole question for TSMC — a SUBSTRATE bottleneck caps the industry's shippable units WITHOUT capping TSMC's wafer starts, while an ADVANCED-WAFER bottleneck does the opposite. Same executive, same day, two houses, two answers. Logged unresolved; resolve on the next primary.**

For the merchant ABF names it is worse than a customer complaining and better than a customer exiting: the incremental capacity arrives already spoken for.**

  - ✅ **CONNECT IT TO THE UNIMICRON ITEM DIRECTLY ABOVE, BECAUSE TOGETHER THEY ARE STRONGER THAN EITHER ALONE:** the supplier says AI is *"widening supply constraints"* on ABF substrates through size, layer count and design complexity, with upstream ABF and T-glass tight (MS · Howard Kao, SEMICON Taiwan, 2026-09-02). The customer is putting its own capital into substrate capacity. ➤ **A CUSTOMER VERTICALLY INTEGRATING INTO A SHORTAGE IS A STRONGER SIGNAL THAN A SUPPLIER COMPLAINING ABOUT ONE — the supplier has an incentive to talk tightness up, the customer has every incentive not to spend. Two-sided confirmation, and it dates the relief: fiscal '27 at the earliest, and only for Broadcom's own book.**

Stifel's framing of where the value goes: **"value capture broadening beyond wafer — node, package, and now PHOTONS."**

Logged as a NEW characterisation, not as a confirmation — and it is a better falsifier than a capacity number, because it is a claim about management's own track record: check it against each TSMC call's balance language rather than against wafer counts.**
  - 🔴 **AND THE TEST STIFEL SETS IS THE RIGHT ONE FOR THIS PAGE: capacity additions of **~115-140K wpm exiting 2026** *"being **ABSORBED WITHOUT ASP PRESSURE** would confirm demand is still setting the pace for next-gen HPC compute."* ➤ ✅ **The range CONFIRMS the ladder this page already runs (Arete/Mizuho/UBS at ~140k wspm YE26, rising to 200-235k by YE27-28) and Stifel's low end is simply a wider band around the same number — no mark is superseded. What is NET-NEW is the FALSIFICATION TEST: the question stops being "how many wafers" and becomes "did the ASP hold while they landed." A capacity add absorbed at flat-to-up ASP is demand; the same add absorbed only on price is the top of the cycle. That is now the cleanest single indicator on this dossier, and it is observable in TSMC's quarterly packaging revenue against its own capacity ladder.**
  - **THE PACKAGING-ADJACENT PHOTONICS LEG, because it lands on this page's CoWoS queue too:** Stifel has **CPO volume materially starting 2H28** at *"CoWoS / SoIC-class assembly"*, with **[[TSM]] COUPE gen-1 (1.6T EIC-on-PIC via SoIC-X) qualified 2025** and *"COUPE on substrate"* CPO in production ~2026-27, and **[[NVDA]] Quantum-X / Spectrum-X photonic switches as the first named wins**. ➤ **Every CPO unit is an additional consumer of SoIC/CoWoS-class capacity, on top of accelerators and (per the HBF item below) potentially high-bandwidth flash. The queue is getting longer at both ends.** Full photonics detail on [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md).

See [[INTC]] and [[MEDIATEK]].**

All of them are TSMC being full. *"TSMC is constrained"* (@insane_analyst, 08-31).**

To support this, **20k WPM of N3 capacity is needed, which is nearly 10% of the TOTAL N3 capacity in 2027** (a lot). **Diversifying to Intel makes a lot of sense**"* (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 2026-08-31).** ➜ **HBM base dies would consume ~10% of TSMC's entire N3 output, competing directly with accelerators for the same wafers. **That is a CAPACITY-allocation problem, not a pricing or capability one — and it is the strongest structural argument on this wiki for external foundry/packaging capacity winning real volume.** ⚠️ Independent calculation; the 4EB and mix assumptions are the author's.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 29, /run-inbox — **TWO NEW MEMORY ARCHITECTURES TAKE OPPOSITE POSITIONS ON CoWoS: ONE CONSUMES IT, ONE IS DESIGNED TO ESCAPE IT**)

**Bernstein's AI-memory primer maps the emerging in-package memory standards, and for this theme the useful cut is which of them ADD to advanced-packaging demand and which are explicit attempts to route around its cost.**

- 🔴 **HBF *CONSUMES* CoWoS — and this page should treat it as additive packaging demand, not a substitution. Led by [[SNDK]] and [[SKHYNIX]], HBF (high-bandwidth flash) stacks NAND dies the way HBM stacks DRAM dies; critically, *"HBF stacks then are MIXED WITH HBM STACKS and are also packaged with XPU with CoWoS-EQUIVALENT PACKAGING TECHNOLOGY"* (exhibit sourced to Sandisk itself).** ➤ **If HBF ramps, it competes for the same [[TSM]] CoWoS-class capacity as HBM rather than relieving it — so HBF adoption TIGHTENS the constraint this theme tracks, and HBF's own ramp is capped by it regardless of NAND wafer supply.**
- 🔴 **HBC is the explicit CoWoS ESCAPE, and it is dated. [[QCOM]]'s HBC (high bandwidth compute) *"decides to COMPROMISE ON PERFORMANCE IN ORDER TO AVOID THE HIGH COST OF CoWoS. Using conventional packaging, HBC won't have as many wires connecting memory with accelerators, but AVOIDS THE COST OF INTERPOSER AND 2.5D ORGANIC SUBSTRATE."* It substitutes LPDDR dies for HBM's DDR dies to cut power. **Qualcomm targets gen-1 shipping in FY2027 and gen-2 in 2028.**** ➤ **The first dated, named product on this wiki whose stated design goal is to reach AI inference WITHOUT advanced packaging allocation. Worth watching as a leading indicator: if CoWoS scarcity persists, HBC-style conventional-package parts become the marginal supply of inference silicon — and a genuine demand leak away from the CoWoS queue.**
- **zHBM goes the OTHER way on packaging intensity: [[SAMSUNG]] aims to move HBM stacks from beside the XPU (2.5D, via interposer) to directly ON TOP of it (3D), using **wafer-to-wafer hybrid bonding** between DRAM dies.** ⚠️ **Bernstein does not underwrite it: *"we wonder how the DRAM dies in zHBM can withstand the heat dissipated from XPU in the bottom and keep its operations within the designed specifications."* Hybrid bonding is read as partly a thermal mitigation. A 3D stack on top of the hottest die in the system is the risk.**
- **NVHBM keeps 2.5D packaging but reassigns the base die: designed by [[NVDA]] and *"manufactured by TSMC"*. ➤ **Read for this theme specifically: it moves incremental silicon content from the memory suppliers into the FOUNDRY, i.e. more [[TSM]] value per accelerator package independent of CoWoS wafer count.**
- **[[INTC]]'s ZAM reorients DRAM dies 90 degrees for thermal reasons (with SAIMEMORY, a SoftBank subsidiary) but is targeted only at **FY2029** practical use — outside this theme's forecast window.**

_(Bernstein · Mark Li / Mark C. Newman / Edward Hou / Phoebe Sun, "Global Memory: An AI memory primer", 2026-08-28) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_SNDK_Global_Memory_An_AI_memory_primer.html)_

### 🆕🔭 **2026-09-01 ADDENDUM TO THIS SECTION (/newsletters-to-vault) — A THIRD POSITION ON CoWoS: NOT "CONSUME IT" AND NOT "COMPROMISE TO AVOID IT", BUT *"THE PACKAGE WALL IS THE REASON MEMORY LEAVES THE PACKAGE AT ALL"*, WITH THE STACK ARITHMETIC ATTACHED**

🔭 **FundaAI (Substack)** — *"Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray, and the Bandwidth Opportunity Could 10x Scale-Up"*, **2026-09-01** ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK PUBLISHER, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model**. **The section above splits the new memory architectures into CoWoS-consuming (HBF) and CoWoS-avoiding (HBC). This note supplies the third framing and, more usefully for this page, the STACK-COUNT arithmetic behind it.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

- 🔴 **THE PACKAGE-WALL ARITHMETIC, WHICH IS THE NET-NEW CONTENT: *"HBM’s advantage comes from a wide, short interface. HBM3E typically uses **1,024 bits**, HBM4 moves to **2,048 bits**, and [[MU]] has disclosed more than **2.8 TB/s per 12-high HBM4 stack**. **Every stack consumes footprint, interposer routing, logic-die perimeter, power delivery, thermal budget, and CoWoS capacity.**"* And the ladder: *"**Eight HBM4 stacks provide roughly 22.4 TB/s and 288 to 512 GB, and twelve stacks roughly 33.6 TB/s and 432 to 768 GB. More than 1 TB of local HBM requires 16 to 24 stacks** or higher capacity per stack, which pushes package size, substrate size, power, and cooling toward their limits."* ➤ **Read for this page: that is a CoWoS-area budget expressed in stacks. It is the cleanest available statement of WHY the >1TB-per-package configurations this theme has been tracking do not simply happen, and it is the mechanism underneath the de-spec debate rather than another mark in it.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)
- ⚠️ **THE PACKAGE-CAPACITY MARKS HE ATTACHES — AND ONE OF THEM IS OUT OF DATE AGAINST THIS PAGE, WHICH IS ITSELF THE SIGNAL: *"NVIDIA’s announced Rubin Ultra specification puts **1 TB of HBM4E** in each package for the **Kyber rack generation planned for 2027**. Industry reports in August 2026 say NVIDIA is testing configurations between **192 GB and 256 GB with FEWER HBM STACKS, possibly stepping back to HBM4**, as memory supply tightens. NVIDIA responded that its roadmap remains intact. **The reported configurations hold a fifth to a quarter of the announced 1 TB, so further growth in memory per GPU would have to come from OUTSIDE THE PACKAGE.**"* ✅ **The 192-256GB band CORROBORATES the low end of the Rubin Ultra de-spec debate held on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md) (192GB / 256GB / 288GB / 384GB marks against the originally previewed 1TB) and is consistent with the *"Rubin Ultra 384GB HBM4E reset"* this page already cross-references.** ⚠️ **BUT: this page and `themes/optical-cpo` carry **KYBER AS CANCELLED (08-18) vs PUSHED OUT (08-20)** — an unresolved conflict already logged — while FundaAI still writes *"the Kyber rack generation planned for 2027."* **FLAGGED, NOT FIXED: the publisher’s framing does not reflect the cancellation, and the FEWER-STACKS mechanism is the part to carry forward, not the Kyber date.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)
- 🔴 **THE ATTACH POINT MOVES INTO THE BASE DIE, WHICH IS AN ADVANCED-PACKAGING CLAIM RATHER THAN A MEMORY ONE: *"[[SAMSUNG]]’s custom HBM replaces the standard HBM PHY with a **die-to-die interface on an advanced logic base die**, and its **second phase houses PHYs and controllers for off-chip memory expansion IN THE BASE DIE ITSELF**, which we believe is where a future optical PHY would go."* [[MU]]’s Hot Chips tutorial *"lists a memory-optimized SerDes die-to-die PHY and co-packaged optics side by side as the two memory I/O options."* ➤ **This page already carries NVHBM as moving base-die silicon content into the foundry and zHBM as moving stacks on top of the XPU. This adds the third variant: the base die becomes the place where OFF-PACKAGE memory expansion (electrical now, optical later) is terminated — i.e. incremental logic content per stack, not less packaging.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)
- ⚠️ **AND THE COUNTERWEIGHT HE PUBLISHES HIMSELF, WHICH IS THIS PAGE’S OWN BULL CASE RESTATED BY A SCEPTIC OF IT: risk #2 in the note is that *"the **local-HBM roadmap keeps extending through HBM4E, CoWoS-L, glass substrates, and panel-level packaging**, which pushes out the moment pooled capacity becomes cheaper than local capacity."* ➤ **Every one of those four is an advanced-packaging capacity item this theme already tracks. The note is therefore NOT a CoWoS-bear argument: it says the escape route (pooled memory over optics) only opens when packaging stops extending — and it dates first in-tray optical product to *"two to three years"* after scale-up optics ships in 2027, i.e. 2029-30+.** ⚠️ **Also from the note and relevant to the 3D roadmap on this page: **imec** at ITF Taiwan (Semicon Taiwan, opening day) showed *"3D integrated optics for chip-to-chip scale-in interconnect"* — a step from pluggable → co-packaged → **an optics layer stacked directly UNDER the XPU and HBM**, with the 3D step pulling away from CPO by *"roughly two orders of magnitude in bandwidth density per unit of energy (Gbps/mm per pJ/bit) by 2035."* **A 2035 research curve, not a capacity plan — logged as direction only.** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

_(FundaAI (Substack) — *"Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray, and the Bandwidth Opportunity Could 10x Scale-Up"*, **2026-09-01** — ⚠️ **independent Substack publisher, NOT a broker: no rating, no price target, no house model, and no dollar TAM in the note by design.**) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_Optics.html)_

**Source: Jefferies "2026 Semiconductor Conference Recap" (Blayne Curtis, Janardan Menon, Kevin Garrigan et al.), 2026-08-27.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_Semiconductor_Conference_Recap.html)

- 🔴 **THE TWO-SOURCE EMIB-T CONFIRMATION, WHICH IS WHAT THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING: [[AMAT]] — *"**BETTER POSITIONED in CoPoS/EMIB-T vs CoWoS** as customers require HIGHER-END PVD"*. [[INTC]] — *"**EMIB-T backlog supports IFS revenue from 2H27 into C28 and beyond**, with a portion of capex tied to the ramp."*** ➤ **A deposition vendor saying the alternative architecture suits it better, and the architecture's owner saying it has a BACKLOG with a dated revenue window. Same architecture, same 2H27+ timing, two independent sources with different incentives. That materially strengthens the "EMIB-T is a real CoWoS alternative" leg of this page relative to a single yield claim.**
- **AND A THIRD, FROM METROLOGY: ONTO says the *"**EMIB-T inspection/metrology opportunity [is] roughly SIMILAR to CoWoS**"* — i.e. the tool intensity per unit of packaged output is comparable, which is the quiet answer to whether an architecture shift would shrink the equipment TAM. It would not.** ONTO also reiterates **advanced packaging +80%+ in C26**, with **3D bump inspection revenue ramping to tens of $m** and *"**C27 backlog mainly TIER-2 RESERVATIONS**, with Tier 1 discussions extending into C27/28."* ⚠️ **Tier-2-led 2027 backlog is a notable mix detail: the second tier is booking capacity ahead of the leader.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE WARNING, AND IT IS ABOUT THE SHAPE RATHER THAN THE SIZE OF DEMAND: SUSS (SMHN) reports a *"**SPIN COATER ORDER BOOM** with further Taiwanese orders in Q3 after the **$115m OSAT order**, **for CoWoS and other AI apps**"* — but adds that *"**LONG LEAD TIMES with the BULK OF ORDERS FOR 2027 (incl. LATE 2027) DELIVERY, so FUTURE ORDER LEVELS COULD BE LOWER**."*** ➤ **A vendor telling investors its own order rate may fall because customers have already booked 2027 is the clearest statement on this page that the CoWoS-adjacent order book is FRONT-LOADED. It does not imply weaker end demand — it implies the ORDER series will decelerate before the REVENUE series does, which is exactly the kind of divergence this page has mis-read in prior cycles.**
- **Also from the same note, on the constraint below packaging: [[LRCX]] reiterates advanced packaging **+70%+ in C26** *"supported by TSV etch, plating and PLP development"* ⚠️ (**not comparable to ONTO's 80%+ — different served bases**), and both [[LRCX]] and [[ASML]] independently name **CLEANROOM READINESS** as their primary physical bottleneck. **[[AMD]] and [[INTC]] separately name SUBSTRATES, not wafers or packaging, as the binding 2027 constraint — consistent with this page's 08-27 note that the bottleneck may have moved downstream to substrates and glass cloth.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /wiki-ingest — **[[INTC]] EMIB IS NOW REPORTED AS A *YIELD-CONFIRMED* CoWoS RIVAL WITH FOUR NAMED EXTERNAL PROGRAMMES — INCLUDING ONE THAT APPEARS IN A MEMORY MAKER'S OWN PUBLISHED ROADMAP — AND THE BINDING CONSTRAINT MAY HAVE MOVED DOWNSTREAM TO SUBSTRATES AND GLASS CLOTH**) — 🔴🔴

### 🔴🔴 EMIB CROSSES FROM "OPTION" TO "QUALIFIED SECOND SOURCE"
- ***"**Intel EMIB advanced packaging has become a CONFIRMED RIVAL TO TSMC with strong yields and satisfied customers**, media report, citing unnamed supply chain sources, who say **Intel's work with clients [[GOOG]] and [[MEDIATEK]] has been so smooth it indicates that COSTS AND PRODUCTION YIELDS ARE MEETING TARGETS.** And **with [[TSM]] CoWoS capacity STILL TIGHT, clients are likely to DEEPEN work with Intel**"* (@dnystedt, corpus, 2026-08-27, ~84k views).**
- **DIGITIMES adds the qualification detail and the staffing tell: *"**MediaTek's EMIB solution has met Google's ORIGINAL REQUIREMENTS for technology, yield, cost, and production capacity.**"* … *"While **MediaTek still considers TSMC its PRIMARY foundry partner and TRIES TO MINIMIZE DISCUSSION of alternative suppliers**, several **senior executives on its U.S.-based ASIC team are FORMER INTEL EMPLOYEES**"* (via @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-27).** ⚠️ *Both are press/supply-chain relays citing unnamed sources, and MediaTek is described as actively downplaying it. Not company-confirmed.*
- 🔴 **THE HARDEST OF THE FOUR DATAPOINTS IS NOT A RUMOUR — IT IS A COMPETITOR'S OWN DECK: at **Hot Chips 2026, [[SKHYNIX]] compared *"how CoWoS-S, CoWoS-L, and INTEL EMIB impose different mechanical and thermal stresses on HBM stacks and silicon interposers,"* and its **2.5D HBM ROADMAP FEATURES EMIB ALONGSIDE CoWoS-L, CoWoS-R AND CoWoS-S**"** (Wccftech / ServeTheHome, via Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26). ➤ **A memory maker publishing EMIB in its own 2.5D roadmap means it is engineering for it, which is a stronger signal than any supply-chain check.**
➤ **RUNNING TALLY OF NAMED EMIB EXPOSURE ON THIS WIKI: [[SKHYNIX]] next-gen HBM (logged 08-25), plus [[GOOG]] and [[MEDIATEK]] (08-27), plus EMIB's presence in Hynix's published roadmap. **Four attachments in three days. The CoWoS single-source premise that has underpinned every capacity-constrained forecast on this page now has a dated, named, yield-confirmed alternative.** ⚠️ **Counterweight to keep: JPM's standing view that *"Intel 18A is comparable to TSMC N3E"* — the packaging story is running well ahead of the leading-edge logic story, and EMIB does not require 18A.**

### 🔴 THE ARCHITECTURE IS MOVING *THROUGH* THE INTERPOSER — THREE VENDORS IN ONE WEEK
- **[[NVDA]] **NVHBM** (announced with the print, 2026-08-26): *"Traditional HBM architectures place the **memory controller on the XPU die**… **NVHBM INTEGRATES NVIDIA'S CUSTOM MEMORY CONTROLLER INTO THE HBM BASE DIE**"* — claimed **+30% bandwidth, −15% HBM power, +25% more free XPU die area vs standard HBM4E**, established as a **standard *"available from multiple memory providers"***, with **[[AMZN]] Annapurna the first adopter, from Trainium4**, on a *"common rack-scale architecture."*
- **[[SAMSUNG]] **cHBM → zHBM**: cHBM puts **advanced logic on the base die** and swaps the HBM PHY for a compact **D2D** interface (*"from HBM4 to HBM5 Samsung expects this approach to significantly reduce the area devoted to PHY and D2D interfaces"*), with a **Heat Path Block (HPB) covering 50% of the PHY area, cutting peak temperature by >35%**; then **zHBM stacks HBM DIRECTLY ON THE XPU, *"ELIMINATING the need for a conventional 2.5D INTERPOSER"*** — claimed **70% higher power efficiency, 230% greater DRAM bandwidth vs a standard HBM4e stack, 8.3% more power freed for the XPU.**
- **[[SKHYNIX]] **I-HBM** plus a hybrid-bonding timetable: I-HBM *"embeds a high-thermal-conductivity, electrically insulating cooling material within the HBM D2D PHY area… **reduc[ing] thermal resistance by MORE THAN 30%**"* for **HBM5**, alongside **Advanced MR-MUF** (already used for **16-Hi HBM3e**). And the timing: *"existing packaging approaches remain viable **UP TO HBM4**… **full-scale hybrid bonding most likely at HBM4E OR HBM5, where stack heights EXCEED 20 LAYERS**"*, gated by **costs 2-3x flip-chip**, with **fluxless TCB adopted first** and Cu-Cu-via-TCB next — *"when no viable alternatives remain, the industry will shift toward hybrid bonding."* Hybrid bonding vs TCB: **speed ~11.9x, heat dissipation >100x, integration density 15x.** Research is underway on **3D HBM with NO TSV.**
➤ 🔴 **THE JOINT IMPLICATION FOR THIS PAGE: **base-die logic content and advanced packaging steps are rising; the conventional 2.5D SILICON INTERPOSER is what all three roadmaps route around.** That is bullish TSV formation, hybrid bonding (later), MR-MUF, thermal materials and base-die foundry — and bearish long-run interposer area per package. ⚠️ **And it partially settles the 08-25 back-end debate: MS's desk wins the TIMING (HBM4 needs no hybrid bonding), BofA wins the DIRECTION (migration raises intensity), and the bear premise *"you don't scale to 16-high"* is already wrong about the present because **Hynix ships 16-Hi HBM3e today**. The live question is 20+ layers.** See [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md), [[BESI]], [[TER]], [[ADVANTEST]].

### 🔴 CoWoS DEMAND AND THE NEW BOTTLENECK — WHICH MAY NO LONGER BE PACKAGING
- **DEMAND: JPM's Gokul Hariharan has **[[NVDA]] CoWoS up 65% in C27**, with the desk's caveat *"we don't think it's necessarily going to be that easy to get extra wafer — **particularly N3 for Vera, LPX and other add-ons**"* (JPM desk · Meyers, 2026-08-26). [[ANET]] supplies the lead-time anchor: *"customers planning **18-24 months out due to 52-WEEK LEAD TIMES for TSMC wafer starts**"* (2026-08-26). And Jefferies' ~100-company conference: *"**supply rather than demand is increasingly the binding constraint. NVDA appears to have moved aggressively to secure SUBSTRATES, DRAM and other critical inputs**, making availability an **underappreciated competitive advantage** for Vera"* (2026-08-27).
- ⚠️⚠️ **BUT THE CONSTRAINT MAY HAVE MIGRATED PAST CoWoS ENTIRELY, AND THIS ONE HAS NO DE-SPEC LEVER: *"**ABF substrates & PCB/CCL are the biggest constraints for 2027.** Nvidia or [[GOOG]] can **reduce HBM per GPU and DRAM per server to increase shipments, but THEY CAN'T WORK AROUND THIS**"* (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 2026-08-27). Concretely, **Kinsus**: ABF utilisation rebounded to **85%**, heading to **~95% by year-end** on AI-server/HPC demand — but **T-glass supply is constrained such that *"approximately 10% TO 15% of potential MONTHLY REVENUE cannot currently be realized"*, *"the KEY OPERATIONAL BOTTLENECK."*** Plans **+25% high-end ABF capacity in 2027** with material allocations already secured, built at **Taiwan** facilities, *"dismissing market speculation that existing BT production lines could be converted to ABF"*; **BT utilisation ~75% → ~85%** *"supported by memory demand and growing opportunities in high-speed optical communications"*; customer engagement on **2027-2029 capacity planning starts in 4Q**, with a further round possible **as early as mid-2027 if optical accelerates** (Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26). **Taiwan's tape agreed: *"TAIEX +1.5% with CPO/ABF/CCL/Cooling leading the rally."***
➤ **The forecasting consequence is real: models keyed to CoWoS wpm are measuring the wrong ceiling for 2027 if ABF/CCL/T-glass binds first. And unlike HBM, substrate content cannot be de-spec'd away.**
- **A packaging-adjacent entrant: Jefferies/Fubon initiated **Chipbond (6147 TT, BUY)** on *"**gold bumping in high-speed optics**"* (2026-08-26); Jefferies Silk Road initiated **BUY on Luxshare and Chipbond Tech** (2026-08-27). **Chroma ATE approved a new Taiwan facility at ~NT$4bn** (2026-08-26). *All off-coverage; logged as chain-level capacity signals.*
- ✅ **AND A CONSUMABLES READ THAT SCALES WITH PROGRAMME COUNT RATHER THAN WINNER: Bernstein forecasts **EUV mask-blank growth accelerating to a 25% CAGR (2025-28E)** on volume plus ASP, because *"**every CSP and AI company is making their own GPU, CPU, and AI accelerators, and each design needs MULTIPLE SETS of expensive EUV masks and mask blanks**"*, with **advanced-node chip designs growing at a 21% CAGR** (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-08-26). ⚠️ *The note was distributed TRUNCATED at *"This is also evident from TSMC's announcement that its 2nm node …"* — the TSMC evidence and stock conclusions were NOT read here.*

That distinction matters because the page's central open question on the second-source thread is exactly whether spillover is a shortfall (temporary, closes when capacity arrives) or a concession (structural). A named platform-level space constraint is evidence for the SHORTFALL reading on this particular flow.**

On 08-18 TSMC's Head of IR said the tightness has MOVED OFF CoWoS and ONTO THE FRONT END, and answered a direct question about wafers waiting for a CoWoS slot with a flat *"do we see a mismatch, inventory, [pile-up]? NO."* BofA says TSMC is out of CoWoS-R space for CPU. RECONCILABLE, AND THE RECONCILIATION IS THE POINT: TSMC's answer is about the AGGREGATE flow and about not building ahead of the system, while CoWoS-R-for-CPU is one sub-platform inside it — and TSMC in the same conversation said it actively WELCOMES ASE and Amkor taking packaging because it *"enables more wafer business."* An outsourced sub-platform is not a mismatch on TSMC's own definition. Carry both; do not read the IR line as a denial of the BofA datapoint.**
- 🔴 **THE NEXT ASIC WAVE GETS A DATED ENTRY POINT INTO COWOS, WHICH THIS PAGE HAS LACKED: new ASIC projects are *"picking up strongly from the FRONT END manufacturing perspective and then will be through the BACK END packaging, the COWOS PACKAGING PRETTY SOON **FROM FOURTH QUARTER THIS YEAR**"* — to support **TPU volume DOUBLING YEAR-ON-YEAR IN 2027*** (BofA, 2026-08-20). ➜ **A 4Q26 CoWoS loading start for the ASIC cohort, tied explicitly to a 2027 TPU doubling. It is the packaging-side companion to the unit work filed on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md) this run, where BofA and JPM independently converge on Google TPU at ~4-4.5mn units this year going to ~8-9mn next (JPM: ~3.5mn at MODULE level, ~2x next year).** ⚠️ **BASIS NOTE BEFORE ANYONE CHAINS IT: "TPU volume doubling y/y in 2027" is a UNIT statement, not a wafer or wpm statement, and it does not convert to CoWoS capacity without a dies-per-package and a yield assumption — both of which vary by generation on this page (v9's four compute dies; the 8-12x reticle / 5-10x ASIC-outs unlock, Mizuho 08-16).**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRUCTURAL DATAPOINT OF THE RUN, AND IT COMES FROM A TOOL VENDOR RATHER THAN A BROKER: ASM International calls advanced packaging *"THE WILD WEST."*** Verbatim in substance: AP is *"a NEWER MARKET for us"* with *"NOT a very narrow roadmap and NOT a very strong standard — there are MANY STANDARDS. **Company A has their own way, company B has their own way, THE OSATs HAVE THEIR OWN WAY.**"* ASM frames that fragmentation as **its opportunity to penetrate with innovative solutions**, says it has started **inroads via CVD applications**, and calls AP *"a NEW GROWTH AREA for ASM which we didn't have before."* ➜ **🔴 WHY THIS IS GENUINELY USEFUL AND NOT JUST COLOUR: a tool vendor describing advanced packaging as STANDARD-LESS is a statement about the PROCUREMENT STRUCTURE of this theme — it means TOOL SELECTION IS PER-CUSTOMER RATHER THAN PER-NODE. In front-end logic, a node defines a recipe and incumbency compounds across every customer running it. If AP has no such convergence point, incumbency compounds less and ROADMAP AGILITY matters more, which is the mechanism by which a new entrant can win layers inside a market the established deposition names already serve.** ✅ **And note it is INDEPENDENT, VENDOR-SIDE CONFIRMATION of something this page has been documenting as a COMPETITIVE story without naming it as a structural one: the whole CoWoS-S/-L/-R vs SoIC vs EMIB-T vs CoPoS vs FOPLP taxonomy on this page — five-plus platforms with different substrates, yields, reticle limits and supplier maps, and this run adding an OSAT-specific spillover — IS the fragmentation ASM is describing. The page has treated the multiplicity as a race between architectures; ASM's read is that the multiplicity is the market's steady state.** ⚠️ **Interested-party testimony from a vendor explaining why a market it is new to is winnable. It also carries no sizing for AP — ASM gives none.**
- **[[NVDA]] VERA RUBIN VOLUMES, FILED HERE FOR THE PACKAGING AND TEST READ-THROUGH — AND THE SHAPE IS A CASCADE OF SHRINKAGE DOWN THE CHAIN, WHICH IS THE DISCIPLINE THIS PAGE NEEDS (JPM, 2026-08-20): **chip level at [[TSM]] ~2.0-2.1mn · test level (KYEC) ~1.7-1.8mn · downstream ~1.5mn for 2H26 · rack level modelled at ~10,000 RACKS × 72 = ~700k GPUs**, with the gap between the last two attributed to **production lead time**.** On the test leg: **KYEC is *"still trying to improve production yield"*, and *"right now they were talking about 1.5 million but I think there should be some upside."*** ➜ **🔴 Read the ladder as a level-of-chain rule rather than as four competing forecasts: 2.0-2.1mn at the foundry becomes ~700k at the rack inside the same period. Any Rubin number quoted on this page or on [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md) must state WHICH RUNG it sits on — the same basis discipline this run imposed on the TPU units (upstream ~4-4.5mn vs module ~3.5mn) on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md).** **Schedule, all checkable: TWO schedule changes YTD but NO FURTHER change identified; first **"UVB" sample out three weeks ago, yield not high "but that's pretty normal"**; **module MP schedule unchanged for next month**; **rack/system level targeting ~OCTOBER for the vanilla version, LATE OCTOBER TO NOVEMBER for customised**.** ⚠️ **AND THE ATTRIBUTION THAT MATTERS MOST, BECAUSE IT MOVES THE BLAME OFF THE PACKAGE: recent concerns trace to **a PCB ISSUE AT VICTORY GIANT**, NOT to Rubin itself. A substrate/PCB-tier problem being mistaken for an accelerator or packaging problem is exactly the misattribution this page exists to catch.** ⚠️ **Product and company names in this source are ASR-unreliable — "UVB" is quoted as spoken and not decoded; KYEC and Victory Giant have no wiki pages.**

**Source this block: TSMC Investor Relations · **Jeff Su** (Head of IR), Itáu BBA Global Connect group call, **2026-08-18, 21:00 BRT** — ASR transcript archived at `../../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-08-18_TSMC-IR_JeffSu_ItauBBA-group-call.md` (verbatim sidecar + full ASR decode key). ⚠️ **THIS IS A TSMC COMPANY PRIMARY. Itáu BBA is only the HOST — do NOT file it as an Itáu view.** ⚠️ **The paste is INCOMPLETE (ends mid-sentence in the final answer) and heavily garbled; every figure below is decoded and the ambiguities are flagged inline.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT OPERATIONAL DATAPOINT FOR THIS THEME IN MONTHS — the binding constraint has migrated: "if you take a slight step back and look at the last few years with the rise of AI, **more of the tightness has been concentrated on… advanced packaging or CoWoS**, and we've been adding a lot of capacity to support the customers. **NOW, TODAY, MORE OF THE TIGHTNESS IS… ON THE FRONT-END WAFER SIDE.**"** And it does not resolve, it oscillates: *"it's going to continue to be **chicken and egg**, because the more capacity we can add — not just through capex, but the other areas — then you need more packaging."* 🔴 **Why this matters for the theme's timeline: packaging relief can be bought from OSATs (ASE, Amkor) in quarters; front-end relief needs a 2-3 year shell build. - 🔴 **ASKED WHETHER WAFERS ARE SITTING IN INVENTORY WAITING FOR A COWOS SLOT (the "production running ahead of CoWoS capacity" question), the answer was a flat no: "what we're trying to do is **narrow the gap**… the demand for our packaging is very strong, just like the demand for our leading edge logic technologies… So maybe the concern, **do we see a mismatch, inventory, [pile-up]? The question was asked directly and NOT answered quantitatively — so every CoWoS wpm number on this page (JPM/Gokul 115k/175k/220k end-26/27/28; UBS's 130k+ raise; MS/Chan 200kwpm '27) remains a broker estimate uncorroborated by the company.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE STRATEGIC/FINANCIAL DISTINCTION, AND IT REFRAMES HOW TO VALUE THIS ENTIRE THEME FOR [[TSM]]: "advanced packaging is certainly important business, **BUT THE SIGNIFICANCE OF IT IS STRATEGIC. NOT FINANCIAL.** The importance of advanced packaging for TSMC, CC said, is **to enable simply MORE LOGIC WAFER MANUFACTURING BUSINESS**… No matter which way you cut it — capex-wise, revenue-wise, **most importantly PROFITABILITY-wise** — where do we make our money? Packaging? **No.** Manufacturing semiconductor logic wafers. **That's our bread and butter.** So packaging is just, again, to enable more wafer business."** ⚠️ **Hold this against the standing disclosure that back-end/CoWoS is ">10% of FY26 revenue, growing faster than corporate" — both are true, and the reconciliation is that packaging is a revenue line TSMC runs to protect a much larger and more profitable wafer line, not a profit centre it is optimising.**
- 🔴 **WHICH IS WHY OUTSOURCING PACKAGING IS ACTIVELY WELCOMED RATHER THAN DEFENDED AGAINST — including by a competitor: "we also… have partners like **ASE**, like **Amkor**, these guys to help. **Sure, no problem.** You are my partner, you can help me do the packaging — **that enables more wafer business**." And on [[INTC]] **EMIB** specifically: "CC even said, **EMIB, Intel… it looks good on paper. Because everything looks good on paper. Because that helps our customers out on the packaging side, and that's gonna mean more wafer business for us.**"** 🔴 **Read-through for the OSAT/packaging complex: TSMC has publicly declared it is NOT trying to hold this layer, which is structurally supportive of merchant packaging volumes — and structurally unsupportive of the idea that packaging capacity is a durable TSMC moat.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE ONE CLAIM TSMC EXPLICITLY DISPUTES, and it is the load-bearing one for [[INTC]]'s foundry pitch: "The part we disagree is, he also said on the call, that **packaging will lead to or be the GATEWAY to foundry business. Wei's — *"that's why **ASE, who does a lot of packaging — why can't they do foundry?**"* — and Jeff Su's own, which he calls *"even more direct"*: **"Intel says they can use EMIB and that will be the on-ramp to foundry business. Well, we have had a competitor in South Korea, [[SAMSUNG]] Electronics, who is the king, the leader, **undisputed in memory**. And for **the last 15 PLUS YEARS**, they have been trying to leverage memory to be the gateway for their foundry business. **But look at the result so far. Tells you it's not so easy.**"** ⚠️ **Interested-party testimony from the incumbent monopolist. Note precisely what it concedes and what it denies: it concedes EMIB may work AS PACKAGING; it denies the funnel into front-end. The falsifier is specific and observable — a customer that starts at Intel on EMIB and subsequently moves front-end wafers to Intel 14A/18A.**

- **PRICING ADVANCED-PACKAGING TOOLS INTO [[TSM]] HAS A HARD STRUCTURAL GATE:** the cost-of-ownership hurdle is fixed against the prior generation **when a node is introduced** — *"you can't come back in the middle. The **only opportunity with TSMC to raise price is when there is a new node**."* KLA *"really pushed back"* on the investor assumption that AP mix lifts gross margin. ⚠️ **A caution on every "AP mix = margin accretion" bridge on this page.** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-29)

## Recent signals (Aug 11, /run-inbox — PRIMARY PDFs) — 🔴 THE INCUMBENT'S OWN ANALYST SAYS TSMC IS **DELIBERATELY CONCEDING** COWOS SHARE, AND PUTS A CAPACITY LADDER ON IT: TSMC FROM ~92% TO ~46% OF THE COWOS-EQUIVALENT POOL BY 2028E — PLUS [[SAMSUNG]]'s CUBE LINE GETS ITS TSMC EQUIVALENTS
- 🔴 **THE STRATEGIC CLAIM, verbatim, and it re-frames every share number on this page: "TSMC is still the best CoWoS supplier, but AMKOR OFFERING SIMILAR SERVICES EARLIER PROVES THIS DOESN'T HAVE A LONG-TERM DEFENDABLE MOAT. TSMC THUS WON'T BUILD CAPACITY TO DEFEND SHARE. Instead, TSMC STRATEGICALLY LETS COWOS PROLIFERATE so that its front-end service won't have a downstream bottleneck"** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai, "TSMC: Higher revenue & capex on CPU", 2026-08-10). ⚠️ **This page has treated advanced-packaging share loss as a competitive outcome. Bernstein treats it as a CHOICE, and prices the consequence: "we expect this means the MARGINS OF COWOS WILL BE STRUCTURALLY LOWER, but TSMC's cautious expansion can keep its CoWoS capacity highly utilized, and the HIGHER ASSET TURNS can offset the lower margins." The distinction is not academic — a capacity shortfall closes when capacity arrives; a deliberate concession does not.**
- **THE CAPACITY LADDER (Exhibit 9, CoWoS-equivalent capacity at year-end, k wpm — chart read, labelled as such):**

| Supplier | CY2025 | CY2026E | CY2027E | CY2028E |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **TSMC** | 60 | 130 | 160 | 170 |
| **ASE & Amkor** | 5 | 30 | 60 | 100 |
| **[[INTC]] EMIB-T** (panel converted to wafer-equivalent) | 0 | 0 | 25 | 100 |
| **Total** | ~65 | ~160 | ~245 | ~370 |

➤ **TSMC goes from ~92% of the equivalent pool to ~46% by 2028E ON BERNSTEIN'S OWN NUMBERS, and the share it gives up splits roughly evenly between the OSATs and Intel. Compare UBS (07-27, on [[TSM]]): TSMC 80-85% of advanced packaging in 2026-27E falling to 65-70% by 2030 in the base case, Intel to 13%. Bernstein is materially more aggressive on the pace of dilution AND more relaxed about what it means.**
- ⚠⚠ **THREE DIRECT CONFLICTS WITH FUBON, ALL DATED INSIDE TWO WEEKS, LOGGED RATHER THAN NETTED. (1) CAPACITY LEVEL — Bernstein has TSMC CoWoS at 160k end-2027 and 170k end-2028 against Fubon's 180k and 220k (restated live on the 08-03 call) and Arete's 235k YE28. A ~50k wpm gap on the 2028 exit rate. (2) SoIC DIRECTION — Bernstein: "we expect TSMC to RAPIDLY EXPAND hybrid bonding capacity in the next 2 years," with SoIC named as the next focus so dies stack in z and packages stay inside CoWoS's round-wafer reticle. Fubon (07-28, 08-03): the 4Q28 SoIC exit rate CUT from 90k to 70k, located in AP7 phases 2 and 3. (3) THE CAUSE — Fubon attributes half that cut to "the CPU demand is also being PUSHED OUT"; Bernstein's entire thesis is that CPU demand is the upside surprise, compounding 50-60% into 2027. Note where each analyst is looking: Fubon reads the packaging fab's capex schedule, Bernstein reads the customer order book.**
- **The trigger events Bernstein names for the proliferation, in order: Amkor packaging low-end NVIDIA GPUs (already happened) → [[NVDA]]'s Vera CPU packaged by Amkor and [[AMD]]'s Venice by ASE (happening) → "[[INTC]] packaging [[GOOG]]'s TPU v9 with EMIB-T… STARTING LATE 2027 and MORE MEANINGFULLY IN 2028" → [[SAMSUNG]]'s entry, which Bernstein calls "INEVITABLE given the resources that Samsung commands."** ⚠️ **Note the CPU mechanism underneath it, which is easy to miss: most CPUs are not advanced-packaged at all, and the ones that are go to OSATs rather than TSMC — so the CPU wave that is driving TSMC's capex hike is NOT a CoWoS demand event. Bernstein is explicit: "TSMC's recent capex hike is primarily for WAFER capacity, and much less so for CoWoS capacity. CoWoS capacity is still being expanded, but AT A PACE PLANNED ORIGINALLY."** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_259272.html)
- 🔴 **[[SAMSUNG]]'s PACKAGING LINE GETS ITS TSMC EQUIVALENTS, FIRST TIME ON THIS PAGE (Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon et al., "Global Memory, NVIDIA & Broadcom: Quick thoughts on strategic partnerships", 2026-07-27; primary PDF ingested 08-11): the [[AVGO]] MOU covers "2.3D (CUBE-E & -R, SIMILAR TO TSMC CoWoS-L AND -R) & 2.5D (CUBE-S, SIMILAR TO TSMC CoWoS-S) advanced packaging technology for 'AI & NETWORKING' silicon" — i.e. Samsung is fielding a FULL CoWoS-equivalent portfolio, not a single point alternative.** **And the foundry leg is noded: "the foundry service will focus on 2NM & BELOW for products such as Wireless Broadband Communications solutions (but how about AI ASIC?)" — the parenthetical is Bernstein's own.** ➤ **Read against JPM's 07-27 decomposition (90-95% HBM / 5-10% foundry wafers, foundry leg for networking/broadband/telco): the PRODUCT call is confirmed, the implied MATURE-NODE reading is not — Samsung is landing 2nm volume, just not for accelerators on this evidence.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260726_Bernstein_NVDA_Global_Memory-_NVIDIA_-_Broadcom-_Quick_thoughts_on_stra.html)

## Recent signals (Aug 9) — CoWoS enters the MEMORY model as an explicit yield haircut on HBM supply: 92% → 94% → 96%, and it is the binding gate between HBM bits made and HBM bits shippable

JPM's memory S&D model does something most memory models do not: it carries **two HBM supply lines — raw bit supply and "bit supply after CoWoS"** — and the wedge between them is an assumed packaging yield. That makes advanced packaging a *quantified* input to the memory shortage rather than a qualitative caveat. (J.P. Morgan · **Jay Kwon** / Sangsik Lee / Neelay Kamath / Harlan Sur / Mio Shikanai, *"Global Memory TAM and S&D update"*, **2026-08-09**)

- **The haircut, and its improvement path.** HBM **bit supply 4,152 / 6,613 / 9,350mn (1GB-eq)** for 26/27/28E becomes **3,802 / 6,205 / 8,960 after CoWoS** — implying a **CoWoS yield of 92% / 94% / 96%**, revised **+1 / +1 / +3pp** versus the May-26 model. **~350mn / ~410mn / ~390mn 1GB-eq of HBM bits per year are made but not shippable** on this model. At JPM's own 2028E HBM ASP of $3.4/Gb that ~390mn 1GB-eq wedge is on the order of **$10bn+ of HBM revenue gated by packaging yield alone**.
- **Why the +3pp in 2028E matters more than it looks.** It is the second-largest single contributor to JPM's *narrowing* of the HBM shortage (glut −22% in 28E vs −37% prior). **Packaging yield improvement is doing real work in easing the modelled HBM shortage** — which is the direct inverse of the framing this page carries elsewhere, where packaging is the constraint. Both are true at different margins; the point is that **whoever moves CoWoS yield moves the memory S&D balance**, and JPM is the first house on this page to size that sensitivity.
- **⚠️ Basis flag — do not compare this "yield" to a fab yield.** JPM does not define whether the 92-96% is die-level assembly yield, capacity-allocation-adjusted throughput, or a blended fudge for the CoWoS bottleneck. It is applied as a **supply multiplier on HBM bits**, so it should be read as *"share of HBM bits that clear packaging into a shippable accelerator"* — closer to an allocation-and-yield composite than to a clean OSAT yield. Held as JPM's construct.
- **Cross-check against the note's demand side:** JPM adjusted its **merchant GPU/ASIC unit shipment assumptions off its own CoWoS model update** before setting HBM content assumptions — so the HBM bit-demand cut (−4% / −10% / −19%) and the CoWoS supply haircut are internally linked, not independent. Anyone lifting one number without the other double-counts.
- **The HBM5 packaging fork is now explicit across all three memory makers** — relevant here because it decides the bonding-equipment mix into 2028-29: **[[SAMSUNG]]** developing 16-Hi HBM5 with **both TCB and HCB** (plus HPB thermal architecture and a **2nm GAA base die, +50% speed vs HBM4E**); **[[SKHYNIX]]** flagged **HCB** as its potential HBM5 packaging solution on the 2Q26 call, with **iHBM** as the thermal answer; **[[MU]]** shows *"limited signals … considering HCB"*, with local sources pointing to **trench cooling** (etched micro-grooves with circulating fluid) instead. **Three suppliers, three thermal/bonding answers, all late-28E.** Detail on [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md).

## Recent signals (Aug 2) — ★ the first DOWNWARD capacity revision of the cycle, and it is SoIC not CoWoS; plus EMIB-T's substrate economics turn out to be [[INTC]]-subsidised

**★ ① SoIC CUT −22% FOR 2028 WHILE CoWoS IS HELD WITH UPSIDE — the split is the signal (Fubon, Jefferies' Taiwan affiliate, via William Beavington's Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-08-02).** **"Our affiliate Fubon CUT ESTIMATES ON TSMC's SoIC EXPANSION, with its SoIC capacity now expected to rise to 70K WPM BY Q4-28 VS 90K PREVIOUSLY. SoIC capacity is estimated at 35K WPM [today]. TSMC's CoWoS capacity CONTINUES TO BE FORECAST to rise from 120K WPM IN Q4-26 TO 220K WPM BY Q4-28, WITH POTENTIAL UPSIDE TO THIS NUMBER."**
- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE FIRST TIME AN ADVANCED-PACKAGING CAPACITY LINE HAS BEEN REVISED DOWN ON THIS THEME, and the fact that it is 3D STACKING (SoIC) rather than 2.5D INTERPOSER (CoWoS) is the whole content. It says the marginal 2027-28 AI die is still CoWoS-attached — HBM beside logic on an interposer — rather than logic-on-logic stacked. Every roadmap on this page that assumed SoIC scaling in lockstep with CoWoS needs the ratio re-cut: CoWoS is now forecast to grow ~1.8x from Q4-26 to Q4-28 while SoIC grows ~2x off a base 3.4x smaller and with a lowered ceiling.**
- **THE TOOL-LEVEL READ-THROUGHS ARE THE ACTIONABLE PART: "the impact of potentially lower SoIC capacity is on [[BESI]], WHICH SUPPLIES HYBRID BONDERS FOR THIS PROCESS" — a direct −22% out-year cut to the process BESI's tool is qualified into. Offsetting: "any upside to CoWoS/CoPoS capacity expansion will be POSITIVE FOR SUSS, which supplies UV SCANNERS to TSMC for these processes." Fubon names TSMC's SoIC customers as [[AMD]] and [[AAPL]], "with CPO for optical connectivity a key longer-term driver," and the desk adds its own hedge: "HOWEVER, WE REMAIN MORE CONSERVATIVE IN OUR EXPECTATION FOR RATE OF CPO ADOPTION IN THE INDUSTRY."** ⚠️ **Note the loop: SoIC capacity is the physical enabler of co-packaged optics, and the house cutting SoIC is also the house least convinced by CPO timing. The two views are consistent — and together they push the CPO-driven packaging step-up out beyond the 2028 forecast window. See [themes/optical-cpo](optical-cpo.md), [[LITE]], [[COHR]].**
- **Same note, the product-side datapoint that explains part of the SoIC trim: "Fubon also sees [[NVDA]]'s RUBIN VOLUMES BEING REVISED DOWNWARD IN 2026 TO 1MN UNITS, due to its THERMAL DESIGN CHANGE AND PRODUCTION PUSH-OUT, but HIGHER BLACKWELL SHIPMENTS ARE EXPECTED TO COMPENSATE."** ⚠️ **A mix shift back down the product stack holds wafer and CoWoS demand roughly constant while deferring the advanced-packaging content step-up — which is exactly what a SoIC cut with CoWoS held looks like from the other end. Two independent sources now describe Nvidia's rack-scale roadmap slipping for PHYSICAL-INTEGRATION reasons (thermal, PCB midplane) rather than silicon reasons; see the SemiAnalysis Kyber/NVL576 delays already on [[NVDA]].**

**★ ② EMIB-T: THE SUBSTRATE ECONOMICS ARE DISCLOSED, AND THEY ONLY WORK BECAUSE INTEL IS PAYING (@IntelProMUltra relaying UNIMICRON's Q2'26 earnings call, 2026-08-01).** **Four disclosures: "(1) The company expects EMIB-T SUBSTRATE MASS PRODUCTION TO BEGIN IN 2027. (2) The EMIB-T substrates will initially be supported by UNIMICRON AND 2 JAPANESE VENDORS (IBIDEN AND SHINKO ELECTRIC)… long-standing advanced-substrate partners. (3) The INITIAL YIELD TARGET IS AROUND 50%. (4) More importantly, Unimicron indicated that INTEL WILL PROVIDE COMMERCIAL PROTECTION DURING THE EARLY RAMP, ALLOWING IT TO MAINTAIN REASONABLE PROFITABILITY IN THE LOW YIELDS."** **The relay's own read: "this does not look like a conventional supplier-customer relationship. Intel appears to be COORDINATING the technology, supplier qualification [and] manufactur[ing]" — i.e. platform-owner behaviour, the same posture Intel took building the ABF substrate industry.**
- ⚠️ **THE YIELD NUMBER NOW HAS TWO INDEPENDENT BRACKETS AND THEY DISAGREE ON LEVEL: J.P. Morgan's supply-chain checks say "~60% CURRENTLY AT SUBSTRATE LEVEL AND ~90% AT CHIP ON SUBSTRATE LEVEL" (JPM · Hariharan on [[MEDIATEK]], 2026-08-02); Unimicron's own call says the initial SUBSTRATE yield target is ~50%. So EMIB-T substrate yield is 50-60% today — and only ONE of the two sources mentions that the vendor economics at that yield are underwritten by Intel. JPM treats the yield question as the CLEARED obstacle; Unimicron's disclosure suggests it is cleared because someone else is absorbing the cost. Anyone quoting "EMIB-T yield is fine" should carry the subsidy alongside it, and note it lands in Intel Foundry's 2027 gross margin — the same year the turnaround case needs GM expansion.**
- ⚠️ **TIMING REMAINS UNRESOLVED AND UNIMICRON IS THE SOURCE OF BOTH DATES: substrate mass production "in 2027" (this call) vs its own 07-31 statement that EMIB-T is "unlikely to reach full mass production before 2027," vs MediaTek management dating EMIB-T MASS PRODUCTION TO 2028 with "BACK-UP PLANS (e.g. TSMC CoWoS/CoPoS)" and the caveat that "SWITCHING THE TECHNOLOGY MIGHT TAKE TIME" (MS · Charlie Chan, 2026-08-01). The reconciliation that fits all three: SUBSTRATE ramps 2027, CUSTOMER HVM 2028. Carry it that way until one source states both.**
- **The demand-side commitment is nonetheless the firmest external validation of an Intel packaging technology this theme has logged: three qualified substrate vendors, a 2027 substrate ramp, MediaTek's first-party "we are MORE COMMITTED to EMIB-T," and Unimicron expecting customers "to stay on EMIB-T FOR THREE PRODUCT GENERATIONS" on lower cost and better performance vs CoWoS.** ⚠️ **Read against ① though: TSMC's CoWoS capacity forecast was NOT cut. If EMIB-T were displacing CoWoS at volume in the forecast window, the CoWoS line should have moved. It did not — which bounds how much of the 2027-28 accelerator volume the market currently expects EMIB-T to take.** See [[INTC]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[TSM]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md).

So we look at things like… **the wafer bonding**, then you've got more opportunity"* — with the mix effect being *"higher value systems in the portfolio."* Both **"traditional or co-op type packaging"** and hybrid bonding are cited as CY27 growth drivers (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 call, 2026-07-28).

See [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md).

MediaTek's claim is yield on track for v9 MP in 2028. What is NO LONGER DISPUTED is the demand rationale: "the main reasons customers are considering switching from CoWoS to EMIB-T are LOWER COSTS and IMPROVED PERFORMANCE," and Unimicron "expects these customers to remain with EMIB-T for THREE PRODUCT GENERATIONS, pointing to sustained market demand for long-term alternatives to CoWoS."
