## What it is / why it matters
CoWoS (chip-on-wafer-on-substrate) is the 2.5D advanced-packaging technology that stitches GPU/ASIC logic dies and HBM stacks onto a silicon interposer — it is the physical gate on every leading-edge AI accelerator, and through 2027 it is as binding a constraint as 3nm wafers themselves. The buy-side reason to care: CoWoS (plus its 3D sibling SoIC and the substrate/OSAT layers around it) sits *upstream* of the entire AI compute P&L. TSMC owns the bulk of the capacity, so its expansion cadence rate-limits NVDA/AVGO/AMD shipments; but the more interesting move is that value is now visibly migrating *out* of TSMC toward OSATs (ASE, Amkor), substrate vendors, and — as a wildcard — Intel's EMIB panel-level packaging. A former TSMC sales exec put the punchline bluntly: CoWoS was "a technology of the future" that TSMC nearly killed for a decade because it wasn't profitable, and is now "something everybody's depending on" (TSMC former exec, 2026-04-14).

## State of play (latest)
- **NEW (2026-09-18) — 🔴🔴🔴 THE SUBSTRATE CALL GETS ITS FOURTH INDEPENDENT DESK IN TWELVE DAYS, AND THIS ONE IS THE STRONGEST FORM OF THE EVIDENCE: THE CUSTOMERS ARE PAYING CASH UP FRONT TO LOCK FC-BGA THROUGH 2030.** BofA's Korea industry overview states that the long-term-agreement structure *"is not just limited to the memory chip business and is increasingly being [used] for FC-BGA"*, and that **US Big Tech companies or hyperscalers have, in BofA's view, already agreed to prepay a few billion dollars to SEMCO / LG Innotek to secure FC-BGA sourcing for 2028-30**, with those prepayments funding capacity expansion **particularly in Vietnam**; it reads **Doosan Corp's more aggressive CCL capacity expansion (mostly for GPU and switch boards)** as consistent with the same FC-BGA demand (BofA Securities / Merrill Lynch Seoul · Simon Woo, CFA, w/ Matt Shin — "Korea super-cycle continues into 2030, driven by AI and globalization", 2026-09-18, 66pp).
  - ➤➤ **Why this ranks above the three marks already in the 09-17 block's table: those are SUPPLY-side observations (a tool lead time, a materials deficit, a broker's gap estimate). This is a BUY-side commitment — the same prepaid-LTA structure that re-rated memory is now reported inside the substrate layer, and it is being signed for 2028-30, i.e. two years beyond the CoWoS capacity ladder this page argues about.** **If accurate, substrate scarcity is no longer a 2027 timing problem; it has been priced and pre-funded to the end of the decade.**
  - ⚠️ **SOURCE CLASS: this is BofA's own analytical read (*"in our view"*, *"according to our analysis"*), not a disclosed contract. The dollar figure is "a few billion" — a range, not a number. SEMCO, LG Innotek and Doosan Corp are off-coverage with no wiki pages; deliberately plain text.**
- **NEW (2026-09-18) — 🔴🔴 THE BONDER/TCB DEMAND LEG IS NOW EXPLICITLY HOSTAGE TO THE STACK-HEIGHT FIGHT, AND THE TWO SIDES WERE PUBLISHED THE SAME WEEK.** BofA (same note): **next-generation HBM requires more DRAM dies stacked — *"for 16-hi, 20-hi, etc."* — with a logic die (controller), so advanced packaging *"TCB (thermal compression bonding) or eventually HB (hybrid bonding) will become increasingly more critical for new AI data center builds"***. Against it, SemiAnalysis the same week: *"HBM bandwidth matters way more than HBM capacity. For inference workloads where memory bandwidth matters the most, **4-hi HBM provides the best $/bandwidth and therefore lowest cost per token**"* (SemiAnalysis · Bryan Shan, Cam Quilici, Alec Ibarra, Kimbo Chen, Myron Xie, Dylan Patel — "Engrams Embedding Entendre: Codesign for Efficient DRAM/SSD Offloading", 2026-09-18, extending their 09-13 "Why 4-hi HBM Wins").
  - ➤ **Bonds per stack scale with dies per stack. A 4-hi world and a 20-hi world differ by ~5x in bonding inserts for the same HBM bit — which means every TCB and hybrid-bonding TAM on this page inherits an unresolved 5x range. Neither side is adopted; both are dated. The fork is tracked on [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md), where a third position (an Intel Technology Research systems researcher, 09-17: *"I do not think reducing stack height would reduce the capacity requirement itself"*) sits between them.**
- **NEW (2026-09-18) — 🔴🔴 THE HBM TRADE RATIO IS BOUNDED AT LAST (3:1 → 4:1) AND IT IS A *PACKAGING* NUMBER AS MUCH AS A WAFER NUMBER.** Deutsche Bank models an incremental HBM bit as requiring **~3x the silicon of an incremental standard DRAM bit today, *"climbing higher towards 4:1 with HBM4 / HBM4e"***, and attributes the ratio to **multi-die stacking, advanced bonding techniques and through-silicon-vias — *"all of which require more cleanroom capacity"*** (Deutsche Bank · Melissa Weathers / Joe Schwab / DJ Sebastian — "Global Memory: Chipping away at industry supply imbalance", 2026-09-16, 14pp). **[[SAMSUNG]] management confirmed the same range and its direction from the vendor side three days later: capacity constraints *"are further aggravated as HBM trade ratio continues to rise by each generation (vs. the current 3-4:1), driven by the increase in chip size"*** (Goldman Sachs · Giuni Lee / Taeyong Lee / Daiki Takayama — Samsung management virtual meeting, 2026-09-18).
  - ➤ **Read for this page: the packaging content per HBM bit is rising on a bounded, now twice-confirmed path, and DB routes the consequence into FLOOR AREA rather than into tool count. That is the same constraint [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md) carries from [[AMAT]] (08-20), [[LRCX]] (09-09) and JPM (09-17) — and it says the cleanroom problem gets worse as the memory mix shifts, independent of capex.**
- **NEW (2026-09-18) — 🔴 THE HBM *BASE DIE* IS NOW A LEADING-EDGE FOUNDRY CONSUMER, ON THE RECORD FROM THE #1 DRAM VENDOR.** Samsung management: **4nm foundry capacity *"remains very tight due to demand from HBM4 base die"***, the company is **considering adding 4nm capacity in Korea**, and **new line additions at 2nm for HBM5 base dies are being reviewed** (GS / Samsung management, 2026-09-18).
  - ➤ **This page has treated the base die mainly as a packaging-integration question (who stacks it, on what platform). The vendor is treating it as a NODE question, and it is walking it from 4nm to 2nm across one generation. That pulls HBM into the same leading-edge capacity queue as the accelerators it serves — and it is a competitive datapoint for whoever is not making its own base dies.** ⚠️ *"Considering" and "being reviewed" — intent, not committed capacity.*
- **NEW (2026-09-18) — 🔭 A CONTINGENT NEW PACKAGING DEMAND POOL: HBF WOULD NEED AN HBM-CLASS PACKAGING ECOSYSTEM, AND THE EXPERT SAYS TODAY'S PATH IS TOO EXPENSIVE.** Asked directly whether the industry needs an HBF equivalent of the established HBM packaging ecosystem, the expert on BofA's HBF call answered yes: *"it does need a packaging technology that is needed. And right now it is based on somewhat expensive interposer type of technology… that is not cheap enough"*, and the improvement he wants is **a move to organic packaging — or, failing that, the approach Intel has been pursuing, which he named in explicit contrast to CoWoS** (BofA hardware expert call, 29th of the 2026 series — host **Wamsi Mohan** (BofA), expert **Sudar Sudarshan**, ex-President Nintendo Technology Development, ex-NVIDIA — "HBF vs HBM in AI Memory Hierarchy", 2026-09-18).
  - ➤ **Sized correctly, this is optionality rather than a forecast: he puts first HBF devices in the latter part of 2027, says there is no measured silicon yet (*"it is largely hypothetical… they don't have any evidence for any of these things at the moment"*), and expects the first volume to land at the EDGE rather than in AI datacenters. But the direction is worth holding — if HBF ships at all, it arrives as a second stacked-memory product needing interposer-class integration, i.e. incremental demand on the same constrained platforms, with an explicit commercial push toward organic substrates to escape them.**
  - ⚠️⚠️ **ASR TRANSCRIPT THAT GARBLES PROPER NOUNS (the host is rendered "Wazi Mohan"). The specific Intel packaging platform he names is not legible in the transcript and is therefore DESCRIBED, not quoted — do not resolve it to a product name without a second source.**
- **NEW (2026-07-27) — THE SUBSTRATE LAYER BELOW CoWoS IS ITS OWN 2027-2030 BOTTLENECK: ABF UNDERSUPPLY RETURNS FROM 2027, ~25% GAP BY 2030, AND THE T-GLASS INPUT IS ALREADY ~20% SHORT (Morgan Stanley "Playing the AI Infrastructure Dip", Howard Kao, Greater China Tech Hardware, 2026-07-27).** Adjacent to, not the same as, the CoWoS capacity ladder this page tracks — but it gates the same packages.
  - **The mix flip is the whole argument: PC-related demand, "once ~70% of ABF value in 2015," falls to <15% by 2030, while server / AI GPU / AI ASIC / networking rises from ~10% to >75% over the same period — because AI chips consume roughly 10x more ABF than PC-related packages** ("larger physical form factors and higher manufacturing complexity").
  - **The gap: "structural undersupply of ABF substrates to return from 2027, persisting through 2030 (~25% gap by 2030, more severe at the high end), driven by larger chip sizes, higher layer counts, and new packaging complexity (embedded passives, EMIB-T) that lowers manufacturing yields and effective output."** Note EMIB-T appears here as a *yield drag on substrate output*, which is a different framing from the Intel-EMIB-as-capacity-relief argument elsewhere on this page — worth holding both.
  - **The upstream input is tighter still: "a ~20% supply/demand gap in low-CTE glass cloth (T-glass)"** — needed for the thicker, warpage-resistant CCL cores AI packages require — which, "coupled with increased precious metal pricing (primarily gold and copper), is already leading to inflationary material pricing for substrate makers. **Hence, their cost pass-through will support superior pricing power.**"
  - **Why MS thinks this cycle is underwritable where 2020-22 was not: "unlike the 2020-22 WFH-driven cycle, this up-cycle has much clearer demand visibility because suppliers can engage directly with AI end customers ([[NVDA]], [[AMD]], [[GOOG]], [[AMZN]], [[META]], [[AVGO]]), giving them confidence to invest in capacity — and end-customers are reportedly OFFERING SUBSIDIES TO SECURE SUPPLY, a sign of prolonged supply tightness."** Customer-funded capacity is the strongest tell on this page for how binding the substrate constraint is.
  - **Names (off-coverage, no wiki pages — plain text deliberately):** MS likes **Unimicron (3037.TW), Nan Ya PCB (8046.TW) and ZDT (4958.TW)**, with ZDT the specific China play "because all of its ABF revenue today comes from supplying to China." See [outros-asia](outros-asia.md). (Morgan Stanley, 2026-07-27) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Morgan_Stanley_700HK_Global_Thematics_Playing_the_AI_Infrastructure_Dip_Whe.html)

  - **THE FULL 2027 ALLOCATION TABLE (Figure 5; % of TSMC CoWoS and wafers).** **[[NVDA]] 50.0% / 945,000 · [[AVGO]] 22.3% / 421,800 · [[AMZN]] (AWS) 10.4% / 196,500 · [[MEDIATEK]] 7.3% / 137,700 · [[AMD]] 7.0% / 132,300 · [[MSFT]] 1.0% / 18,900 · XLNX 0.5% / 9,450 · [[CSCO]] 0.5% / 9,450 · Others 1.0% / 18,900.** For the 2026 comparison on the two names that move: **MTK 1.5% / 17,400 → 7.3% / 137,700 (a ~7.9x wafer increase)** and **AVGO 22.5% / 263,100 → 22.3% / 421,800 (share roughly flat, wafers +60%)**. **The share-vs-wafers distinction matters and the relays blurred it: AVGO is NOT losing wafers, it is losing 20bps of a pie that is itself +62% — MediaTek's gain comes out of "Others" (4.3% → 1.0%) and AWS/AMD share drift far more than out of Broadcom.**
  - **⚠️ TWO DIFFERENT NVDA ALLOCATION BASES ON THIS PAGE — DO NOT STACK THEM. The 945,000-wafer line above is TSMC-ONLY. Separately Fubon writes: "We estimate nVidia's total capacity allocation to reach 1.03mn in 2027 vs. 629k in 2026" — that figure is TSMC PLUS SPIL, with SPIL carrying "additional capacity support… for Vera CPU production."** The ~85k gap between 1.03mn and 945k is the SPIL/Vera leg. Same logic on the other side of the table: **"AMD's total allocation [to] significantly increase to 300k in 2027, with the majority of the increase coming from ASE"** — so AMD's 300k is TSMC (132,300) + ASE, not a TSMC number, on "agentic AI and strong server CPU demand."
  - **INTEL'S EMIB CAPACITY LADDER, WHICH IS NET-NEW TO THIS PAGE AS A DATED MONTHLY NUMBER: "Intel's EMIB capacity will reach 10-12k by the end of 2026, and the plan is to double its capacity to 24-25k per month by the end of 2027"** (Figure 8). Fubon reads Intel as **"becoming more positive in capacity expansion, which is positive to the Google supply chain,"** and notes Intel has already announced its capex increase — **"although there is no detailed breakdown… the CFO highlighted that a great portion of the new capex is earmarked for expanding advanced packaging capabilities such as EMIB-T."** **The honest debit, in Fubon's own words and unchanged from its prior stance: "there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement."** The named watch item: **"Intel's major supplier Ibiden's upcoming earnings call and potential capex increase in the next two years will be another highlight"** — a dated, checkable catalyst for the substrate leg this page already tracks ([outros-asia.md](outros-asia.md)).
  - **HOW THIS SITS AGAINST THE UBS STRESS TEST ABOVE.** UBS models **Intel at ZERO external EMIB-T revenue until 2028** and 13% industry share only by 2030 in a scenario it labels a *stress test*, not a base case (the label correction below stands and is not disturbed). **Fubon's EMIB ladder is a capacity statement, not a revenue statement, and the two are compatible** — 24-25k/month of EMIB capacity by end-2027 with the external revenue arriving in 2028 is exactly UBS's shape. Where they differ is confidence: **UBS calls EMIB-T execution risk "manageable"; Fubon still calls the learning curve "a lot of noise."** Both stay. [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-28) — Jefferies' updated CoWoS/SoIC model, and the allocation table is where the news is.** Capacity: **[[TSM]] end-2027E/28E CoWoS unchanged at 180k/220k wpm — with potential UPSIDE to 2028 “as our latest checks show that TSM is slowing down its SoIC expansion to prioritize CoWoS.”** SoIC end-2026E/27E held at **45k/65k**; the 2028 figure was revised (⚠️ **the two Jefferies relays state the 2028 SoIC revision in OPPOSITE directions** — Wong: “cut to 90k (from 70k)”; Beavington: “revises down to 70k (from 90k)” — the *direction of travel* (SoIC deprioritised for CoWoS) is consistent across both, the endpoint is not; **flagged, not reconciled**). ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-07-28 by the primary Fubon text, now on disk — Beavington's relay was the correct one. Fubon verbatim: "Compared to our earlier forecast of 90k capacity in 4Q28, we revise down our capacity expansion forecast to 70k in 4Q28." So the 4Q28 SoIC mark is 70k and the move is a CUT from 90k. Wong's relay (which read it as a raise to 90k) is left above as originally logged, but is superseded** (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28). **ASE end-2027E FoCoS raised to 50k (from 45k)** to support CPU and networking demand (Jefferies · Rankie Wong / Sherman Shang, 2026-07-28).
- **NEW (2026-07-28) — the 2027 customer allocation shifts, which are the most decision-relevant lines: [[NVDA]] UNCHANGED at 50%** (with additional support from **SPIL for the Vera CPU**); **[[MEDIATEK]] RAISED to 7.3% (from 5.3%) ≈ 138k wafers** — “the biggest change” — on higher [[GOOG]] TPU shipment targets; **[[AMD]] total allocation (TSM+ASE) up significantly to ~300k wafers**, majority of the increase from **ASE**; **[[AVGO]] TRIMMED to 22.3% (from 24.3%)** but still TSM's #2. **Read as a set: MediaTek and AMD are the only two customers taking incremental 2027 allocation, and they take it partly at AVGO's expense** — the cleanest available read on custom-ASIC share shifting inside a fixed packaging envelope.
- **NEW (2026-07-28) — Rubin cut again, and the interposer math partly offsets it. “In the near-term, we see Rubin forecasts this year further revised down to ~1mn (from 1.5mn)”** on the earlier thermal design change and production push-out — corroborated by **KYEC (2449 TT) cutting its FY revenue guide on a 1-2 month Rubin push-out**. Also flagged: **a new “Taycan” rack design NVDA is exploring as an alternative to Kyber.** **But post-Kyber-cancellation: Rubin Ultra's interposer is only slightly larger than Rubin — ~~12 interposers per CoWoS wafer vs Rubin's 10~~ — so total NVDA chip output RISES to 12.4mn in 2027E vs 8.2mn in 2026E.** ⚠️ **CORRECTION 2026-07-28 — THE INTERPOSER COUNTS WERE INVERTED IN THIS RELAY. The primary Fubon text reads: "We currently estimate 12 and 10 interposers per CoWoS wafer for Rubin and Rubin Ultra, respectively." So it is 12 per wafer for RUBIN and 10 for RUBIN ULTRA — which is the only reading consistent with the sentence it sits in, since a LARGER Rubin Ultra interposer must yield FEWER dies per wafer. The struck text is preserved above as originally logged; the 12.4mn/8.2mn chip-output figures are unaffected** (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28). Sherman's HBM4-adjusted BOM implies a **Rubin GPU price of US$78-80K** at a 75-80% NVDA gross margin (Jefferies, 2026-07-28).
- **NEW (2026-07-28) — the read-across the desk itself drew: “Revising downward Rubin is negative for the entire A.I. sector, the entire semiconductor sector. Direct EU readacross to [[ASML]], ASM, VAT, SUSS, [[IFX]]”** — because **Rubin carries higher dollar content than Blackwell**, a push-out costs more per unit than a Blackwell slip would. **The SoIC deprioritisation hits BESI hardest** (it supplies the hybrid bonders for TSMC SoIC, its largest customer). Offsetting, Jefferies is **more confident in the CPU chain (ASE top pick on Vera/Venice), the SPE chain, and TPU momentum ([[MEDIATEK]] top pick)** than in the GPU chain (Jefferies · William Beavington, Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-07-28).
- **NEW (2026-07-27) — 🔴 THE FULL UBS NOTE ARRIVES AND IS ARCHIVED: the year-by-year stress-test build, the panel-carrier/glass clarification, the equipment and substrate maps, and one label correction to the bullet below (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri / Shingo Hirata / Jerry Su / Diana Chang "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership? — Intel's EMIB-T vs TSMC's CoPoS", 2026-07-27).** The 07-26 bullet below was written off the email preview of this note; the full 27 July report is now on disk and this bullet carries only what the preview did not. **⚠️ LABEL CORRECTION FIRST: UBS does not call the 13%-Intel-share case a "base case." Figure 5/16 is headed "Stress test scenario" and Figure 6/17 "Lower case scenario"** — so the framing is *TSMC still compounds even under stress*, not *this is what UBS expects*. UBS's own house verdict is the sentence in Key insights: **"rather than representing a thesis-changing threat to TSMC, we believe the emergence of Intel's EMIB-T should be viewed as validation of the structural growth opportunity in advanced packaging,"** with "the most likely outcome… not a winner-takes-all market" but Intel as "a meaningful secondary source while TSMC retains the dominant share of the highest-performance AI packaging opportunity through the end of the decade."
  - **THE STRESS-TEST TABLE, YEAR BY YEAR (US$mn, 2026E→2030E).** Industry advanced-packaging revenue **19,025 / 36,300 / 58,080 / 87,120 / 121,968** (YoY **+87% / +91% / +60% / +50% / +40%**). TSMC advanced-packaging sales **16,034 / 29,730 / 41,676 / 59,207 / 81,378** (+80/85/40/42/37%), = **9% / 12% / 13% / 16% / 18% of total TSMC sales.** Industry share: **TSMC 84 / 82 / 72 / 68 / 67%**, **Intel 0 / 0 / 9 / 11 / 13%**, **OSATs 16 / 18 / 19 / 21 / 20%.** **Lower case** (Figure 6/17): same industry revenue to 2028 then **86,883 / 120,081**; TSMC sales **56,578 / 74,227** (47% CAGR, 15%/16% of sales); **Intel 9 / 14 / 18%**, TSMC **65 / 62%**, OSATs 21 / 21%. Two things to take from the table rather than the headline: **Intel is modelled at ZERO external EMIB-T revenue until 2028** in both cases, and **the OSAT share band (16→21%) barely moves between scenarios** — the EMIB-T debate is a TSMC-vs-Intel split, not an OSAT one.
  - **THE INTEL BUILD, AND IT IS ALMOST ENTIRELY GOOGLE.** Intel external EMIB-T sales **5,438 / 9,570 / 15,791** (2028-30E), of which **Google TPU 5,003 / 8,374 / 13,159** and "Others" only **435 / 1,196 / 2,632**. The unit basis is explicit: **Units (mn) 2.3 / 3.5 / 5.0 for Google and 0.2 / 0.5 / 1.0 for Others, at EMIB-T content of US$2,175 / 2,393 / 2,632 per unit** — and UBS defines the unit as a *package*, not a die: **"close to 24mn AI accelerate dies or ~6mn chipsets with four XPUs per package."** So 2030E = 6.0mn packages × 4 XPU dies = 24mn dies. Lower case: Google **2.3 / 4.5 / 6.5mn** packages (US$5,003 / 10,766 / 17,106) and Others **0.2 / 0.5 / 1.5mn**. ⚠️ **Cross-check against Arete (07-21) before mixing bases: Arete prices an Intel EMIB-T package at ~$733/good package on a 510×515mm panel, roughly 3x below UBS's US$2,175 content — almost certainly a different package definition (single Rubin-class chip vs a 4-XPU chipset). Carried side by side, not reconciled.**
  - **THE PANEL-CARRIER / GLASS CLARIFICATION — UBS's most useful contribution, and it CUTS AGAINST the MS glass bullet below.** UBS separates the two places glass shows up. **(1) Panel carrier:** *"During the CoP packaging process, the logic and HBM chips are not attached until after the redistribution layer (RDL) interposer has been formed on the carrier panel, similar to CoWoS-L's chip-last process. The carrier panel serves as a temporary platform to support interposer fabrication and subsequent die attach"* — TSMC is evaluating materials and **"glass is the leading candidate based on current visibility."** **(2) Glass-core substrate** = "next generation ABF substrate," glass at the core wrapped in ABF layers for rigidity, using high-aspect-ratio **TGVs instead of TSVs**, needing new laser/etch tooling and facing copper-implanting, micro-crack and reliability learning curves. **UBS's call: "we believe that glass adoption (whether for panel carrier or glass-core substrate) should not be a major yield or production risk for the ramp of either TSMC's CoPoS or Intel's EMIB-T in 2028… both packaging technologies should be in mass production in 2028 without requiring glass-core substrates,"** with glass-core itself **"unlikely to reach mass production before 2029-30E, if not later"** (Ibiden's own slide puts glass-core at **2030+**). ⚠️ **That is a slower glass-core timeline than MS's per-player ramps logged 07-26 (Innolux+TSMC+Ibiden "MP 2H28 or 2029"; Lens Tech+Intel "meaningful volume from 2H27"; BOE MP 2028) and slower than the MS 06-30 "2H28 adoption" read. Not resolved — but note UBS is arguing glass is NOT ON THE CRITICAL PATH, which is a different claim from arguing it is late.**
  - **THE MECHANICAL DETAIL BEHIND THE ~50% SUBSTRATE YIELD.** EMIB-T is designed for substrates **"5x or even larger vs current EMIB package,"** potentially **>9-10x reticle for AI accelerators (vs 2-3x today) with 20+ layer count**, and needs **more silicon bridges** each carrying TSVs — requiring **"precise metal contacts at both the top and bottom of the substrate cavities"** plus highly accurate bridge placement, steps that **"do not exist in conventional ABF substrate manufacturing."** UBS: **standard EMIB substrate yield has matured to "close to 60%"; EMIB-T is ~50% "for the leading Japan suppliers."** And a tooling consequence the page did not carry: **"TC NCF (thermal compression with non-conductive film) process will be required for bridge attachment on the panel-level substrate cavities, rather than a standard die-attach process,"** forcing bonder suppliers to re-optimise TC NCF tools for **510×515mm² panel format.** The ~90% packaging-yield figure is also now sourced precisely: it is **"based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype,"** against a bar of **"the high 90% range"** for HPC given the value of the compute dies and HBM being integrated.
  - **CoPoS SPECIFICS.** First generation on **310×310mm² panels, migrating to 510×515mm²**; the motivation is that **CoWoS "suffers from meaningful side-area loss when package size increases"** on a round wafer. **TSMC began installing its CoPoS pilot line in late Q226 (set up June 2026) for technology evaluation and qualification, and UBS expects the first-generation process flow, equipment specifications and key suppliers to be finalised by mid-2027** — a dated decision window for every tool vendor on this page. On the roadmap, UBS confirms **14x-reticle CoWoS "with support for around 10 large compute dies and 20 HBM stacks," slated for production in 2028 with potential to scale beyond 14x reticle in 2029**, up from the prior **9.5x-reticle** plan — ⚠️ note the **20-HBM-stack figure attaches to 14x-reticle CoWoS, not to CoPoS**, and UBS says **"scaling CoWoS beyond 10x reticle remains challenging,"** which is the argument for CoPoS. UBS explicitly rebuts the form-factor framing: **"Contrary to the view that TSMC's CoPoS is merely a form-factor change from CoWoS (ie, from circular to square carrier), we think CoPoS represents a compelling advanced packaging upgrade"** — a **"full-generation upgrade to CoWoS rather than merely a packaging form-factor transition."** The power-integrity leg: CoPoS allows a higher count of **embedded deep-trench capacitors (eDTC) / integrated capacitors (iCAP) within or underneath the interposer** (a 2026 TSMC symposium slide), vs EMIB-T's on-bridge MIM caps.
  - **WHY THE SECOND SOURCE EXISTS AT ALL, in UBS's words:** *"due to persistent CoWoS capacity constraints at TSMC in recent years, other advanced packaging solutions from competing foundries and OSATs are gaining traction"* — plus **"geopolitical concerns, which have encouraged customers to diversify supply sources."** And the reopening clause on TSMC's share: **"TSMC's share could further increase if Google's new TPUs (eg. v10) return to CoPoS and adopt CPO integration"** (see [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md)).
  - **THE SUPPLY-CHAIN MAP (Figures 23-24), which is net-new to this page in list form.** *CoPoS:* deposition & RDL **Applied Materials, SCREEN, Tokyo Electron, Canon**; bake/thermal **APT, C Sun, GMM, Kokusai**; wet process **GPTC, Scientech**; debonding **SÜSS MicroTec, Tokyo Electron, EVG, Scientech, E&R engineering, Daxin**; CMP **Applied Materials, Disco, Ebara, LAM Research, Tokyo Electron, GPM**; molding **Apic Yamada, Towa**; pods **Gudeng, Chung King**; underfill dispense **All Ring, Asymtek**; die attach **Kulicke & Soffa, ASMPT, Shibaura**; metrology/inspection **KLA, Chroma, Camtek, Favite, Ta Liang, V5**; OSAT **ASE/SPIL**; substrate **Ibiden, Unimicron, Toppan, Shinko, Nanya PCB, Kinsus**. *EMIB-T (a deliberately shorter list — "a simpler equipment set… it only involves chip-on-substrate assembly and does not require a separate chip-on-panel stage"):* substrate **Ibiden, Unimicron**; OSAT **Amkor**; die attach **Kulicke & Soffa, ASMPT**. **The leading indicator UBS names for EMIB-T capacity: TCB die-bonder tool demand** (Intel has long favoured TCB; K&S and ASMPT its primary suppliers). Also flagged — **"Intel may rely more on US-based equipment suppliers whereas TSMC's advanced packaging supply chain is more localized in Asia."** Share detail: **GPTC could take 70-80% of CoPoS wet process vs ~50% in CoWoS**; **All Ring is currently TSMC's sole supplier of underfill equipment for on-substrate and UBS expects it to stay sole-source in CoPoS and "likely penetrate the CoP process as well"**; **Chroma's BLiS white-light-interferometry metrology entered TSMC in 2024 and has taken an initial CoPoS (310×310mm) order in H126**; **Gudeng is the most credible challenger to incumbent Chung King in advanced-packaging pods.** OSAT split: **ASE the primary Taiwan on-substrate partner for CoPoS; Amkor "the most likely on-substrate partner" in Arizona; Intel vertically integrating EMIB-T at its Penang, Malaysia plant** (under expansion) while the **2025 Intel-Amkor partnership makes Amkor's Korea, Portugal and US lines available for EMIB assembly.** One forward outsourcing vector: **C4 wafer bumping**, "where the industry is in tight supply."
  - **SUBSTRATE — the value-migration leg, with a share number.** **Ibiden holds 70-80% share of ABF substrate in NVIDIA Blackwell / Blackwell Ultra** (⚠️ this is *Ibiden's* share, not Unimicron's) and UBS expects it to stay dominant in both CoPoS and EMIB-T, on the argument that **"ABF package costs represent only a small portion of the overall bill of materials for GPU/HBM,"** so reliability and track record outweigh cost even as customers multi-source into an anticipated high-end ABF shortage. **Ibiden's ¥220bn Gama-plant investment (announced Feb-2026) targets EMIB-T mass production from late 2027 with a meaningful ramp in 2028 — and "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity, increasing the likelihood of additional capex announcements."** Because EMIB-T's bridge-cavity/TSV steps do not exist in conventional ABF flows, **"substrate makers can enjoy much higher value add relative to standard ABF substrate"** — the cleanest statement yet of the value-migration thesis this page tracks. **Unimicron** is a key CoPoS beneficiary and a prior low-volume EMIB partner now developing EMIB-T, but **"yields remain below optimal levels"** → UBS sees it as **Intel's second source if volumes exceed expectations**, with **Nan Ya PCB also expanding capacity with a CoPoS focus.** UBS top substrate picks: **Ibiden + Unimicron.**
  - **RATINGS / PTs (Figure 1; Taiwan priced 24 Jul 2026, non-Taiwan 23 Jul 2026).** TSMC **Buy PT NT$3,650** (NT$2,350); Intel **Neutral PT US$121** (US$100); MediaTek **Buy NT$6,500** (NT$3,750); Broadcom **Buy US$485** (US$392); **All Ring 6187.TWO INITIATED Buy PT NT$1,550** (NT$942) — a second initiation on the name after BofA's Buy/PO NT$1,500 (07-13), the two desks within 3% of each other; ASE **Buy NT$835**; ASMPT **Buy HK$200** (HK$167); Chroma ATE **Buy NT$2,960**; Gudeng **Buy NT$710**; GPTC **Buy NT$5,000**; Powertech **Neutral NT$240** vs a NT$279.50 price (the only PT below spot in the table); Ibiden **Buy ¥20,500**; Unimicron **Buy NT$1,200**. Off-coverage Asian detail on [outros-asia.md](outros-asia.md); the TPU leg on [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md). [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_adv_packaging.html)
  - **⚠️ THE DISAGREEMENT THIS NOTE DOES NOT SETTLE, AND MUST NOT BE READ AS SETTLING — TPU v9's packaging outcome.** UBS models **Google TPU as the entirety of Intel's external EMIB-T revenue from 2028**, calls TPU v9 (developed by MediaTek) Intel's **"first major external product"**, and cites **~90% packaging yield on MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype trial runs**. **Fubon (07-08) says the opposite outcome:** Google set a **95%+ yield bar**, judged "very challenging to achieve by mid-2027," and concluded Google **"will at last rely on TSMC's [2028 CoWoS] expansion plan."** **Both remain on this page. They are not reconcilable on the facts available — one is a revenue model built on the program landing at Intel, the other a checks-based judgement that it does not.** UBS is also internally loose on the date (EMIB-T "targets H227 commercial ramp" / "slated for mass production in late 2027" / TPU v9 "set for mass production in 2028"; Ibiden's EMIB-T line "late 2027… meaningful ramp 2028") — read the window as **H2'27 start, 2028 volume**, not a point estimate. Separately, UBS's scalability verdict **agrees** with the MS/Amerian 06-30 read that EMIB-T wins above ~9.7 reticles (UBS: EMIB-T "well suited for ultra-large packages using substrate scaling," CoPoS "constrained by interposer/panel warpage") while **disagreeing** with the inference drawn from it, since UBS still has TSMC at 65-70% share in 2030E.
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — 🔴 THE FIRST FULL SELL-SIDE STRESS TEST OF EMIB-T vs CoPoS, with a TAM and a stated bull case for Intel that TSMC still wins (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership?", 2026-07-26/27).** **Sizing: advanced-packaging TAM US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR.** UBS's **base case** hands Intel "a meaningful **13% industry share, or US$16bn of sales by 2030, equivalent to packages for ~24mn accelerator dies**" — and even then **TSMC compounds advanced-packaging sales at a 50% CAGR 2026-30, holds 65-70% share, and takes advanced packaging to 18% of TSMC sales in 2030E from 9% in 2026E.** UBS's **lower case** (stronger EMIB-T adoption) only moves that to **47% CAGR / 62% share / 16% of sales** — so **the base-vs-bear spread is roughly 2pts of TSMC revenue mix over four years.** That is the most useful bound on packaging-disruption risk now on this page, and it implies the debate matters far more to Intel's numbers than to TSMC's. **Timing, which favours Intel: EMIB-T targets mass production in H2'27-28, slightly AHEAD of CoPoS in 2028** (CoPoS for Nvidia in 2028, others 2029-30). **The technical trade-off, stated cleanly:** EMIB-T's edge is **structural cost via interposer elimination plus scalability to ultra-large packages built directly on large substrates**; CoPoS offers **tighter interconnect, better signal integrity, broader integration including 3D stacking "and eventually, CPO," plus TSMC's execution record.** **Each side's named risks:** Intel must lift EMIB-T from **~90-92% validation yields** to HVM levels and faces **EMIB-T substrate yields "may currently be at ~50%, vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates"** (with **Ibiden capacity expansion able to "offset lower yields and support selected products like Google's TPU v9"**); TSMC must overcome **"CoPoS warpage, panel process uniformity, and new equipment readiness."** **And a commercial risk specific to EMIB-T that is not technical at all: "integration between the foundry (TSMC) and back-end (Intel) processes, as well as yield responsibility"** — who eats the loss when TSMC owns the die and Intel owns the package. UBS's verdict: **EMIB-T "could be a meaningful second-source for selected hyperscaler ASICs, although CoPoS should remain the preferred platform for customers prioritizing the highest performance and execution reliability."** Ratings: **TSM Buy; INTC Neutral "due to valuation considerations"; likes MediaTek on its TPU opportunity, seeing "the EMIB-T execution risks as manageable."**
- **⚠️ NEW (2026-07-26) — THE YIELD PICTURE ON THIS PAGE IS NOW WORSE-DEFINED, NOT BETTER, AND THAT IS THE HONEST STATE. Four marks on EMIB-T packaging yield: Fubon 60-70% (06-05) · UBS ~90-92% "validation" (07-26) · KeyBanc "have reached 98%" (07-24) · ex-Intel CVP Kaizad "mature yield 98-99%" (07-16). Two marks on EMIB-T substrate yield: UBS ~50% (07-26) · KeyBanc 60-70% (07-24).** A 60-to-99% span on the single gate the whole EMIB-T-vs-CoWoS debate turns on (**Google set a 95%+ bar for TPU V9**). Partial reconciliation available: **UBS's is the only figure explicitly labelled *validation* rather than production yield**, and KeyBanc's is second-hand via a trade newsletter with no line-vs-packaged-unit distinction. **Not resolved. Do not pick one.** What both the 07-25 and 07-26 sources agree on is that **the binding constraint has moved upstream from bridges to SUBSTRATES**, and UBS's base case routes around it via Ibiden capacity rather than solving it via yield.
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — GLASS CORE SUBSTRATES now have dated ramps per player, and Intel is ~12-18 months ahead of TSMC on this axis too (MS · Derrick Yang / Vivi Huang / Andy Meng, "Progress for Glass in Advanced Packaging," 2026-07-26):** **Lens Tech (300433.SZ) + Intel on glass core with TGVs — "pilot line ready by end-2026, and more meaningful volume shipments might start from 2H27"** (MS thinks Lens Tech "may be working with other potential Korean players on this as well"); **Innolux (3481.TW) + TSMC + Ibiden — "mass production seen in 2H28 or 2029"**; **BOE (000725.SZ)** with fabless customers in China and North America, **capacity in 2027, MP 2028** if things progress, aspiring to the full glass-core ABF process; **AUO (2049.TW)** focused on glass for LEO antenna modules and micro-LED optical, no MP timetable. **Same ordering as EMIB-T vs CoPoS — worth naming as a pattern rather than a coincidence: on both bridges and substrates, Intel is earlier and TSMC is betting on the higher-performance version arriving later.** Why glass matters here: it attacks **warpage** (CTE matching) — one of the three CoPoS risks UBS names above — plus lower signal loss and greater mechanical strength at panel dimensions, and it is the structural answer to the ~50% ABF-substrate-yield constraint. MS's caveat kept: display-chain players "are gaining more traction in the TGV and copper plating processes," but **"technical challenges are still there, since the requirement for precision and density is much higher than what they used to deal with. The hurdles are more related to process control instead of pure equipment capability"** — and "more meaningful revenue contribution should take time."
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — the demand side, in management's own words, and it is the justification for all of the above: "TSMC highlighted at its 2Q26 call that back-end sits in deeper shortage than front-end, with packaging capacity so tight it now limits customer growth"** (via Jefferies Global SEMIS Daily) — i.e. **packaging, not wafers, is the named growth constraint.** Corroborated from a completely different seat by an **ex-ASM International senior process engineer (AlphaSense expert call, 07-25)**: demand for **"CoWoS and future CoPoS technologies as outpacing supply for the foreseeable future, with supply constraints driven more by TOOL AVAILABILITY than by financial or market factors"** (bonding tools specifically), a market **bifurcated between TSMC in complex packaging and OSATs in mature**, and a **TSMC-NVIDIA relationship "deeply integrated… making it difficult for competitors to break in."** Caveat: 2-page expert-network summary, front-end background — a well-informed prior, not an audit. Also: **TSMC announced two ADDITIONAL advanced-packaging fabs on the 2Q26 call**, and **NVDA prepaid $1.5bn to AMKR** for Arizona back-end capacity from early C28 ([[NVDA]], 07-23).
- **NEW (2026-07-24) — the biggest package shipping, and the first ACTIVE bridge: AMD's MI455 is the largest CoWoS-L module at 5.5x reticle with 3,470mm² of logic silicon, the only product on TSMC SoIC-X hybrid bonding — and SemiAnalysis believes it is the first chip shipping active Local Silicon Interconnect, with TSMC's own ISSCC "test vehicle" being the MI455 interposer itself (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The most consequential packaging-roadmap datapoint of the batch, and it is a genuine capability step, not a size record. **The package:** *"The MI455 package is the largest CoWoS-L module shipping at 5.5x reticle size. AMD is still the only company adopting TSMC's SoIC-X hybrid bonding, which allows AMD to scale silicon footprint in the z dimension as well as the x and y dimensions. All this comes together to bring the MI455 to a total of **3,470mm² of logic silicon in the package, by far the most amount of silicon that is being shipped in a single package**"* — built from **8 N2 XCDs hybrid-bonded onto 2 reticle-sized base dies plus 2 N3P I/O dies, with 12 HBM4 stacks** (memory leg on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md)). **The net-new roadmap item — active LSI (aLSI):** in the brief history of CoWoS-L, the bridges connecting chiplets **"have been passive, containing only wiring as well as capacitors. Active LSIs come with actual circuitry."** At **TSMC's aLSI presentation at ISSCC 2026**, TSMC demonstrated **active bridges with a low-power repeater circuit that regenerates signals mid-channel** — *"because the bridge now shares the burden of maintaining signal integrity, **the PHYs on the top dies can shrink meaningfully, at a nearly negligible energy cost, reclaiming leading-edge silicon and shoreline for compute and memory**."* SemiAnalysis's tell that it is already shipping: **"the 'test vehicle' TSMC showed is the MI455 interposer: with two base dies, 12 HBM4 stacks, and two IO dies."** Read for this theme: **the bridge stops being passive plumbing and becomes an active silicon layer that buys back beachfront on the expensive top dies** — a new axis of packaging value (and a new yield/known-good-die problem) on top of the area/reticle-count race this page has been tracking. **Rack-level interconnect content per Helios rack (the connector/cable read):** **10,368 differential pairs of copper backplane cable** (each GPU's 72x 200G lanes carried over two DPs each = 144 DPs per GPU, plus a 144DP male and 144DP female connector per GPU), **1,728 flyover cables** routed from the backplane connectors to the ports around each TH6 switch ASIC, **>550 Broadcom Ethernet retimers** (AMD's weak SerDes means up to 85% of the backplane must be retimed), and **Molex "Genesis" cables carrying the 128G UALink between the MI455X and the Pensando Vulcano NIC in the compute tray — up to 12 per tray**, crammed into 1U alongside liquid-cooling tubes and power cables. SemiAnalysis's own warning: *"all the cables become potential points of failure during assembly and will make manufacturing inefficient."* **Content sizing: total backplane + compute-tray content per rack = $68,928 ($44,352 backplane + $24,576 flyover cables).** (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24; see [../AMD.md](../AMD.md), [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md), [../APH.md](../APH.md); Molex and Celestica have no wiki pages; the optics/MI500 leg is on [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **EXTENDS the 2026-06-14 Kirin bullet below (dedup pass, 2026-07-24) — Huawei's τ scaling law / LogicFolding, now with the actual density roadmap and the like-for-like caveat that deflates it (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14).** The teardown is already on this page as the "packaging IS the scaling vector" China proof point; the numbers behind Huawei's claim were not. **What LogicFolding actually is:** at **ISCAS 2026** Huawei unveiled its **tau (τ) scaling law**, reframing process scaling in the time domain — τ being the time cost of data movement and processing (switching delays, RC propagation, compute/memory/networking latency), which "outside Huawei's terminology is called system-technology co-optimization." **LogicFolding is its implementation: an aggressive 3D stacking approach that goes beyond AMD's V-Cache (SRAM above/below a CPU die) and the MI350X's active interposer dies (AIDs carrying cache, IO, NoC and embedded MIM caps) — "parts of the same logic block are split across multiple active dies bonded face-to-face at ultra-fine pitch,"** so the bond interface behaves like an additional metal layer and the longest critical paths get shorter, cutting buffer overhead rather than merely adding cache. **The roadmap:** prime-core frequency from **2.75 GHz in the Kirin 9030 to roughly 5 GHz by 2031** (3.1 and 3.39 GHz prime cores already in Huawei's labs, power unknown; everything beyond is design/simulation/pathfinding, so these are targets); density from **SMIC N+3's ~114 MTr/mm² normalised Bohr basis to 215 MTr/mm² by 2030 and 295 MTr/mm² in 2031** — the 2031 jump "implying either a third active layer, partial EUV insertion or aggressive planar DUV scaling." ⚠️ **The caveat that has to travel with the headline: this is density per package footprint using stacked logic, not a like-for-like foundry comparison — and Huawei's own methodology flatters everyone else more. "Applying it to AMD's MI450X with an N2 top die on an N3P base die yields a theoretical density of 460.2 MTr/mm² in 2026, compared with Huawei's 295 MTr/mm² in 2031."** Also worth holding: **the Kirin 9030 itself does NOT use LogicFolding** — it is a conventional mobile SoC package and serves as "the baseline for how far Huawei and SMIC can push planar scaling." **The Kirin's own package, for the record:** an all-organic **iPoP** stack — **BT laminate memory substrate, organic RDL interposer, ABF-over-BT package substrate, and no silicon interposer** — chosen to keep CTE close to the PCB and avoid interposer cost the SoC's bandwidth does not require. (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, 2026-06-14; see [../SMIC.md](../SMIC.md), [../AMD.md](../AMD.md); Huawei has no wiki page; the litho/WFE leg is on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md), the policy leg on [china-export.md](china-export.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **BACKFILL — HISTORICAL, dated 2025-08-20 (ingested 2026-07-24; ~11 months old) — the GB200 NVL72 backplane anatomy and why the connector is a consumable: Amphenol Paladin HD male/female pairs whose screw tension governs signal integrity, and which endure at most 200 mating cycles before disposal (SemiAnalysis "H100 vs GB200 NVL72 Training Benchmarks — Power, TCO and Reliability Analysis", 2025-08-20).** A packaging/interconnect datapoint with a direct [../APH.md](../APH.md) read-through that this page did not carry. Rack anatomy: **each GB200 NVL72 rack is 18 servers (compute trays), 9 NVSwitch trays and 4 copper backplane cartridges** (72 GPUs because only 64 contribute to the mega training run, the other 8 held as hot spares). **The cartridges connect to each compute tray through a set of male and female Amphenol Paladin HD connectors** — the female screwed onto the edge of the Bianca board PCB inside the compute tray — and *"if these screws are not fastened with a precise amount of tension, the entire connector can suffer from signal integrity issues."* Diagnostics are the real cost: identifying which of the compute-tray, switch-tray or cable-cartridge Paladin connector is faulting "can take upwards of a couple hours." **The consumable point: because the Paladin HD connectors are plated with a thin layer of gold for signal integrity, they have "an endurance of at most 200 mating cycles before the component must be discarded"** (Amphenol datasheet cited). Service reality: **a backplane swap takes 8-24 hours and requires first removing 10 rack stiffeners** and re-torquing the new backplane precisely — and it **drains the whole rack** (as does a switch-tray swap), which is why hot-spare *racks* are needed on top of the 8 hot-spare GPUs. Read for this theme: **at rack scale the connector is a wear part with a bounded mating-cycle life and a torque-sensitive install** — a recurring-content argument for the connector vendors and a serviceability argument for cableless/fewer-connector designs (the same axis on which SemiAnalysis criticises AMD's Helios rack in the 07-24 bullet above). (SemiAnalysis "H100 vs GB200 NVL72 Training Benchmarks", 2025-08-20 — **historical backfill; GB200-generation figures, superseded by GB300/Rubin-era rack designs**; see [../APH.md](../APH.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2025_08_20_ENC_H100_vs_GB200_NVL72_Training_Benchmarks_-_Power_TCO_and_Reliability_Analysis_Software_Improvement_Over_Ti.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-21) — Arete "EMIB vs CoWoS" deep dive: Rubin Ultra reverts to a two-die solution as CoWoS-L warpage/heat slow the four-die transition; Intel EMIB-T a credible incremental second source at a 25% price discount → $1.5bn ('27) / $3.7bn ('28) AP revenue (Arete Research "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21).** Arete (Janco Venter) frames advanced packaging as "the fulcrum" of post-Moore compute scaling, with **[../TSM.md](../TSM.md) CoWoS fully booked through '27** and customers already planning '28 — the opening for Intel. Key reads: **(1) Rubin Ultra likely a two-die (not four-die) solution** — the move from single-die Hopper (CoWoS-S) → dual-die Blackwell (CoWoS-L gen1, 2-reticle GPU + 8 HBM stacks) → four-die has been slowed by **heat dissipation + interposer warpage** as interposer reticle size grows from 3.3x → 6x/9x; NVDA looks to the **PCB to drive compute density** instead (dovetails with the Rubin Ultra 384GB HBM4E reset on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md)). **(2) TSMC CoWoS capacity 140k wspm YE26 → 235k YE28** (total incl. SPIL 25k + Amkor 16k = **176k → 276k**); TSMC's first US AP facility in permitting, construction to start before end-'26, Amkor Arizona as overflow. **(3) Intel EMIB-T AP capacity 14k → 60k wspm by YE28** (New Mexico 12k→35k, Malaysia 2k→25k) — of which **0k / 109k / 272k** annualized available to external customers ('26/'27/'28, 45% of total by '28 after 40%-uplift internal reservation) → **$0 / $1.5bn / $3.7bn Intel AP revenue** at ~$14k/panel & 100% util. **(4) Pricing framework:** CoWoS-L gen2 ≈ **30% of front-end wafer** (N2 $25k / A16 $33k → $7.4k / $9.8k CoWoS; 10 packages/wafer at 5.5x reticle → **$740-977/package**); Intel's **510×515mm EMIB-T panel fits 16-25 packages → 14-23 good at 90% yield**, priced at a **25% discount to TSMC ≈ $733/good package** (~$10.5k/panel for a Rubin-class chip). EMIB-T's structural edge: **no silicon interposer** (Si bridges embedded in substrate = fewer steps, lower cost, better Si utilization) + **panel (rectangular) vs round-wafer edge waste**. **(5) Reticle roadmap:** Intel EMIB-T proven **8x '26 → 12x '28**; TSMC CoWoS-L **5.5x '26 → 9.5x '27 → 14x '28**. **(6) Google placed a 3M-unit TPU order with Intel for '28** (per The Information) — first evidence of major Intel AP adoption (Intel invested $5bn late-'25); at $733/package ≈ $2.2bn. Arete's net: TSMC CoWoS stays "the more mature process and the go-to for large GPU/XPU packages," but **excess demand flows to Intel + OSATs** as customers keep a credible second source for pricing leverage + supply security. Also flags four AP tech vectors: **panel-level (TSMC CoPoS to 310×310mm), glass substrates/glass-core interposers, co-packaged optics (COUPE), and relentless package-size/HBM-count growth** (Intel targeting 120×120mm/12+ HBM → 120×180mm/16+ by '28). (Arete Research "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21; see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semis_Hardware_Real_Time_Views_July_26_Edition_1.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-16) — JPM/Gokul post-TSMC-2Q: [../TSM.md](../TSM.md) "revised up our CoWoS numbers ~3-4x this year"; advanced packaging grows ~10% → low-teens % of revenue; EMIB ramp mostly 2028 via MediaTek TPU V9; value shifts to substrate makers from OSATs (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-16).** In its TSMC 2Q26 post-earnings read, JPM says TSMC **"revised up our CoWoS numbers ~3-4x this year,"** with **advanced packaging growing from ~10% of revenue to low-teens (~60% growth vs ~40% corporate)**; **EMIB is substrate/panel-level with its ramp mostly in 2028 via the MediaTek TPU V9**, and **value shifts toward substrate makers (Ibiden / Unimicron) from OSATs**, with **TCB via ASMPT / KNS** and **demand > supply for advanced 2.5D packaging over the next 2-3 years**. Corroborates the "value migrates off TSMC to substrate/OSAT" and EMIB-T-2028-via-TPU-V9 threads already tracked below. (J.P. Morgan · Gokul Hariharan "TSMC 2Q26 post-earnings", 2026-07-16; see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../MEDIATEK.md](../MEDIATEK.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_16_tsmc_post_earnings.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-16) — Expert call (Kaizad, ex-Intel VP) "EMIB vs CoWoS" trade study: EMIB reticle limit ~6-7x vs CoWoS ~3-4x; CoWoS wins on warpage/power/IO but wastes ~2/3 of the wafer; the two are converging as package footprints approach ~10,000mm² (ex-Intel VP expert call, 2026-07-16).** A packaging trade study from an ex-Intel VP: **EMIB reaches a reticle limit of ~6-7x vs CoWoS's ~3-4x**, but **CoWoS's single interposer is better on warpage / power delivery / IO while wasting ~2/3 of the (circular) wafer**; the two approaches are **converging — TSMC CoWoS-L goes bridge-based while Intel EMIB-T adds TSVs**. Package specs cited: **8-12 HBM stacks** and **package footprints approaching ~10,000mm²**. Corroborates the EMIB-T-vs-CoWoS convergence + circular-wafer-utilization limits already tracked on this page (SemiAnalysis ECTC 2026). (Expert call · Kaizad, ex-Intel VP, 2026-07-16; see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_16_emib_vs_cowos_expert.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — Jefferies/Fubon post-2Q call: CoWoS crunch is an explicit OSAT + Intel-IBP tailwind; TSMC to raise 2027 wafer prices ≥10% (Jefferies/Fubon call — Sherman Chang, 2026-07-17).** Post-TSMC-2Q, Fubon frames the advanced-packaging crunch as a **structural positive for OSATs (ASE, Amkor)** and for **Intel's IBP** advanced-packaging offering — TSMC publicly welcomes other suppliers to alleviate CoWoS tightness (though there are rumors of a "slow learning-curve improvement in IBP"). Also new: TSMC plans **2027 wafer price hikes of at least 10% for advanced nodes (3nm/2nm)** on supply tightness (GM-accretive after the N2-ramp dilution), with **super-hot-runs** (e.g. NVDA Blackwell) helping near-term and **no mid-year hikes this year**. Fubon models FY26 capex $60-64bn → **$80bn 2027 / $95bn 2028** (~10% of which is advanced packaging/test/mask) and **server-CPU "very strong growth" 2027+** feeding CoWoS demand for CPU projects. Corroborates the 07-16 call reads below. (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17; see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_jefferies_update_with_fubon_post_tsmc_2q_earnings.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — Jefferies/Fubon: the CoWoS-generation split is the OSAT-opportunity map; ASE doubling to 45–50K/mo; adv-packaging ~20% of TSMC capex ≈ $16B (2027) / $20B (2028) (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17).** TSMC publicly acknowledged **advanced-packaging (CoWoS) capacity constraints and welcomed other suppliers (OSATs) to step in** — read as positive for **ASE, Amkor** and for **Intel + MediaTek**. Generation map: major GPUs / custom ASICs mostly use **CoWoS-L** (where TSMC's capacity expansion is concentrated), while **CPUs and networking chips use CoWoS-S/R** (examples cited as stated: Nvidia "V100", AMD "MI200", Broadcom Tomahawk) — TSMC lacks incremental CoWoS-S/R capacity, so it **outsources those to OSAT partners**. **ASE advanced-packaging capacity doubling from ~30K to 45–50K units/month** ("very aggressive"), with domestic CoWoS-equipment suppliers (transcript: "C-Sung", GPTC, "All Ring") benefiting. **Advanced packaging ≈ 20% of TSMC capex → ~$16B (2027), ~$20B (2028)**, with strong AI/server-CPU demand into 2027. _(The ASE ~30K→45–50K/mo mark sits alongside the existing Fubon FoCoS 30k→45kwpm end-27 and UBS 20k→~50k wpm end-27E figures — distinct broker views, none superseded.)_ (see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_jefferies_update_with_fubon_post_tsmc_2q_earnings.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — ASML read-through: ASML is entering 3D integration / advanced packaging — XT 260 i-line tool launched, hybrid bonding under development; strategy to be detailed at the June 2027 CMD (ASML mgmt "2Q26 London Roadshow", 2026-07-17).** ASML is moving into **3D integration / advanced packaging** (shifting some stacking toward the front end): it launched the **XT 260** i-line-based tool (corroborates the TWINSCAN XT:260 entry already tracked below), with **hybrid bonding under development**. Rationale — with shrink slowing while AI demands ~10x more transistors every 2 years, stacking becomes the vector (potential DRAM-on-logic). ASML will detail its 3D-integration strategy at its **Capital Markets Day in June 2027**. (see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../BESI.md](../BESI.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_asml_2q26_london_roadshow_virtual_group_meeting.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-16) — TSMC 2Q26 call confirms CoWoS "severe shortage," welcomes Intel EMIB-T as complementary; additional US$100bn Arizona incl. advanced-packaging fabs; glass substrate ~1 year from maturity (TSMC 2Q26 earnings call transcript, Bloomberg, 2026-07-16; Fubon "Capex hike a strong indicator", 2026-07-16).** On the call, C.C. Wei said packaging capacity is **"so tight… limited by customers' growth"** and explicitly **welcomes competitor alternatives (Intel EMIB-T) as flexibility that "helps TSMC's front-end wafer business"** (front-end and back-end are "two different things"). Fubon reads this as a **positive for Intel's EMIB-T advanced-node foundry business in 2027-28** and for **MediaTek** (which could raise its custom-ASIC market-share target on its upcoming call) — TSMC is CoWoS-cleanroom-space-constrained. On glass/alternative substrates: TSMC is developing lower-cost alternatives with substrate vendors, and the earlier-announced product line is **"about another one year to be mature"** before production; **CoWoS remains "the majority."** TSMC also reaffirmed the **14× reticle CoWoS roadmap** and that **COUPE (CPO) is in production now**, ramping. Capex: the **additional US$100bn Arizona investment (→US$265bn total, "four or more fabs… front and back end")** includes advanced-packaging fabs; FY26 capex raised to **US$60-64bn** with **~10-20% to advanced packaging, testing, mask & others**. Fubon estimates **N3 210-220k wpm and N2 120k wpm by end-2027** (front-end capacity feeding the packaging pull). (see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md), [../MEDIATEK.md](../MEDIATEK.md)) [Fubon source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Capex_hike_a_strong_indicator.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-13) — BofA initiates All Ring (6187 TT) Buy — independent sizing: packaging & testing industry capex +46% CAGR '26-28E led by TSMC CoWoS + ASE FoCoS (BofA · Cathy Hsu Liu / Haas Liu, 2026-07-13).** BofA initiates coverage on back-end equipment supplier **All Ring (6187 TT) at Buy, PO NT$1,500 (36x 2027 P/E; price NT$1,050)**, modeling **sales/EPS +34%/+42% CAGR 2026-28E** with GM 50-55%, OpM 29%→34%. The read-through for this page is the demand framing: All Ring's semi-equipment business grows **+34% CAGR** driven by **packaging & testing industry capex rising at a ~46% CAGR '26-28E, "led by TSMC CoWoS and ASE FoCoS expansion"** (All Ring holds high share in chip-level dispensing + heat-sink), plus rising TAM across FOPLP / SoIC / CoPoS. A second, off-coverage broker corroboration of the ASE-FoCoS + CoWoS capacity ramp already tracked below (Fubon: ASE FoCoS to 45kwpm end-27). All Ring is off-coverage (no wiki page) — logged here as a theme read-through. (BofA · Cathy Hsu Liu, All Ring initiation, 2026-07-13; CPO-equipment leg on [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md); see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-11) — Susquehanna: TSMC COUPE FAU-constrained through YE27, high-volume ramp a 2028 story; CoWoS ~130K→195K wpm (YE26→YE28); adv-packaging spend/wafer ~$5→~$8 (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-11).** Susquehanna frames TSMC's **COUPE (co-packaged optics, 3D/hybrid bonding) as FAU-constrained through YE27, with the high-volume ramp a 2028 story**: COUPE PIC capacity is **<0.5K wpm today → only ~5-10K wpm by YE27**, and FAU (fiber-array-unit) challenges cap COUPE shipments **<0.5M units/qtr** (contributing to Spectrum-X yield constraints). Broader packaging roadmap: **CoWoS ~130K wpm (YE26) → ~195K wpm (YE28)**, SoIC ramping through 2028, and **advanced-packaging spend/wafer ~$5 (2021-24) → ~$8 (2025-28)**. Industry adopts **NPO + 400G/lane pluggables first**, with broader CPO at the 3.2T node ~2028. Competitive read: **GLW's FAU/glass-packaging stack (co-developed w/ GlobalFoundries) an alternative to TSMC CoWoS-L/COUPE**, and a faster COUPE ramp is **neutral-to-negative for TSEM** (standalone PIC supplier). (see [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md), [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../GLW.md](../GLW.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-09/08) — End-27 CoWoS side-by-side: GS ~220k pure CoWoS (~280k incl. WMC) vs Gokul/JPM ~175k→200k potential — the divergence is the signal (Goldman Sachs "TSMC preview" — Evelyn Yu, 2026-07-09; JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08).** Two desks size end-2027 TSMC CoWoS differently. **GS** now models **pure CoWoS ~220k wpm by end-2027 (from ~130k end-2026)** and **CoWoS + WMC ~280k wpm by end-2027**, having raised its CoWoS number by **+30k into 2027 chiefly on the Nvidia Vera CPU**; GS also lifts TSMC **FY27 capex to $78bn (from $70bn)**, FY26 ~$56bn, on stronger N3/N2 + CoWoS expansion (Goldman Sachs "TSMC preview", 2026-07-09). **Gokul/JPM** currently has **2027 CoWoS ~175k with upward-revision potential to ~200k** — but most of the incremental upside goes to **OSATs (ASE, Amkor), not TSMC**, because it is driven by the two merchant CPUs (Nvidia Vera on CoWoS-R, AMD Venice on CoWoS-L), which are only ~20-25% packaged at TSMC with the rest at OSATs (AMD largely ASE; Vera split TSMC/Amkor/ASE) (JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08). Both sit within the JPM/Jefferies/Fubon end-27 range tracked below. (see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md)) [GS source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_09_gs_on_tsmc.html) · [Gokul source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_08_gokul_preview_tsmc_2q26.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-08) — Gokul: SOIC is complementary to (not a replacement for) CoWoS — logic-stacking still needs CoWoS for logic-memory (HBM) integration; full SOIC customer map (JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08).** Gokul frames **SOIC as logic-die stacking** (integrating 4-5 compute dies + an IO die heterogeneously rather than one low-yield mono-die), but the composite logic die **still integrates with HBM via CoWoS** — so SOIC layers on top of CoWoS, not instead of it. SOIC customer roadmap: **AMD** (MI300 → MI450/MI500, SOIC + CoWoS-L), **Apple** (M5 Max / M5 Ultra), **AWS Trainium 4**, **OpenAI 2nd-gen Habanero ASIC**, one of **Meta MTIA 400/450** (2H27/2028), and **some Nvidia Finman SKUs** (SRAM-on-die stacking or a fast-inference die alongside the GPU); **Google's next TPU stays on CoWoS, not SOIC**. Also flagged: SOIC used for CPO (PIC+EIC hybrid bonding). On CoWoS mass-production timing: **pilot 28 / MP 29**, so **Finman is likely too complex for CoWoS — "Finman Ultra" (29) probably the first CoWoS product**. In-house Arm CPUs (Google/AWS/MSFT) still on simple BGA today, but follow-on gens may adopt CoWoS. (see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../AMD.md](../AMD.md), [../AAPL.md](../AAPL.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_08_gokul_preview_tsmc_2q26.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-10) — Rumor: TSMC 2027 CoWoS capacity ≥200k wpm ("conservative"); plants on 24-hr shifts (@dnystedt, media report, 2026-07-10).** Media report (citing unnamed supply-chain sources) puts **TSMC's 2027 CoWoS capacity at ≥200,000 wpm as a conservative estimate**, with AI-chip packaging still in severe shortage; **nearly all TSMC plant-construction projects are running 24-hour shifts to accelerate the build-out**, partly due to competition from Intel and others for construction/equipment. Sits at the top of the JPM (~175k end-27) / Jefferies (180k end-27) / Fubon (180k end-27) range already tracked below — a rumor/upside data point, not a rating. (see [TSM.md](../TSM.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-08) — MS 2027 CoWoS/rack math: ~7mn Rubin chips + 90k NVL72 racks in 2027; AMD 2027 CoWoS ~240k (MI455/MI450 ~1.5mn); Blackwell "inventory" was a supply buffer, fully consumed in 2026 (MS · Charlie Chan, "AI Supply Chain — 2027 CoWoS Allocation & ASIC Dynamics", 2026-07-08).** MS expects **~7mn Rubin chips and ~90k NVL72 racks in 2027**; **AMD's 2027 CoWoS allocation ~240k with combined MI455/MI450 shipments ~1.5mn**; TPU MP timing skews to 4Q; the Blackwell chip "inventory" turned out to be a supply-chain buffer that will be **fully consumed by 2026**. Google TPU checks: KYEC 3Q26 rev ~+10% q/q (below prior +15%) on slight Rubin/Sunfish delays; Sunfish full-year ~960k (mostly 4Q); Zebrafish 4Q26 ramp unchanged. Jefferies (Cristina Titu, 2026-07-09) adds that TSMC advanced-packaging outsourcing is tilting to Taiwanese suppliers (C Sun expanding capacity ~6x; CoPoS mini-line orders 1H26, MP 2H27) and JPM/Gokul (WSTS, 2026-07-09) sizes the **N3 supply-demand gap at ~600k wafers**. (see [AMD.md](../AMD.md), [TSM.md](../TSM.md), [GOOG.md](../GOOG.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — ECTC 2026 crystallizes the "advanced packaging IS the scaling vector" thesis; EMIB-T the credible CoWoS alternative, plus microfluidic cooling + custom HBM (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02).** SemiAnalysis's ECTC roundup frames the structural read plainly: "as transistor-density scaling has slowed, **advanced packaging has become the primary scaling vector**," but the package itself is now hitting limits — **circular interposers constrain package size and wafer utilization**, HBM4E doubles I/O count, and multi-kW packages overwhelm conventional cooling. Concrete disclosures: **(1) Intel EMIB-T** = next-gen EMIB with TSVs, tighter bump pitch, larger packages, on-bridge MIM caps + power delivery — "expected to be used in Google's TPU v9" and **"the most credible alternative to TSMC's CoWoS platform for large-package AI accelerators."** Intel validated **36/35µm bump pitch on 2× reticle silicon** (65% denser than Granite Rapids' 45µm), expanding to **4.5× reticle with certification targeted end-2026**, and showed a **240×240mm quarter-panel (≈67 reticles) test vehicle** (albeit with "severe warpage"); TSVs cut package DC voltage drop **68–80%** and on-bridge MIM caps improve PDN AC impedance **>82%**. Still behind CoWoS (TSMC already deployed DTC/eDTC, integrated voltage regulators, active LSI): "Intel is still catching up to an ecosystem that has been executing in volume for years." **(2) Microfluidic cooling** — TSMC pushed coolant directly into silicon via **micropillars on CoWoS-R** (dissipating **5.3 kW at 8 LPM**, uniform >5 kW across the vehicle), while **Microsoft etched straight microchannels into an actual Nvidia GH200 GPU** (51–60% lower GPU junction-to-inlet thermal resistance, ~50% package-level reduction; only 9 potential clogging events across ~4,370 observations over 6 months). **(3) Marvell custom HBM** shortens package routing — a custom base die on an advanced logic process reduces host-ASIC HBM-PHY footprint **~60%** and cuts interposer channel length **6.5mm → 1.5mm** (see [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md) / [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md) for the memory/optical legs). **(4) Interposer alternatives** proliferating beyond EMIB-T — panel-scale organic (Resonac 320×320mm; ASE RDL on 600×600mm panel divided into four), IBM's Direct Bridge Multi-die (30µm-pitch Si bridge, no interposer), Unimicron's bridge-under-chip — all shifting integration to panel-level or interposer-less formats to escape the circular-wafer size/utilization limit. On hybrid bonding, **Applied Materials + EV Group demonstrated 450nm-pitch W2W bonding at 98% yield** across 20M links; **Mitsui Chemicals + ASE** showed pressure-less Cu/polymer bonding at 200°C/10µm pitch. Intel presented an **industry-first 510×515mm 24-layer glass-core panel** with copper-filled TGVs + embedded EMIB bridges + co-formed optical waveguides, no SeWaRe after thermal shock — but glass "still supports manufacturing development rather than high-volume adoption" (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02). _This is the primary-source technical corroboration for the EMIB-T-vs-CoWoS and CoPoS/panel debates already tracked below and in the Key debates section._
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — UBS advanced-packaging read: TSMC leadership intact, ASE the key OSAT beneficiary (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02).** UBS (Sunny Lin) reiterates TSMC's advanced-packaging leadership and Buy, and flags **ASE (Buy, PT NT$835) as a key beneficiary** of the CoWoS full-process / LEAP ramp (ASE capex US$8.5bn 2026 with upside, LEAP & testing sales more than doubling to US$3.5bn 2026 from US$1.6bn 2025 → UBS models US$3.6bn/6.7bn 2026/27E; corporate GM 21.2% (2026) → 25.0%/26.0% (2027/28)); **GPTC (Buy, PT NT$5,000) UBS's top pick in advanced-packaging equipment** (tool shipments 220 units 2026 → 330/400 2027/28E, >80-90% share at ASE vs ~50% at TSMC, panel-level/SoIC/CPO tool ASP rising 22%/16% 2027/28E); **Gudeng (Buy, PT NT$710)** on >85% EUV-pod share as TSMC ramps N2. UBS also notes **CPO development strengthens TSMC's Cloud-AI moat and is positive for ABF-substrate makers** (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02). _(This UBS note's TSMC/ASE/GPTC CoWoS capacity-raise figures — 130k/180k wpm end-26/27, ASE full-process 20k→50k wpm, PT raises — are already logged in the 2026-07-01 State-of-play bullet and Changelog below from the same UBS report; the above adds the ECTC-adjacent ASE-LEAP/GPTC/Gudeng detail.)_
- **NEW (2026-06-25) — Cantor/AMAT "Master Class" sizes Applied's advanced-packaging ramp: AP revenue $500M (CY20) → $1.7B (CY25) → >$2B (CY26); panels expand usable interposer area >7x vs 300mm wafers; 70% SEMVision eBeam share (Cantor · C.J. Muse "AMAT Master Class: DRAM & Advanced Packaging", 2026-06-25).** Applied's DRAM/AP Master Class framed advanced packaging around four inflections — **HBM, hybrid bonding, co-packaged optics, and panels** — and disclosed AMAT AP revenue **$500M (CY20) → $1.7B (CY25) → path to >$2B (CY26)**. The headline packaging read: **larger AI accelerators drive the move to panels, which expand usable interposer area >7x vs 300mm wafers** — HVM is 2-3 yrs out but **equipment selections are happening today**, with AMAT bringing Topaz (deposition/etch/eBeam), a **new maskless digital-litho tool (Ushio partnership)**, ECD (NEXX acquisition) and Kinex surface-prep. On inspection, the more complex back-end requires far more advanced eBeam tools (optical inspection can't catch the smaller defects) — AMAT cited **~70% share on its SEMVision eBeam tool** and announced a new model designed specifically for AP fabs. Its EPIC Center co-innovation model (founding partners Micron/Samsung/TSMC/SK Hynix; Advantest/Broadcom/SCREEN) is pitched as driving earlier design-ins at future packaging inflections. Corroborates the panel/CoPoS + hybrid-bonding + CPO vectors tracked on this page from the equipment-supplier side (the WFE/DRAM legs sit on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md)). (Cantor · C.J. Muse, AMAT OW/PT $650, 2026-06-25; see [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_25_FW_CantorAMAT_Todays_Master_Class_Focus_on_DRAM_and_Advanced_Packaging_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-23) — Google Principal Architect (expert call): CoWoS is "ramping up" and "easing" — no longer the top bottleneck (vs HBM / EML lasers / power / labor, still tight) (Google · Arthur Khessin, Office of the CTO, expert call, 2026-06-23).** In a hyperscaler-perspective memory-cycle call, Google's Arthur Khessin ranked the physical bottlenecks constraining 2027-28 AI build-out (power/grid gear #1, skilled labor #2, then HBM and EML lasers) and put **advanced packaging (CoWoS) as "ramping up" and "easing"** as significant wafer-capacity investment comes online — i.e. CoWoS has moved from the binding constraint to a resolving one, neutral-to-positive for downstream users (NVDA) as it removes a supply gate. A hyperscaler datapoint that the CoWoS crunch is being worked down even as the front-end/OSAT capacity numbers on this page keep rising (the HBM-tightness / EML-laser legs sit on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md) / [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md)). (Google · Arthur Khessin expert call, 2026-06-23; see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_23_Call_Google_-_Principal_Architect_Office_of_the_CTO_Arthur_Khessin_Memory_Cycle_2306_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-14) — SemiAnalysis Kirin 9030 teardown: advanced packaging + system-technology-co-optimization is China's scaling lever without EUV; Huawei claims foundry-"14A-equivalent" density by 2031 via 3D integration (SemiAnalysis "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14).** SemiAnalysis's reverse-engineering of Huawei's Kirin 9030 (SMIC N+3) shows the packaging read-through of the EUV export ban: with planar density unable to keep pace (SMIC N+3 ~114 MTr/mm², ~38% below Intel 18A's HD library), **Huawei shifts the burden onto architecture, packaging and system-level integration (system-technology co-optimization / STCO)** — the mechanism by which Huawei claims it can reach **foundry-"14A-equivalent" density by 2031 via 3D integration**. The Kirin itself uses a conventional **integrated package-on-package (iPoP) stack with an organic RDL interposer and NO silicon interposer**; the note contrasts this with leading-edge 2.5D/3D (e.g. **AMD's MI350X, which places active interposer dies (AIDs) under the accelerator/compute dies to carry cache, IO, NoC and embedded MIM caps**). Read for this theme: advanced packaging is explicitly the substitute scaling vector when litho is constrained — reinforcing the "packaging IS the scaling vector" thesis, now with a China/export-controls proof point (the litho/WFE and China legs sit on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md) / [china-export.md](china-export.md)). (SemiAnalysis, 2026-06-14; see [../SMIC.md](../SMIC.md), [../AMD.md](../AMD.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **Capacity trajectory raised repeatedly through 1H26.** JPM (Gokul) now models total CoWoS capacity at ~**115K / 175K / 220K wpm by end-26 / 27 / 28E**, with total CoWoS *consumption* +86% this year and +30% next (Gokul/JPM CoWoS update, 2026-05-27). Jefferies (Rankie Wong) independently moved its forecast to **180K / 220K wpm by end-27E / 28E** (NVDA briefing, 2026-06-05). Earlier waypoints: Gokul had 145K/175K end-26/27 in mid-May, and 115K/155K/175K on 2026-04-13 — the numbers have only inched up.
- **The non-TSMC share is the real story.** The portion of CoWoS *not* done at TSMC is being raised hard — to roughly **35K wpm (~25–30% of supply) by end-27, up from only ~3–5% / 10–12K today**, mostly ASE + Amkor, a ~4x jump in OSAT full-stack process in 2027 (Gokul, 2026-05-27). ASE skews to AMD (Venice CPU) + some NVDA; Amkor to NVDA (CoWoS-R for Vera) + some Broadcom.
- **SoIC is the fastest-ramping leg.** 3D hybrid-bonding capacity goes from ~10–14K today to **~40K end-27 → ~65K wpm end-28E** (Gokul, 2026-05-27). Driver is migration of high-end AI accelerators to SoIC — NVDA Feynman, AMD MI, Google TPU V9, Trainium 4 — plus a slice for silicon-photonics/CPO integration. TSMC's new AP7 fab phases are being dedicated to SoIC while CoWoS is increasingly outsourced. ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED IN PART (Fubon primary transcript, 2026-08-03): the AP7 phases dedicated to SoIC are being SLOWED — "mainly in AP7 and to be more specific, it's in PHASE TWO AND PHASE THREE… they were supposedly to add SoIC capacities there," with TSMC prioritising CoWoS first and, as a second cause, "the CPU demand is also being pushed out." Old framing preserved here and in `## Changelog`; see the Aug 3 signals block for the full read.**
- **Substrate is the next bottleneck.** Substrate (ABF/CCL) supply can grow only ~15%/yr against >15% demand; spot ABF +30%, contract +5–10%, with first LTAs locking ~40–50% price hikes off end-25 base (Gokul/JPM Computex team — Jerry, 2026-06-11; NVDA briefing dnystedt, also flags Ajinomoto pondering 30% hike). Because EMIB-style packaging has no interposer, value that TSMC used to capture accrues directly to substrate vendors (Gokul, 2026-05-27).
- **TSMC is building packaging in Arizona.** The US buildout now contemplates ~5 CoWoS assembly/test factories alongside the wafer fabs, plus the first TSMC R&D fab outside Taiwan (TSMC former exec, 2026-04-14; [TSM page](../TSM.md)).
- **Fubon nudges CoWoS up again + sizes SoIC/CoPoS.** Fubon raises end-27 CoWoS capacity to **180kwpm (from 170k)**; +40kwpm in 2028 → **220kwpm end-28**, plus **20-30kwpm CoPoS** by end-28 (R&D line at VisEra, process complete 2H27-1H28, MP targeted 2H28; "top-priority project" for Feynman/Coupe 2.0). SoIC capacity to **45/65/90kwpm by end-FY26/27/28** — driven by silicon photonics, Broadcom TPU V9/V10 (V9 = two 3D-stacking chips, 2x N2P + 2x N3P dies; V10 = four 3D chips, all-N2, 400G SerDes), plus AWS and NVDA entering SoIC by 2028 (Fubon "Semi 2027 outlook", 2026-06-05).
- **OSAT capacity inflecting hard (corroborates value-migration).** Fubon revises ASE FoCoS up to **45kwpm end-27 (from 30k)** to serve NVDA Vera CPU (via SPIL), AMD Venice CPU, and Broadcom switch chipsets/transceivers; ASE LEAP revenue $4.2bn (FY26) → $8.3bn (FY27), >40% of ATM revenue by FY27 (Fubon, 2026-06-05). Redburn flags OSAT FY26 capex expectations up **+122% YTD**, with ASE utilization recovered to 80% (1Q26) and tightness emerging in leading-edge 2.5D (Redburn, 2026-06-15).
- **Hybrid bonding (SoIC) — Redburn: Logic > Memory.** Redburn raises BESI FY26-30 revenue 9-20% on two new HB logic legs: (1) China — Huawei "LogicFolding"/Tau-scaling logic-on-logic stacking (EUV-restricted path to density), a **cumulative 73-bonder** opportunity to 2030; (2) **CPO** — reliability gains tip scale-up networking to CPO by 2028/29, **50 bonders to 2030** for TSMC's COUPE (PIC-EIC SoIC bonding); HB rev 60% CAGR to €1.085bn FY30, PT raised €270→€350 (Redburn "BESI", 2026-06-15). BESI Investor Day 18 June flagged as the catalyst on memory-HB throughput/yield.
- **The memory-HB wildcard (bear nuance).** Redburn keeps a probability-adjusted haircut on BESI's HBM D2W bonder market (cumulative 247 units to 2030, lower share than logic): Korean makers lean on local supply (SEMES, Hanwha Semitech); and two alternatives threaten the D2W TAM — **Wafer-to-Wafer (W2W)** bonding (8x throughput; imec/EVG showed 200nm W2W at sub-40nm overlay — could bond DRAM *pairs* and halve D2W bonds, viable mainly at ≤2-high given yield penalties on ~95% DRAM yields) and **optically-networked commodity DRAM** (3-4x pin speed but ~300ns latency keeps HBM on the critical path — judged a long-dated revolution, not medium-term) (Redburn "BESI", 2026-06-15).
- **CoWoS→CoPoS the back-end inflection (Jefferies Asia, 2026-04-07):** interposer chips/package degrade fast as accelerators scale — **Blackwell ~15-16 chips per CoWoS interposer → Rubin ~12 → Rubin Ultra only ~5-7** — forcing a move to chip-on-panel-on-substrate (a square panel format). Early CoPoS machine orders already showing at **C Sun**. TSMC plans to localize the CoWoS/packaging supply chain from ~10% to ~30% share (read-through caution on Shibaura Mechatronics). Jefferies flags Shibaura's 6500W (2x throughput at +50% price) means TSMC needs *fewer* Shibaura tools (Jefferies "Asia tech", 2026-04-07).
- **CoWoS / SoIC capacity (JPM Gokul preview, 2026-04-02):** CoWoS ~**+70% in 2026 to ~1.1M wafers** (~1.5M in 2027); **3D SoIC ramps more aggressively from H2-2026, reaching ~30-40K by end-2027** with broad ASIC adoption (Trainium 4, TPU V9, some Feynman) in 2028.
