Second SoIC bonder vendor likely **Shibaura then ASMPT**; CoWoS-S (TPU/Broadcom) capacity not being expanded — TSMC pushing those customers to OSATs (Amkor, ASE) (JPM "TSMC preview/Gokul", 2026-04-02).
- **UBS raises TSMC CoWoS capacity to 130k/180k wpm end-26/27 and lifts ASE, GPTC, EMIB-T reads (Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).** UBS raises its TSMC CoWoS capacity forecast to **130k wpm by end-2026 (from 120k) and 180k wpm by end-2027 (from 150k)**; industry-wide CoWoS capacity estimated to rise from **160k wpm at end-2026 to 250k wpm by end-2027**. **ASE full-process CoWoS capacity to more than double from 20k wpm (end-2026E) to ~50k wpm (end-2027E), vs UBS's prior 40k estimate.** TSMC backend/advanced-packaging sales estimated at **~12%/15% of total sales in 2026/27E**, sustaining ~50% CAGR over the next 5 years — TSMC remains the largest CoWoS/advanced-packaging player even as Intel EMIB-T and TSMC's own CoPoS enter mass production in 2028. UBS raises ASE's PT to **NT$835 (from NT$660)** and GPTC's (test/packaging equipment) PT to **NT$5,000 (from NT$4,000)** on 33x 2027-28E PE; Intel's EMIB-T is gaining traction but seen resource/capacity-constrained in 2028, likely prioritizing internal products plus Google/MediaTek TPU v9 first (UBS/Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).
- **AMD Venice/MI450 and Nvidia Vera CoWoS demand growth quantified (UBS, 2026-07-01).** UBS estimates AMD Venice server-CPU CoWoS demand could grow **232% in 2027E** (Venice ramp + MI450/455), while Nvidia CoWoS demand grows **+57% in 2027E** on the Vera CPU ramp (**5.5mn units in 2027 vs 1.6mn in 2026**) (UBS/Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).
- **Probe-card and Japan AI-supply-chain names rally on advanced-packaging read-through (Jefferies Silk Road desk, 2026-06-30).** Probe-card names (MPI, Winway, Gallant) up ~10% in Taiwan trading tied to Fubon's July call on advanced packaging; in Japan, Sumco +11%, Ibiden +9%, Taiyo Yuden +15% on broad AI-supply-chain strength (Jefferies/Cristina Titu, 2026-06-30).
- **Preliminary 2027 CoWoS customer allocation (MS, 2026-06-23):** MS raised its **2027 TSMC CoWoS capacity assumption to 200kwpm by year-end (from 170kwpm)**, implying ~60% Y/Y growth for the global AI XPU industry. Industry-check customer breakdown: **Nvidia** is the major user — 2027 CoWoS-L consumption could reach **~910k (+~40% Y/Y)**, single-sourced for Blackwell/Rubin, with strong CoWoS-R bookings implying Vera CPU shipments nearly doubling; **Google TPU ASIC** is the #2 user — MediaTek booking of **180k CoWoS-S** implies ~3.6mn units of TPU v8t (ZebraFish, above MS's 2.5mn assumption that bakes in ABF/T-Glass substrate shortages), and **Broadcom bookings of 365k CoWoS-S** (TPU v7 Ironwood, v8i SunFish, Tomahawk 5/6 + smaller ASICs), of which ~330k to v8i SunFish implies ~3.9mn units (larger die / higher value vs MediaTek's v8t). **CPU now consuming significant 2.5D capacity** for agentic AI: Nvidia 3nm Vera CPU to **5.75mn units in 2027**, AMD 2nm Venice CPU to **6.75mn in 2027 (vs ~1.25mn in 2026)**. MS reiterates OW on TSMC; ASE (Venice CPU), KYEC and MediaTek (Top Pick) also flagged (MS Asia AI Supply Chain, 2026-06-23).
- **TSMC SOIC expansion is aggressive into 2027–28; Besi still the sole qualified hybrid bonder (Conor + Sherman, 2026-06-23).** The Asia trip's back-end takeaway is bullish for SoIC/hybrid bonding: **TSMC has a very aggressive plan to expand SOIC capacity in 2027–2028, and each 10,000-unit SOIC line requires 15–20 hybrid bonders** (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23 — Sherman). **Besi remains the *only* TSMC-qualified hybrid bonder — Shibaura still not qualified** (TSMC keeps raising the specs), confirmed every quarter (Redburn, 2026-06-23). New SOIC use cases driving the expansion: **silicon-photonics chips and Broadcom's next-generation TPU** both start using hybrid bonding. In a first, **Samsung disclosed it has a hybrid-bonding project and is willing to offer it at a premium** (for higher HBM stacks / HBM4E) — a positive read-through for Besi owners (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). Conor's net: he's been "very bearish for quite some time on Besi" but came away *bullish* on the SOIC numbers + hybrid-bonding tailwind — though buy-side expectations for both Besi and ASML "have got up quite high," so a lot is already priced in.
- **CPU packaging the new Asia supply-chain leg — AAC, Kinsus, ASPEED (Sherman/Conor, 2026-06-23).** With agentic AI driving a massive CPU shortage, the CPU-packaging supply chain becomes investable: **AAC benefits most as TSMC outsources CPU production to it — including NVIDIA's Vera and AMD's Venus**; **Kinsus benefits from Unimicron capacity constraints** (Unimicron's capacity is focused on GPU/custom-ASIC projects, leaving no room for CPUs, so NVIDIA/AMD turn to Kinsus); and **ASPEED has very high general-server exposure** (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23 — Sherman). This is the same value-migration dynamic the theme already tracks (TSMC routing CPU work to OSATs), now extended to the Vera/Venus CPU programs. (These are off-coverage Asian names with no wiki page.)
- **ASML enters packaging litho (AlphaSense, 2026-06-22):** ASML launched the **TWINSCAN XT:260 i-line scanner** (~4x legacy productivity, ~€3M/tool) for RDL patterning on advanced interposers — its first 3D-integration/adv-packaging tool; also evaluating hybrid-bonding entry (BESI consolidation logic). On the deposition/etch side, **Lam dominates the "drill and fill" TSV sequence** (Syndion deep-Si etch + SABRE 3D copper ECD), >90% est. share in high-aspect TSV etch for HBM, adv-packaging rev +~50% y/y in CY26 toward >$1B; wafer-level packaging market $6B (2025) → $11B (2028) (AlphaSense "DR ASML/LRCX primers", 2026-06-22).

- **CoWoS-vs-EMIB-T becomes the market's glass/panel narrative (MS Amir Amerian TMT Spec Sales, 2026-06-30).** MS sales flags that INTC's **EMIB-T has performance/cost (warpage) advantages over TSMC CoWoS at very large chips (>9.7 reticle-size)** on higher sample yields — driving the glass/panel rally (Innolux +300% YTD, GLW rally) on the logic that TSMC would then have to adopt glass/panel packaging (CoPoS) more aggressively to compete. Caveats: still early/speculative, original EMIB failed, and Howard Kao notes substrate makers worry EMIB-T production yields stay low. This is the same >90-kÅ / large-package threshold MS's Charlie Chan flagged (2026-04-14). (MS Sales / Amir Amerian, 2026-06-30)
- **Customer-side rebuttal — CoWoS still the 'gold standard' (AlphaSense former-AMD-director expert call, 2026-06-30).** Views TSMC CoWoS as unmatched on reliability/performance/partnership, hard to displace near-to-medium term, and is **skeptical Intel can compete at scale in advanced packaging until it lands significant external customers** — a demand-side counter to the EMIB-T blue-sky. (AlphaSense / former AMD director, 2026-06-30)
- **TSMC fast-tracking CoPoS (panel-level packaging) — MP as early as 2029 (DIGITIMES / @QQ_Timmy, 2026-06-30).** Supply chain reportedly under a gag order; CoPoS uses large square glass panels vs round wafers for **5-6x more chip output**; verification tools already moved into VisEra Longtan, ~30 equipment vendors on the initial list; 2026 validation / 2027 trial / 2H28 possible ramp / **MP ~2029** (vs prior 2030 expectation), Chairman Wei says scale still needs 2-3 yrs. Pulls the CoPoS timeline in vs the 2029-30 framing elsewhere on this page. (DIGITIMES / @QQ_Timmy, 2026-06-30)
- **TSMC gets paid twice across a single HBM unit's journey — the structural rationale for why TSMC protects CoWoS even as it outsources volume (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall", 2025-08-11).** The supply-chain flow behind HBM integration: a **base-die designer** (Nvidia, Broadcom, or a hyperscaler) hands the design to **TSMC, which fabricates the base-die wafer (N12/N3)**; that wafer is then **consigned to the HBM manufacturer** (SK Hynix/Samsung/Micron) for TSV formation, DRAM-die stacking, and packaging; the finished HBM stack ships back to the accelerator designer, which **consigns it to TSMC a second time** for CoWoS packaging alongside the logic die (p.41-42). Net: **TSMC is paid twice across one HBM unit's journey** — once for the base-die wafer, once for the interposer packaging — a structural rent that helps explain why TSMC has historically been reluctant to fully cede CoWoS share to OSATs even while routing overflow volume to ASE/Amkor for capacity reasons. TSMC is acknowledged as the **advanced-packaging leader for HBM integration**, but memory makers retain real leverage via **TSV-formation and DRAM-stacking IP** that TSMC does not itself possess (p.43) — the packaging-side counterpart to the "TSMC fabricates the base die because memory makers mostly lack leading-edge logic capability" framing on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md).
- **Why hybrid-bonding adoption in HBM should be expected to run slow — the TSMC advanced-logic precedent (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall", 2025-08-11).** SemiAnalysis argues the **routing-density case for CoWoS itself is unambiguous**: HBM3E-class integration requires routing **>1,000 wires between the XPU and HBM**, a density that exceeds what conventional PCB or substrate technology can support, which is why interposer-based CoWoS became mandatory rather than optional (p.3). On the timeline for the *next* packaging transition (hybrid bonding), the report reasons by analogy to **TSMC's own adoption curve for hybrid bonding in advanced logic** — which took years to move from pilot to volume — to argue that **HBM's hybrid-bonding adoption will likewise be slow** (p.13). This is a useful counter-anchor to the more bullish 2026-06-23 HB-timeline reads elsewhere on this page (Redburn's Conor/Sherman checks have Besi already the sole TSMC-qualified bonder with SOIC lines expanding aggressively into 2027-28): the SemiAnalysis precedent-based argument implies the *memory-side* hybrid-bonding leg (as opposed to the logic/SoIC leg) could lag the more optimistic capacity numbers, consistent with the W2W/optical-DRAM-as-alternative-path hedge Redburn separately flags on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md).
- **Nomura — "Is the cycle over?": TSMC turns aggressive on CoW, but the bottleneck shifts to WoS + the elephants-vs-grass allocation squeeze (Aaron Jeng, 2026-06-30).** Nomura now expects TSMC to *target* CoWoS capacity of **2,000kpcs (2M wafers) in 2027F, from 1,100kpcs in 2026F**, and estimates it would need **2,500-3,500kpcs of CoWoS *output* by 2029F** to hit management's "high-50%" AI-revenue CAGR (2024-29E). Its contrarian tech-team view: **"WoS" (wafer-on-substrate, *not* controlled by TSMC) and many small components very likely become a bigger bottleneck than "CoW" (controlled by TSMC) into 2027F** — so Nomura only *models* **1,800kpcs of CoWoS output in 2028F** (below TSMC's 2,000kpc target), with "profound implications for different GPU/ASIC vendors." Flags an **unprecedented component supply-mismatch in 2H26F worsening into 2027F** — PCB/CCL, IC substrate, high-end MLCC capacitors, PMIC and optical all already tight, aggravated when Rubin + Trainium 3 ramp from 2H26. **2027F CoWoS allocation (many calls contrarian):** (1) CoW allocation matters *less* than whether GPU/ASIC vendors can secure substrate/CCL/capacitors; (2) **Google TPU CoWoS share rises to ~26% in 2027 (from ~23% in 2026)**, nearly double y/y in absolute growth on Gemini ecosystem gains; (3) **NVDA share slides to <55% in 2027F** (from a targeted ~60%) as TPU squeezes it; (4) **AMD** CoWoS share only marginally improves y/y and **AWS** share may even fall y/y in 2027F; (5) within TPU, **MediaTek's share could more than double to 30%+ in 2027F (from ~15% in 2026)**. Raises TSMC AI-rev growth to **77%/67% for 2026F/27F** (from 69%/24%). On the tech roadmap: TSMC launched its **14x-reticle CoWoS roadmap (2028E)** at the April NA Tech Symposium (vs a prior 9.5x-reticle 2027E plan), and **EMIB-T is "potentially the biggest threat" to TSMC advanced packaging** — so **SoIC and CoPoS are "two equally critical technologies"** to stay ahead. If NVDA Feynman fully migrates to CoPoS in 2029F, TSMC would need **700-800kpcs of CoPoS capacity by 2029F** (~50% of 2029F CoWoS would then need new customers). Feynman is expected to be the **first-ever GPU-on-GPU SoIC stack**, whose bigger footprint + high TDP starts driving **silicon-carbide (SiC) carrier** adoption (read-through to GWC), and "SoIC capacity demand skyrockets through 2028-29F." OSATs (ASE) benefit from TSMC's WoS outsourcing *and* their own CoWoS-like full process for CPUs. Buys: TSMC, ASE (WoS+CoW), ASPEED (CPU), MediaTek (TPU), GWC (SiC/Feynman), KYEC (AI-chip test), plus BESI (CPO + GPU-on-GPU SoIC) and Soitec (SOI wafer for CPO) (Nomura "Asia AI Semi & Server — Is the cycle over?", 2026-06-30).
- **Jefferies — "Revisiting on Pullback": Broadcom TPU roadmap migrating to SoIC; EMIB still weak for large 3D-stacked XPUs (Blayne Curtis, 2026-06-29).** AVGO-focused note with a useful per-program packaging map (Fig. 1, "AVGO ASICs by Customer"): **Google TPU v9 "Pumafish" (N2/3, HBM4) and v9x "Tigerfish" (N2, HBM4) both move to SoIC**, as does **TPU 10i (N2, HBM4)** — confirming SoIC adoption at the leading TPU generations; TPU v8t "Zebrafish" (MTK) and v8i "Sunfish" stay **CoWoS-S**; Meta MTIA 400/450/500, OpenAI V2, Softbank Gen2 are **CoWoS-L**; OpenAI Jalapeno is **CoWoS-S** (N3). **v9 = 3D-stacked logic (N2/3) + 400G SerDes (co-packaged copper)**, launching ~April 27; a v10 in late-C27 likely features **8x logic die (4 stacked on 4)**. On the EMIB threat, Jefferies is more cautious than the bulls: **Intel EMIB "has proven capable for smaller CPUs but remains a challenge for the larger XPU-style chips, particularly 3D stacked die."** MTK's Zebrafish volumes seen at only **~60k in C26** (well short of MTK-analyst forecasts of 400-450k C26 / 2-2.5M C27). OpenAI's Jalapeno (first "Intelligence Processor" XPU with AVGO): single ~740mm² near-reticle compute die + smaller I/O die, **6x 12-Hi HBM3e = 216GB**, 3nm, ~$15k ASP, ships end-C26 with volume in 2H27 (Jefferies "Revisiting on Pullback: TPU v9 on Track", 2026-06-29).
- **MediaTek TPU v9 design complexity signals escalating 2.5D/3D packaging intensity across the custom-ASIC roadmap (JPM, 2026-07-01).** JPM's Gokul Hariharan flags MediaTek TPU v9's design — **4 N2 compute dies + 4 I/O dies + 4 memory I/O dies** — versus v8t's single N3 compute die + single I/O die, underscoring how much packaging intensity is rising generation-over-generation; MediaTek's v9 unit upside is partly contingent on **Intel's EMIB-T packaging yield progress** (JPM/Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-01).
- **TSMC raising capex to match AI demand; DC-AI CAGR lifted to 69% (JPM/Gokul, 2026-07-06).** In its 2Q26 preview ("Stronger GMs and aggressive capacity plans," Overweight, PT NT$3,100), JPM raises TSMC's **FY26/27/28 capex to $58bn/$78bn/$84bn** and lifts its **Datacenter-AI revenue CAGR to 69% (2024-29E) from 59%** (vs TSMC's mid-to-high-50s guide), on a stronger accelerator build + faster AI-CPU growth — read-through to sustained CoWoS/SoIC and advanced-packaging capacity adds through 2027-29 (JPM / Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-06). See [../TSM.md](../TSM.md).
- **MS TSMC preview + CoWoS update by segment (Charlie Chan, 2026-07-06).** MS published a **58-page "TSMC preview and CoWoS update — CPU, GPU, ASIC, Optical, China AI Compute"** (Industry View Attractive) into the July 16 2Q26 call, detailing CoWoS demand across CPU/GPU/ASIC/optical segments — a constructive preview extending the CoWoS-ramp-by-customer framing already tracked on this page (MS / Charlie Chan, 2026-07-06).
- **Citi: tight advanced-packaging/CoWoS + ABF favours design-service leaders (Laura Chen, 2026-07-06).** Citi flags the supply chain — **foundry / advanced packaging / HBM / ABF — remaining tight**, and prefers **MediaTek & GUC** for AI-ASIC growth into 2027; a demand read-through supportive of CoWoS/advanced-packaging pull and the design-service layer (Citi / Laura Chen, 2026-07-06).
- **Goldman: advanced packaging (2.5D / HDFO) a secular growth driver for AMAT, tied to AI/HPC (James Schneider, 2026-07-05).** In its 2Q semi preview, GS calls out **advanced packaging as a secular growth driver for Applied Materials** — management guiding **CY26 advanced-packaging revenue growth of >50%** — as part of the case for raising AMAT to a Buy PT $645 (from $520). GS frames the backdrop as tight: **DRAM fabs running near full utilization with advanced-packaging capacity remaining tight**, and reads 2.5D / HDFO momentum tied to AI/HPC as a key driver for OSAT Amkor (Neutral, PT $65) — investor focus on AI/HPC 2.5D customer concentration + the Arizona ramp (Goldman Sachs "2Q Preview", 2026-07-05). _(WFE-name PT raises AMAT/LRCX/TER/KLAC tracked on [semicap-wfe.md](semicap-wfe.md).)_
- **Qualcomm's HBC approach shifts (not eliminates) the packaging bottleneck (Vik's Newsletter, 2026-07-01).** Qualcomm's High Bandwidth Compute (HBC) approach — stacking DRAM directly on the compute die — bypasses CoWoS, but shifts the packaging bottleneck to **3D-stacking technology** rather than eliminating it (Vik's Newsletter/Vikram Sekar, 2026-07-01).
- **Fubon 2Q26 TSMC preview — advanced packaging a worsening FY27 bottleneck; Google TPU V9 EMIB-T likely falls back to TSMC 2028 CoWoS (Fubon "TSMC — Expecting GM upside going forward", 2026-07-08).** Fubon keeps CoWoS expansion "in the spotlight": it now models **CoWoS ~180kwpm by end-FY27** (AP7/AP8 "very full," designed to support more than the CoWoS expansion), with TSMC considering restructuring old AP fabs for further FY28 expansion → **total CoWoS 210-220kwpm by end-2028**. It frames **advanced packaging (with N2/N3 wafers) as a worsening supply-chain bottleneck in FY27**, part of the widening 15-20% FY27 supply/demand gap. On the second-source debate: Fubon's checks say **Google initially planned to use Intel EMIB-T for TPU V9 production in late-2027/2028** (a CoWoS-relief / packaging-diversification datapoint) and **asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to 95%+ before production** — but Fubon judges that "very challenging to achieve by mid-2027," concluding Google will "at last rely on TSMC's [2028 CoWoS] expansion plan." Net for the theme: EMIB-T is a credible second-source *intent* on a marquee program, but the yield gate keeps CoWoS the likely FY28 fallback — consistent with the EMIB 60-70% vs CoWoS 95%+ yield framing already tracked (Fubon, 2026-06-05) and the two-sided EMIB-T-vs-CoWoS debate in Key debates below (Fubon, 2026-07-08). See [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md), [../GOOG.md](../GOOG.md).
- **Capstone expert call: $300bn WFE spending call "completely unrealistic" (2026-06-30).** A former Samsung EVP, now a photonics-startup CEO, on a Capstone expert call calls the market's $300bn WFE spending target "completely unrealistic" — he expects growth past $200bn but not to the advertised highs, citing fab-capacity and power constraints; relevant backdrop for the packaging/equipment capex debate tracked on this page (Capstone expert call/former Samsung EVP, 2026-06-30).

## Reference framework — **TSMC COUPE**: the packaging platform that gates co-packaged optics (SemiAnalysis CPO Book, published 2026-01-01)

⚠️ **STANDING BACKGROUND, NOT NEWS — the source is a reference book published 2026-01-01, ~7 months stale as of 2026-08-13, and the dated signals above GOVERN on capacity and timing.** Filed here because it is the structural answer to a question this page keeps hitting from the outside (Susquehanna's *"COUPE PIC capacity <0.5K wpm today → only ~5-10K wpm by YE27, FAU-constrained, shipments capped <0.5M units/qtr"*, 2026-07-11): **why CPO is a packaging problem before it is an optics problem.** The full optical/CPO story stays on [optical-cpo](optical-cpo.md); only the packaging and integration layer is extracted here. (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel, Daniel Nishball, Myron Xie and 3 others, "Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) Book: Scaling with Light for the Next Wave of Interconnect", 2026-01-01) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Co_Packaged_Optics_CPO_Scaling_with_Light_for_the_Next_Wave_of_Interconnect.html)

⚠️ **PROVENANCE, so this is not double-counted later: the article was FIRST INGESTED 2026-06-22 and is already cited from [../AVGO.md](../AVGO.md), [../INTC.md](../INTC.md), [../MRVL.md](../MRVL.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md) and [../TSM.md](../TSM.md) — it had simply never been transcribed onto this theme page. It sits on disk under TWO SLUGS: the canonical one linked above and a URL-derived duplicate, [`newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html`](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html). ONE SOURCE, TWO LINKS — not a second publication. And note it is the OLDEST of several dated SemiAnalysis CPO positions (see [optical-cpo](optical-cpo.md) for the full chain); it is not SemiAnalysis's current view on the CPO calendar.**

- **🔴 THE FRAMING LINE, and it is the reason CPO belongs on this page at all: *"'Co-packaged optics', is fundamentally a PACKAGING AND ASSEMBLY CHALLENGE."*** An optical engine is a **PIC** (photonic IC — modulators, waveguides, photodetectors) bonded to an **EIC** (driver, TIA, control logic). Monolithic PIC+EIC on one wafer is the elegant route but *"monolithic processes stop at geometries below around 35nm as photonics processes cannot scale like traditional CMOS… this makes monolithic integration a DEALBREAKER for scaling."* Heterogeneous integration therefore wins by default, and within it *"**3D integration is the ONLY way to reach the performance goals of CPO** from a bandwidth and power perspective"* because trace length drives parasitics (CPO Book, 2026-01-01).
- **COUPE = "Compact Universal Photonic Engine," and [[TSM]] has taken a domain where it was historically weak.** *"TSMC is racing ahead as the foundry partner of choice for the next generation of OEs for both the fabless giants and startups"* — previously *"TSMC had a limited presence in silicon photonics, where Global Foundries and Tower Semi were the preferred foundry partners."* The stated reason it won anyway: leading-edge CMOS for the EIC **plus** packaging — *"TSMC is the **only foundry that has successfully demonstrated die-to-wafer hybrid bonding capabilities at reasonable scale**, having shipped various [[AMD]] hybrid bonded chips in volume. Hybrid bonding is a more performant approach to bond the PIC and EIC, though it does come with a significantly higher cost. **Intel is working to develop a similar capability but has faced significant challenges in pioneering this technology**"* (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). ➜ **Read alongside this page's EMIB-T-vs-CoWoS debate: the same hybrid-bonding capability gap that decides the CPO platform is the one contested on the logic side.**
- **🔴 THE LOCK-IN, WHICH IS THE MOST COMMERCIALLY IMPORTANT SENTENCE IN THE BOOK FOR THIS PAGE: *"Like other major players, TSMC aims to capture as much of the value chain as possible. **By adopting TSMC's COUPE solution, customers effectively commit to using TSMC-manufactured PICs, as TSMC does not package SiPho wafers from other foundries**. Many CPO focused companies have indeed pivoted decisively towards making TSMC's COUPE as part of their go to market solution."*** ➜ **COUPE is not a packaging service bought à la carte — it forecloses the PIC foundry decision. Every COUPE design win is simultaneously a front-end win TSMC would not otherwise have had in silicon photonics, and it is a structural reason to expect TSMC's SiPho share to converge on its packaging share rather than the reverse.** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01)
- **The stack, noded:** **EIC on N7** (high-speed modulator drivers, TIAs, plus *"heater controllers to enable functions such as wavelength stabilization"*); **PIC on SOI N65**, with TSMC supplying photonic layout/verification and RF/noise/multi-wavelength simulation; **EIC and PIC bonded with the TSMC-SoIC-bond process** — bumpless, *"the shortest trace length possible without being monolithic and therefore the most performant possible way to heterogeneously integrate the EIC and PIC. **At iso-power, SoIC based OEs offer more than 23x the bandwidth density of an OE integrated with bumps**"* (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). 🔴 **This is the direct link to the SoIC capacity item on this page (Aug 2: the first DOWNWARD revision of the cycle, and it was SoIC not CoWoS). CPO endpoint volume is gated by SoIC hybrid-bonding capacity, not by CoWoS — so a SoIC cut and a CPO push-out are the SAME datapoint seen from two ends.**
- **What COUPE covers as a platform:** µLens design and wafer-/chip-level micro-lens integration; optical I/O path simulation across **mirrors, µLenses, grating couplers and reflectors**; 3D floorplanning with **SoIC-X / TDV / C4 bump layout**; interface physical checking and high-frequency channel extraction; and *"a complete PDK and EDA workflow."* **Coupling: one common bumpless EIC-on-PIC stack serves both grating (GC) and edge (EC) coupling — COUPE-GC adds a silicon lens on a 770µm Si-carrier plus a metal reflector under the grating coupler, wafer-on-wafer bonded to a chip-on-wafer wafer; COUPE-EC adds an EC facet.** ⚠️ **TSMC *"clearly has a higher preference for GC,"* as does [[NVDA]] (2D fiber density, smaller footprint, easier manufacture, simpler wafer-level test) — at the cost of higher optical loss and narrower optical bandwidth than edge coupling** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01).
- **🔴 THE ROADMAP IS A SUBSTRATE-THEN-INTERPOSER STORY, and the interposer leg is the one that consumes this page's capacity: *"The first iterations of COUPE will be optical engines on the SUBSTRATE with the ultimate goal being able to put the OEs on the INTERPOSER. The interposer offers far more I/O density, therefore enabling greater bandwidth between the OEs and the ASIC PHYs, with the possibility of individual OEs having up to 12.8Tbit/s of bandwidth each, translating to approximately 4 Tbit/s/mm. **The challenge for integrating the interposer is scaling the interposer size (which is more expensive than the package substrate) to accommodate the OE**."*** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01) ➜ **Interposer-area demand per CPO package is a second-order driver this page's CoWoS wpm ladders do not separate out.**
- **🔴 THE PACKAGE ARITHMETIC — the numbers that make CPO a CoWoS line item.** For [[NVDA]]'s Spectrum-X Photonics switch ASIC, **the substrate measures 110mm × 110mm, against the Blackwell package at 70mm × 76mm — *"a very big chip in its own right."*** Assembly sequence and yield: ***"36 KNOWN GOOD OEs need to be flip-chip bonded onto the substrate FIRST – before bonding the interposer module for the 'on Substrate' step to complete the CoWoS assembly,"*** with warpage *"more pronounced as interposer/substrate size scales"* (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). **And the yield tell is disclosed in the product itself: of the 36 OEs in a 102.4T Spectrum-X package, *"only 32 optical engines are active, with the additional 4 being there for REDUNDANCY purposes in case an OE fails. This is due to the OEs being soldered on the substrate, so they are not easily replaceable."*** ⚠️ **An 11% designed-in redundancy overhead on the most expensive components in the package is a live yield admission — and it is incremental substrate and assembly demand per unit shipped.**
- **🔴 [[AVGO]] IS BEING FORCED ONTO COUPE, AND THE REASON IS A HARD PHYSICAL CEILING ON ITS INCUMBENT PACKAGE.** Bailly used **fan-out wafer-level packaging (FOWLP) developed with SPIL**, chosen because it was *"already proven in the mobile handset market and widely supported by OSATs."* But *"the FOWLP approach **doesn't allow scaling beyond 100G per lane due to excessive parasitic capacitance**, as electrical signals must pass through the Through-Mold Vias (TMV) to get to the EIC. To maintain a competitive roadmap, Broadcom must transition to COUPE."* ⚠️ **The switching cost is real and under-appreciated: *"Broadcom's previous generations have used edge coupling as well as MZMs – both of these choices were simpler from an implementation standpoint, but also less scalable… COUPE is biased towards grating coupling and MRMs which is a dramatic change from their existing approach. Despite Broadcom having the most CPO experience, this change in technical approach means that **Broadcom must essentially start fresh on some aspects of their technology**."*** ➜ **"Most CPO experience" and "best positioned on the next node" are not the same claim. Broadcom has committed COUPE to *"its future switch AND customer accelerator roadmaps"* — i.e. this lands inside the custom-ASIC programs tracked on [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md).** (all CPO Book, 2026-01-01)
- **The CoWoS-L link, from Broadcom's own Hot Chips 2024 disclosure:** an experimental package integrating a **6.4 Tbit/s OE with one logic die, two HBM stacks and a SerDes tile**, HBM fanned out east/west to leave room for two OEs — and *"by moving from **CoWoS-S to CoWoS-L**, you move to substrates that exceed **100mm on an edge**. As such, they will be able to accommodate up to **four optical engines and achieve 51.2 Tbit/s** of bandwidth"* (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). ➜ **A concrete, non-HBM reason for the CoWoS-S → CoWoS-L mix shift this page tracks.**
- **[[INTC]]'s parallel path, for the EMIB-vs-CoWoS scoreboard:** 2023 package-to-package electrical I/O → **2024 gen-1 CPO with direct fiber attach** (OFC 2024: a 4 Tbit/s bidirectional Optical Compute Interconnect chiplet co-packaged with a concept Xeon, 64 lanes × 32 Gbit/s, ~5 pJ/bit) → **2025 gen-2 replacing fiber pigtails with a detachable glass optical bridge carrying embedded 3D waveguides and mechanical alignment features** → **2027 target: 3D-integrated photonics via vertical expanded beam coupling** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). ⚠️ **Note the glass-waveguide bridge is the same materials vector as the [[GLW]] Glass Bridge / GlassWorks item already on this page — two independent attacks on TSMC's optical-packaging position, both glass-based.**
- **Where the OSAT layer sits, and it is the same names this page already tracks:** *"OSAT providers will focus on the back-end processes: including OE packaging, OE testing, and system packaging (laser and coupler integration & testing). **ASE/SPIL (3711.TW), Amkor, and Shunsin (6451.TW) are primary providers** of such solutions. Among them, **ASE stands out as a key supplier in Nvidia's supply chain, including involvement in the FUTURE RUBIN-RACK CPO SYSTEMS, while Shunsin maintains close ties with Broadcom**."* Also named: **Fabrinet** (long the assembler of NVDA's in-house optical modules, now building OE packaging/test/system assembly, and a candidate for Broadcom's CPO box assembly alongside Micas and Foxconn), **TFC Optical (300394.SH)** — building advanced-packaging capacity in Suzhou — and **Foxconn** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01). ➜ **Corroborates the ASE-privileged-access-to-COUPE framing already on this page (Jul 31), and dates ASE's CPO revenue to the Rubin rack rather than to the scale-out switches.**
- **The competitive floor for the non-TSMC foundries, stated plainly: *"Global Foundries and Tower are the foundries who have strong SiPho capabilities, but their LACK OF LEADING EDGE CMOS AND ADVANCED PACKAGING constrain their ability to provide the higher bandwidth OEs of the future."*** (CPO Book, 2026-01-01) ⚠️ **Same structural verdict this page reaches on CoWoS alternatives, arrived at from the photonics side: SiPho process capability is necessary and not sufficient; the packaging is the moat.**

## Key debates
- **Bull / where the money is:** Shortage is "the name of the game" and persists into 2027+ across 3nm wafers, memory and advanced packaging (Gokul, Computex, 2026-06-11). The cleanest expressions are the *picks-and-shovels* layers that capture spillover regardless of who wins the GPU war — OSATs (ASE/KSE), bonder/inspection equipment (BESI for hybrid bonding, plus AMAT/KLAC on advanced-packaging tools), and substrate (Unimicron, Japanese vendors Ibiden/Shinko). KSE and the OSATs were "quite constructive" on raising capex; clean-room construction itself is now a constraint (Gokul, 2026-06-11).
- **Bear / what could break it:** Value migration away from TSMC is the structural risk to the capacity owner — CoWoS-R/-S increasingly farmed to ASE/Amkor, and Intel EMIB as a genuine second source. Demand-double-counting also lurks: customers' forecasts historically sum to multiples of the real end market, which TSMC itself discounts (former exec, 2026-04-14). And specific programs slip — Rubin cut to ~2.0–2.2M units this year on HBM4 issues (from 2.8–2.9M); AMD MI450 CoWoS-L pulled down for 2026 on the 2nm ramp delay before a strong 2027 (Gokul, 2026-04-13 / 2026-05-27).
- **Intel packaging as trojan horse.** EMIB (and EMIB-T for the rumored 4-die Rubin Ultra SKU) is the credible non-TSMC route — SK Hynix is doing joint 2.5D/EMIB R&D with Intel amid the CoWoS crunch (NVDA briefing jukan05/dnystedt, 2026-05-11/12). The flagship test case is **Google TPU V9 "Puma Fish" (MediaTek)**: plan A is Intel "EMEM"/EMIB with ~25–28 silicon bridges (yield unproven at that complexity), plan B is TSMC CoWoS-L; Google has pre-paid Intel to reserve 2028 substrate capacity, decision expected late-26/early-27 (Gokul, 2026-05-27). One bull case sizes Feynman I/O-die packaging at ~$4.8B with "Intel w/ EMIB the only game in town" if 4-die wins (NVDA briefing bubbleboi). Counter-view: Jeff Pu argues 3DIC, not CoPoS/4-die, breaks the area limit (NVDA briefing, 2026-05-16); JPM frames the Intel piece as "mostly packaging, not TSMC replacement" (Samik Chatterjee, 2026-06-09). **Update 2026-09-09:** the CFO rejected the on-ramp reading again, in public — *"we don't agree… ASE would have been a wafer manufacturer for many years"* — and said a customer using a competitor's packaging is fine because *"either one enables our wafer business"* (Huang (CFO) @ GS Communacopia fireside, 2026-09-09 — Capstone Notion transcript; see the 09-09/10 block at the top of this page).
- **CoPoS pushed out.** Panel-level CoPoS now looks like 2029–2030, not 2028 — TSMC keeps extending CoWoS (14X reticle in 2028; AMD MI500 already targeting ~14–15X). NVDA Feynman on CoPoS is "touch and go" (Gokul, 2026-05-27). ~60–70% of CoPoS equipment is shareable with CoWoS; the delta is glass/TGV/panel tooling.
- **The three-way CoWoS-vs-EMIB-T-vs-Glass-Core-Substrate debate gets a fourth angle — Corning's optical route (2026-06-29/30 updates).** The debate is no longer just CoWoS-vs-EMIB-T: Morgan Stanley (Amir Amerian sales, with Derrick Yang/Charlie Chan/Meta Marshall) reports TSMC discussed **Glass Core Substrate** technology on June 11 in Japan, naming **Ibiden and Innolux as development partners** — but Derrick Yang stresses this signals proof-of-concept completion only, not a mass-production commitment. Adoption timing is seen as more likely **2H28** (vs the market's assumed "CoPoS" 2029 timeline). The technology would replace organic CCL substrate with glass + through-glass-vias (TGV), aimed at closing TSMC's performance gap vs Intel's EMIB-T for very large chips — positive for glass supplier **GLW** and panel makers **Innolux/BOE**, negative for CCL/T-glass suppliers (**Nittobo**). Notably, **BOE** (OW-rated by MS) is pursuing a different strategy — building the entire Glass Core + Substrate stack itself, targeting GPU/ASIC makers beyond the China ecosystem, explicitly to pressure TSMC into adopting BOE as a supplier alongside Ibiden/Innolux (MS/Amir Amerian, 2026-06-30). Separately, **Susquehanna (Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-06-29)** flags Corning's new **Glass Bridge optical connector** (30μm+ core pitch, <2dB coupling loss) and "GlassWorks AI" end-to-end optical platform as positioning **GLW as an alternative supplier to TSMC's CoWoS-L (COUPE) platform** for CPO commercialization — i.e., a non-TSMC-dependent path for AI-era advanced packaging/optical integration, adding a fourth competitive vector (optical/CPO-native packaging) alongside CoWoS, EMIB-T, and Glass Core Substrate. See the "Customer-side rebuttal" item in State of play (AlphaSense/former AMD director, 2026-06-30) for the demand-side pushback on the whole displacement thesis.

## Management commentary — cross-company evolution (recent quarters)

| Company | CoWoS / AP capacity or allocation | SoIC / 3D hybrid bonding | Packaging as binding constraint | Value migration (OSAT / EMIB / substrate) | AI-mix stakes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [TSM](../TSM.md) | In-house CoWoS ~75–80k→~120k wpm end-26, →180k end-27 ↑; Nomura: *targets* 2,000kpcs '27F (from 1,100kpcs '26F), needs 2.5–3.5M output by '29F ↑ | SoIC 45/65/90k wpm end-26/27/28; AP7 dedicated ↑; Nomura: SoIC demand "skyrockets" '28-29F ↑ | "Very tight" wafers + back-end into 2027 →; Nomura: bottleneck shifting to WoS/substrate/components '27F → | Routing Venice/networking to ASE; Amkor US CoWoS ↑; WoS outsourced to OSATs (Nomura) ↑ | AI accel high-teens%→higher-50s% CAGR '24–29 ↑; Nomura AI-rev +77%/67% '26/27F ↑ |
| [NVDA](../NVDA.md) | ~2/3→>70%→~50%+ of TSMC CoWoS; Nomura: share slides to <55% '27F (targets ~60%) squeezed by TPU ↓; Rubin cut to ~2.0–2.2M units ↓ | Feynman to SoIC/A16 in 2028 ↑; Nomura: Feynman = first GPU-on-GPU SoIC stack, drives SiC carrier ↑ | Gated by packaging not demand; "supply-constrained" → | Vera CoWoS-R farmed to Amkor/ASE ↑ | DC rev +92% y/y; networking $15B, ~3x ↑ |
| [AVGO](../AVGO.md) | CoWoS lifted 187k→231k '26; 2027 share trimmed to ~24–25% ↓ | V9/V10 migrate to SoIC; Jefferies: v9 "Pumafish"/v9x/10i all SoIC (N2/3, HBM4), v8t/v8i stay CoWoS-S ↑; V9 MP pushed to late-3Q27 ↓ | Back-end the FY27 supply constraint → | Networking processors→ASE FoCoS (~10–15%) ↑ | AI semis $3.7B→$10.8B (+143% y/y) ↑ |
| [AMD](../AMD.md) | '26 CoWoS cut ~90k→~77k on MI450 slip; '27 raised to ~120k ↑ | V-Cache memory-on-logic HB broadening to ASICs ↑ | HBM/CoWoS-L tightness gates MI450 ramp → | Venice CPU →ASE FoCoS (256 cores, N2) ↑ | DC rev +57% y/y; AI rev $14–15B→$30–40B+ '27 ↑ |
| [BESI](../BESI.md) | n/a — equipment vendor; >150 cumulative HB systems ↑ | HB the core story; 20 customers, units >2x q/q ↑ | Orders €270m (+105% y/y) on HB; AI ~50% of '25 ↑ | Sells the bonders enabling OSAT/CoWoS/CPO offload ↑ | AI ~50% of orders; HBM-for-HB 2027 (HBM4E→5) → |
| [MEDIATEK](../MEDIATEK.md) | TPU V8t ~2.5–3M units '27 (~100k CoWoS wafers, ~5%) ↑; Nomura: MTK share of TPU could >2x to 30%+ '27F (from ~15% '26) ↑; Jefferies: Zebrafish only ~60k C26 ↓ | V9 moves to N2 compute dies / SoIC path ↑ | 2026 TPU gated by CoWoS; loosens '27 → | V9 packaging EMIB-T vs TSMC CoWoS-L undecided → | DC ASIC >$1B '26 → ~30% rev '27, ~60% '28 ↑ |

**Read:** Every consumer is now packaging-gated rather than demand-gated — capacity numbers keep ratcheting up (TSM, AMD '27, AVGO '26) even as specific programs slip (Rubin, MI450, AVGO V9), and the consistent management tell is value migrating off TSMC to OSAT FoCoS / SoIC / EMIB, which is exactly where BESI (bonders) and the OSAT layer capture the spillover. Two 2026-06-30 reads sharpen the '27 picture: Nomura argues TSMC has turned aggressive on CoW (targeting ~2M wafers '27F) precisely to defend against EMIB-T, but that the *real* '27 bottleneck migrates one layer further out to WoS/substrate/CCL/capacitors (not TSMC-controlled) — and the allocation fight becomes "elephants vs grass," with TPU (Google, MediaTek-led) taking share while NVDA's own share slips below 55%. Jefferies corroborates the SoIC migration from the customer side — Broadcom's leading TPU generations (v9/v9x/10i) all move to SoIC while Intel EMIB "remains a challenge" for large 3D-stacked XPUs — reinforcing that the SoIC/hybrid-bonding leg (BESI) is where the incremental content accrues.

_Source: member-company earnings calls; see each company page for quarter-by-quarter detail._

The likeliest reconciliation is exit-rate vs full-year-average, but **neither document states its basis**. ⛔ Do NOT chain 325k onto 270k to derive a growth rate, and ~230k is a DERIVED figure, not an MS forecast. 🔬 Settled by Chan's own note stating whether 325kwpm is an exit rate or an average.** 🔴 **THE ARGUMENT BEHIND THE NUMBER, AND IT IS TESTABLE: Chan publishes it to defend 2028 against a decelerating backdrop — MS's own Brian Nowak has **2028 CSP capex at just +12% Y/Y** and the market expects moderate 2028 memory-price growth, yet Chan argues *"Semi units and all the related content can still grow meaningfully."* **Packaging units +50% against house capex +12% requires packaging intensity per capex dollar to rise sharply — two MS numbers that are hard to hold at once.** Carry MS 2028 CSP capex +12% Y/Y as a mark in its own right.** ⚠️ **CONFIDENCE CAVEAT, FROM THE ANALYST'S OWN STANDARD: Chan had never published a 2-year-out CoWoS estimate because *"CoWoS equipment lead times are generally ~6-9mo"* and he preferred to wait for visibility. A 2028 print made in Sept-2026 sits outside that window — his most speculative CoWoS figure, not his most confident.** 🆕 **NET-NEW MECHANISM, AND IT INVERTS THE USUAL READ OF A YIELD PROBLEM: EMIB-T's **initial substrate yields are likely LOWER than CoWoS, therefore consuming MORE substrate per good die** — so an EMIB-T ramp is ABF-substrate-positive precisely to the extent it struggles; and it *"likely pushes TSMC to accelerate Glass Core and CoPoS."* Named beneficiaries: Ibiden, Unimicron, Kinsus. ⚠️ Sales-desk opinion, not research.** 🆕 **ADJACENT LAYERS, LOGGED NOT ADOPTED: MLCC 2025-31 market CAGR **raised +14% → +20%** with **2027-28 ASP growth +18%/+22%** (MS Japan · Shoji Sato), on a Murata strategy shift toward high-end capacity focus rather than like-for-like price hikes, read as a 2017-18 analogue; MS Greater China initiated 4 Taiwan CCL/PCB names on AI-server content growth; and GCE (2368) +8.7% on reports of **~20% PCB layer-count increases and M8/M9 spec upgrades** from AI ASIC and 1.6T transceivers (Jefferies Silk Road sales, 2026-09-23, trade-press sourced).** ⚠️ **The note's named longs (incl. [[ADVANTEST]] and [[SKHYNIX]]) are SALES-DESK positioning, not ratings — nothing on any company page is superseded.**
- **🔴🔴 2026-09-24 (/run-inbox, Work Unit E — 2 sources: Rothschild & Co Redburn *"Bondernomics"* 09-23, 57pp; UBS *"Global I/O: 3Q26 APAC Tech Tour"* 09-22, 27pp off 40 company meetings). ONE CAPACITY FIGURE SUPERSEDED — AND IT IS UBS SUPERSEDING UBS INSIDE SIX DAYS.**
  **SUPERSEDED (moved here): industry CoWoS capacity at end-2027, 260kwpm → 270kwpm.** The prior value, adopted on this page 2026-09-16 from the UBS Taiwan Summit (Days 2-3), was verbatim *"We raise **CoWoS industry capacity by end-2027 to 260kwpm from 250kwpm, up from 160kwpm end-2026**. We **keep TSMC's capacity at 180kwpm end-2027** but **lift our ASE estimate to 60kwpm (from 50kwpm)** and **keep Amkor at 20kwpm**."* The new value is *"We forecast industry CoWoS capacity to reach **270kwpm by end-'27, versus 160kwpm by end-'26**"* (UBS · Gaudois/Abrams/Lin et al., 2026-09-22). **The end-'26 base of 160kwpm is UNCHANGED; only end-'27 moves, +10kwpm / +3.8%.** ⚠️⚠️ **NO REVISED PER-VENDOR SPLIT IS PUBLISHED IN THE NEW NOTE, so the 180 TSMC / 60 ASE / 20 Amkor decomposition is now a STALE breakdown of a superseded total and must NOT be re-used against the 270k.** ⚠️ **KNOCK-ON, ANNOTATED IN THE BODY: the 09-17 block on this page headlined that *"the industry CoWoS number converged at 260k wfpm end-2027 across two houses in 24 hours"* — JPM at 260k (TSMC 200k + OSATs 60k) against UBS at 260k. JPM's 260k is unchanged, so the convergence is now JPM 260k vs UBS 270k, a ~4% gap. The SPLIT remains the genuinely contested variable: JPM puts 77% of its 260k inside TSMC; UBS's framing is materially more outsourced and is now hardened by a named second wafer-on-substrate OSAT alongside ASE and a revenue floor under the incumbent (ASE LEAP above US$7.5bn in '27E).**
  🔴🔴🔴 **NET-NEW #1 AND THE MOST REUSABLE THING THIS THEME GAINED — A FULLY-COSTED BOTTOM-UP HYBRID-BONDING LINE MODEL, BUILT ON REALISED RATHER THAN NAMEPLATE THROUGHPUT, AND CRUCIALLY DOING THE SAME FOR THE INCUMBENT.** [[AMD]] CY2025: 19.0m bonds (Epyc-X 0.7m × 10 + Ryzen-X3D 7.9m × 1 + MI355X 0.5m × 8) → **75 uph implied against a 1,500 uph datasheet spec, −95%**. [[SKHYNIX]] MR-MUF CY2025: $20.1bn HBM ÷ $350 ASP = 57.4m units × 12 bonds = 689.1m bonds → **445 uph against a c1,200 uph nameplate, −63%**. ➤ **BOTH technologies miss their spec badly, which is what makes the comparison realised-vs-realised rather than realised-vs-brochure.** ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS PAGE: every uph figure it carries — the 09-15 Bernstein "50nm bonder ~1,500 → ~3,000 UPH", BESI's 8800 Chameo Flex "4,000 uph" — is a NAMEPLATE number and must never be netted against these field rates.** **Cost ladder per bond (fully loaded, per 10,000 sq ft, cleanroom/20yr + tools/4yr + 17h/day): observed HB $3.97 · TC-NCF $1.02 · MR-MUF $0.33 · Kinex HB at 200 uph $1.48 · W2W HB at 2,651 bonds/hour $0.11. Incremental vs MR-MUF: +$3.63 → +$1.15 → MINUS $0.22.** ⚠️ **W2W hybrid bonding is a cost SAVING over mass reflow, and Redburn now calls the W2W alternative "the principal basis for our rising caution on HBM adoption" — an escalation from June, when it was one of "two risks".**
  🔴🔴 **NET-NEW #2 — THE STRUCTURAL FINDING FOR A PACKAGING THEME: "the bonders themselves account for 31% of the total capital cost of a HB advanced packaging fab. Assessing the economics of HB on the price of the bonder alone therefore MATERIALLY UNDERSTATES the true cost of adoption." Per 10,000 sq ft: HB (SoIC) $168.2m (tools $119.5m + ISO3/Class-1 cleanroom $48.8m) vs TC-NCF $162.1m vs MR-MUF $100.6m; per bonder line $9.6m / $4.5m / $4.0m; bonders housed 18 / 36 / 25. The net-new content is FRONT-END in character and is $0.0m in BOTH solder flows: Cu/dielectric CMP $19.1m · post-CMP wet clean $8.8m · plasma activation+degas $8.8m · temporary bond/debond $8.8m · overlay+AFM metrology $6.6m · fab-grade defect/particle inspection $6.2m · acoustic C-SAM $0.8m.** ➤ **[[AMAT]] and [[KLAC]] content that scales with HB adoption rather than with bonder count.** 🔭 **Running the other way, KGD stacked-die test capex is LOWER in an HB line ($5.6m vs $19.5m TC-NCF and $24.1m MR-MUF) — ⚠️ but the lines house different bonder counts (18/36/25), so per bonder it is ~$0.31m / ~$0.54m / ~$0.96m, and Redburn draws no test conclusion from a capex-per-floor-area model built for a bonder thesis. Flagged, not adopted.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW #3 — THE ADOPTION HURDLE IS NOW A RULE WITH A SENSITIVITY TABLE. Redburn's criterion: the maximum acceptable incremental cost to a logic device is 2% of COGS (100bp of GM at a 50% gross margin). Eight D2W bonds into a 16-high HBM stack (COGS $154) cost $9.19 = 6.0% of COGS at 200 uph; 250 uph → $6.82 / 4.4%; 300 → $5.24 / 3.4%; 350 → $4.12 / 2.7%; 400 → $3.27 / 2.1%; 450 → $2.61 / 1.7%. Redburn gives the 2× step to 400 uph a 40% probability.** ➤ **The whole HBM hybrid-bonding question now reduces to REALISED FACTORY OUTPUT IN UPH — a number no vendor discloses. Base-die bonding clears the hurdle at sub-2% of COGS even at 8-high and is modelled to ship from 1H27 at Samsung; probability-weighted DRAM stacking only from 2H28.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW #4 — A COMPETING FRAME FOR THE DE-SPEC DEBATE, LOGGED BUT NOT ADOPTED. Redburn: "Stack height is too often treated as the north star for HB adoption… It is BANDWIDTH, NOT CAPACITY, that gates performance." The mechanism is a pin count — HBM4 took a one-time doubling of I/O channels from 1,024 to 2,048, requiring a c40-micron bond pad pitch, "the limits of mass reflow processes"; doubling again requires tighter pitch and therefore HB. Generation ladder: bandwidth (Tb/s) 0.1/0.3/0.3/0.8/1.0/3.0/4.1/6.1 with gen-on-gen 93%/22%/158%/26%/193% (HBM4)/37% (HBM4E)/50% (HBM5). Hot Chips Aug-2026: Hynix's roadmap is 16-high on a COMBINATION of advanced MUF and HB, with 20-high EXCLUSIVELY on HB.** ⚠️ **If the frame is right, the 8-Hi/12-Hi de-spec is much weaker evidence against hybrid bonding than this theme has treated it as. Held as a competing frame against the de-spec evidence, not over it.**
  🔴🔴 **NET-NEW #5 — AND THE MOST IMPORTANT UBS LINE FOR THIS THEME, BECAUSE OF WHO SAID IT: "Likely longer use of TCB tools for HBM; hybrid bonding adoption push-out: In-line with comments from BOTH SAMSUNG AND SK HYNIX during the Tech Tour, ASMPT is seeing use case of TCB tools for HBM being extended potentially TO HBM5, suggesting push out of VOLUME adoption ('insertions' still possible) of hybrid bonding tools. Cost (higher clean room requirements + more back end tools) but also recent decision at key customer to de-spec HBM density (12Hi > 8Hi) may have contributed… We believe ASMPT may have signed a JOINT EVALUATION PROGRAM WITH SAMSUNG on development of HBM TCB tools targeting HBM5."** ➤ **Three things to separate. (1) It is a VOLUME statement, not a technology rejection. (2) The COST half of UBS's explanation IS Redburn's 31%-of-capital-cost finding, arriving as a customer objection rather than a model output — two houses, two methods, one mechanism, one day apart. (3) ⚠️ It materially weakens the 09-15 Bernstein reading that Samsung is the vocal HB champion and Hynix the cautious laggard: UBS heard it from BOTH, and has the TCB competitor evaluating HBM5 tools with Samsung itself. Bernstein's Outperform and "adoption trend unchanged" are NOT withdrawn — logged as a disagreement between two houses' channel checks.**
  🔭 **ALSO FILED: ASMPT's logic book expanding onto four new surfaces (>50 chip-to-substrate TCB orders announced July; chip-to-wafer TCB with a meaningful FC-bonder→TCB transition at TSMC and tool POR into '28/'29; CoPoS chip-on-panel evaluation units at a leading logic customer plus an [[INTC]] EMIB-T evaluation unit for early '27; and ⚠️ TCB *AND HYBRID BONDING* tools under development for EIC-on-PIC (CPO) stacking — a direct competitive entry on the CPO hybrid-bonding leg in the same week Redburn cut that leg from 50 bonders to 15). Chiplet-driven wafer PROBING content growth, with KYEC citing increasing test intensity, higher power consumption, greater HBM integration and wafer-level probing. Redburn's HB logic-candidate table (Nvidia GPU NO — Rubin microbump-bonded, HB expected with Feynman; Google TPU NO — dual chiplet with microbumps; Broadcom ASICs VARIES — 3.5D XDSiP uses HB; Intel Clearwater Forest Xeon 6+ YES — 12 tiles bonded to a base die, 2H26; Apple M5 Pro/Max YES; all Android APUs NO), with Redburn's own caveat that TPU/Jalapeno/Trainium architectures are "closely guarded". ASE raised 2026 capex a SECOND time by $2bn to US$10.5bn across 20+ projects (13 greenfield, 8 brownfield), lifting its structural GM range from 24-30% to ABOVE 30% (mid-30s in FY27 per Jefferies), and is described as "the largest wafer-level packaging capacity provider outside of TSMC, much bigger than Amkor or Intel," with privileged access to COUPE. Panel-level packaging (310×310mm) ramps 1Q27; CPO and EMIB contribute from 2027, more meaningfully from 2028.** **★ EMIB-T's first customer endorsement, and it comes from [[MEDIATEK]] rather than [[INTC]]: asked directly, management said "YES THIS IS TRUE, WE ARE MORE COMMITTED TO EMIB-T" — corroborated by JPM ("yield on track for MP in 2028"), GS ("packaging and substrate yields progressing well") and BofA (using BOTH CoWoS and EMIB-T for large-reticle 2nm ASICs). This resolves in the affirmative the item MS had flagged as the key open question into that call.** ⚠️ **Counterweight logged and shown to be COMPATIBLE: Unimicron says EMIB-T "will not reach mass production until 2027" — a TIMING disagreement, not a viability one, since MediaTek's claim is v9 MP in 2028. What is no longer disputed is the demand rationale: customers switch for LOWER COST and IMPROVED PERFORMANCE, and Unimicron expects them to stay for THREE PRODUCT GENERATIONS.** **★ The handler/thermal spec escalation is faster than this theme was tracking: LARGE-PACKAGE ATC HANDLERS (>120×150mm) carry a 20-25% PRICE PREMIUM from Oct 2026, with interest going from 1-2 customers to "almost every one" and possibly 20-30% of Hon's AI shipments in 1H27 → >50% in 2H27, as customers move to the platform AHEAD of chip-size migration. ⚠️ CUSTOMER THERMAL ROADMAPS TARGET ~10KW IN 2027-28. CPO test insertion-4 optical-electrical co-test handlers ship on schedule in October as high-ASP option value, and Hon expects ASICS TO SURPASS GPUS in its AI order mix in 2027** (JPM · Huang/Tsai, 2026-07-30). **Onshoring named and multi-vendor: an IDM back-end fab in Thailand, A TAIWAN OSAT US FACILITY, two Taiwan OSAT Singapore facilities, multiple China projects and A KOREA OSAT US FACILITY — plus China FT handler orders +>60% y/y and SLT +>80% y/y at 90-100% China high-end share.** **Test inside the packaging ramp: ASE flags advanced testing +~160%/80% y/y in 2026/27, mainstream growth guidance raised to 20%+ from 13%, and mid-high single-digit PRICE INCREASES in 2H26/2027 on the AI-driven capacity squeeze.** Substrate/passives corroboration: **SEMCO and LG Innotek 2Q OP more than doubled y/y on FC-BGA and AI MLCC, with LARGE PREPAYMENTS funding new fabs and new FC-BGA capacity in Korea and VIETNAM from mid-to-late 2027; Kinsus Q226 "in line; on track to ramp."** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[INTC]], [[AMD]], [[NVDA]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[TER]], [themes/semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md).

- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch). 4 State-of-play bullets. Nothing superseded.** **The main event is UBS's first full-length EMIB-T-vs-CoPoS deep dive, which supplies the TAM this page lacked and stress-tests the disruption risk: advanced-packaging TAM US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR; and even in UBS's stated BULL case for Intel — 13% industry share / US$16bn of sales by 2030 / ~24mn accelerator dies — TSMC compounds advanced-packaging sales at a 50% CAGR 2026-30, holds 65-70% share, and takes advanced packaging to 18% of sales in 2030E from 9% in 2026E. UBS's LOWER case only moves that to 47% / 62% / 16%. So the base-vs-bear spread is roughly 2pts of TSMC revenue mix over four years** — the most useful bound on packaging-disruption risk now on the page, and it implies the debate matters far more to Intel's P&L than to TSMC's. Timing favours Intel (**EMIB-T MP H2'27-28, slightly ahead of CoPoS in 2028**; CoPoS for Nvidia 2028, others 2029-30). Trade-off stated: EMIB-T = **structural cost via interposer elimination + scalability to ultra-large packages on large substrates**; CoPoS = **tighter interconnect, better signal integrity, 3D stacking "and eventually, CPO,"** plus execution record. Named risks: Intel's **~90-92% validation yields** and **~50% EMIB-T substrate yields (vs >80% HPC ABF, with Ibiden capacity able to "offset lower yields and support selected products like Google's TPU v9")**; TSMC's **CoPoS warpage, panel process uniformity, new equipment readiness**. **And one commercial risk that is not technical at all and is new to this page: "integration between the foundry (TSMC) and back-end (Intel) processes, as well as yield responsibility"** — who eats the loss when TSMC owns the die and Intel owns the package. **⚠️ THE YIELD PICTURE IS NOW WORSE-DEFINED AND THAT IS RECORDED AS SUCH: four marks on EMIB-T packaging yield spanning 60% to 99% (Fubon 60-70% · UBS ~90-92% "validation" · KeyBanc "reached 98%" · ex-Intel CVP 98-99% mature) and two on substrate yield (UBS ~50% · KeyBanc 60-70%) — on the single gate Google set at 95%+ for TPU V9. NOT RESOLVED; do not pick one.** Only partial reconciliation available: UBS's is the sole figure explicitly labelled *validation*, and KeyBanc's is second-hand via a trade newsletter. **What every source agrees on is that the binding constraint has moved from bridges to SUBSTRATES.** **Third: glass core substrates now have dated per-player ramps — Lens Tech + Intel (pilot line end-2026, meaningful volume 2H27), Innolux + TSMC + Ibiden (MP 2H28 or 2029), BOE (capacity 2027, MP 2028), AUO (LEO/micro-LED, no timetable) — which puts Intel ~12-18 months ahead of TSMC on substrates too. Worth naming as a pattern: on both bridges and substrates Intel is earlier and TSMC is betting on the higher-performance version arriving later.** MS's caveat retained: the hurdles are "process control instead of pure equipment capability" and "more meaningful revenue contribution should take time." **Fourth, the demand justification in management's own words: "TSMC highlighted at its 2Q26 call that back-end sits in deeper shortage than front-end, with packaging capacity so tight it now limits customer growth"** — corroborated independently by an **ex-ASM International senior process engineer** who puts the constraint on **tool availability (bonding tools), "not… financial or market factors,"** with the market bifurcated (TSMC complex / OSATs mature) and the TSMC-NVIDIA relationship "deeply integrated." Caveat kept: 2-page expert summary, front-end background. Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[INTC]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[NVDA]], [[GOOG]], [custom-asic-tpu](custom-asic-tpu.md), [semicap-wfe](semicap-wfe.md).
- 2026-07-24 — /run-inbox SemiAnalysis batch (theme-dossier pass). **Added two State-of-play bullets, extended one existing bullet, added a Sources entry; all additive, no prior number superseded.** (1) **S10 "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?" (2026-07-24) — the strongest packaging fold of the batch:** MI455 is the **largest CoWoS-L module shipping at 5.5x reticle** with **3,470mm² of logic silicon, "by far the most amount of silicon being shipped in a single package"** (8 N2 XCDs hybrid-bonded on 2 reticle-sized base dies + 2 N3P I/O dies + 12 HBM4 stacks), and AMD remains **the only company adopting TSMC's SoIC-X hybrid bonding**. **Net-new roadmap item — active LSI:** CoWoS-L bridges have until now been **passive (wiring and capacitors only)**; TSMC's **ISSCC 2026** aLSI presentation showed **active bridges with a low-power repeater circuit regenerating signals mid-channel**, so **top-die PHYs can shrink meaningfully at nearly negligible energy cost, reclaiming leading-edge silicon and shoreline for compute and memory** — and SemiAnalysis's tell that MI455 is **the first chip shipping it** is that TSMC's demo **"test vehicle" is the MI455 interposer itself** (two base dies, twelve HBM4 stacks, two IO dies). Rack-level content added for the connector/cable names: **10,368 differential pairs of copper backplane cable, 1,728 flyover cables, >550 Broadcom Ethernet retimers**, up to **12 Molex "Genesis" 128G-UALink cables per compute tray**, and **$68,928 of backplane + compute-tray content per rack ($44,352 + $24,576)**, with SemiAnalysis's warning that every cable is an assembly-time failure point. (2) **S1 "H100 vs GB200 NVL72 Training Benchmarks" (2025-08-20) — flagged HISTORICAL BACKFILL** (~11 months old, GB200-generation): the GB200 NVL72 backplane anatomy and the [../APH.md](../APH.md) read — **18 compute trays + 9 NVSwitch trays + 4 copper backplane cartridges**, connected via **Amphenol Paladin HD** male/female pairs whose **screw tension governs signal integrity**, gold-plated with **an endurance of at most 200 mating cycles before the component must be discarded**; a backplane swap takes **8-24 hours**, needs **10 rack stiffeners removed**, and drains the whole rack. Read: at rack scale the connector is a wear part with bounded life — recurring content for the connector vendors, and a serviceability argument for cableless designs (the same axis on which SemiAnalysis criticises Helios). (3) **S5 SMIC/Kirin teardown (2026-06-14) — DEDUP-PLUS, existing bullet EXTENDED not duplicated:** the teardown was already folded on 2026-07-23 (STCO as China's no-EUV scaling lever, Huawei "14A-equivalent by 2031," iPoP/organic-RDL/no-Si-interposer, MI350X AID reference). Added the missing quantification: the **ISCAS 2026 τ (tau) scaling law** reframing scaling in the time domain; **LogicFolding** as face-to-face ultra-fine-pitch stacking of *active* logic (beyond V-Cache or MI350X AIDs) where "parts of the same logic block are split across multiple active dies"; the roadmap **2.75 GHz → ~5 GHz prime core by 2031** (3.1/3.39 GHz in labs) and **215 MTr/mm² by 2030, 295 by 2031** (the 2031 jump implying a third active layer, partial EUV or aggressive DUV); ⚠️ **the caveat that must travel with it — this is density per package footprint, not like-for-like, and the same method applied to AMD's MI450X (N2 top die on N3P base) yields 460.2 MTr/mm² in 2026 vs Huawei's 295 in 2031**; plus the note that **the Kirin 9030 itself does not use LogicFolding** and the full all-organic iPoP stack description (BT laminate + organic RDL interposer + ABF-over-BT substrate, no silicon interposer, chosen for CTE match to the PCB).
